Motivation: To develop a robust, second-order accurate time-discretization scheme for the coupled Stokes-Biot system that performs well in nearly incompressible regimes and avoids volumetric locking, while providing rigorous stability and convergence guarantees.

Method: Formulates the Biot subsystem in a three-field total-pressure formulation (displacement, pore pressure, total pressure), discretizes all variables in time with BDF2, solves monolithically without operator splitting, and uses the BDF2 G-stability identity for discrete energy stability analysis.

Result: Discrete energy stability is established with robustness with respect to Lamé parameter, Biot-Willis coefficient, and storage coefficient. Second-order temporal convergence and optimal-order spatial convergence are derived via a consistency-stability argument, confirmed by numerical experiments.

Conclusion: The proposed scheme achieves robust second-order temporal accuracy with rigorous stability guarantees, making it suitable for the coupled Stokes-Biot system in challenging parameter regimes.

Abstract: We develop and analyze a second-order, fully implicit, monolithic time-discretization for the coupled time-dependent Stokes and quasi-static Biot system. The Biot subsystem is formulated in a three-field total-pressure formulation, in which the total pressure is introduced as an additional unknown together with the solid displacement and pore pressure. This reformulation improves robustness in nearly incompressible regimes and suppresses volumetric locking. All variables are discretized in time using the BDF2 scheme, and the coupled problem is solved monolithically without operator splitting. Discrete energy stability is established using the BDF2 $G$-stability identity. The resulting estimates exhibit robustness with respect to the Lamé parameter, Biot--Willis coefficient, and storage coefficient. A consistency--stability argument is then used to derive second-order convergence in time together with optimal-order convergence in space. Numerical experiments confirm the theoretical results and demonstrate second-order temporal accuracy in the corresponding energy norms.

</details>


### [26] [Splitting methods for Intermediate Long Wave and perturbed Benjamin--Ono models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05540)
*Yvonne Alama Bronsard,Clémentine Courtès,Benjamin Melinand*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper introduces a novel splitting method for numerically solving a class of PDEs (including the ILW equation and other quasilinear, non-integrable equations) by perturbing an explicit Benjamin-Ono (BO) solution formula based on Birkhoff coordinates. The method achieves first-order convergence in H^s-norm requiring only one additional derivative on initial data, and in the deep-water limit the ILW scheme converges to the BO solution. Unlike classical splitting methods, it avoids restrictive quadratic time-step conditions, enabling efficient long-time simulations, demonstrated through numerical exploration of soliton resolution for ILW and KdV-BO equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To leverage recent advances in Birkhoff coordinates and explicit formulas for the integrable BO equation to develop a numerical method for broader classes of PDEs (quasilinear, potentially non-integrable) with significantly relaxed regularity requirements compared to classical splitting methods, and to enable efficient long-time simulations without restrictive time-step constraints.

Method: A novel splitting method that perturbs the explicit BO formula (derived via Birkhoff coordinates). The scheme decomposes the target PDE into the BO part (solved exactly via the explicit formula) and a remainder, then uses a splitting/perturbation strategy. Convergence is proven in H^s-norm (s≥0) with u_0 ∈ H^{s+1}_0, and the deep-water limit convergence to the BO solution is established for the ILW case.

Result: First-order convergence in H^s-norm established with only one additional derivative on initial data (u_0 ∈ H^{s+1}_0), significantly improving upon typical splitting method regularity requirements. The ILW scheme converges to the BO solution in the deep-water limit. Numerical simulations show the method preserves energy over long time scales without a restrictive quadratic time-step condition, unlike classical splitting methods. Soliton resolution is numerically explored for ILW and KdV-BO equations.

Conclusion: The Birkhoff-coordinates-based splitting method provides a robust, efficient numerical framework for a class of quasilinear dispersive PDEs, with theoretical guarantees under mild regularity assumptions and practical advantages for long-time simulations. This opens new computational avenues for exploring phenomena such as soliton resolution in non-integrable settings.

Abstract: Recently, novel integrability techniques, and most notably Birkhoff coordinates followed by an explicit formula, have been introduced to solve the Benjamin--Ono (BO) equation, leading to major theoretical and computational advances. We propose to perturb this explicit BO formula via a novel splitting method for numerically solving a class of PDEs, which includes the Intermediate Long Wave (ILW) equation, as well as other quasilinear equations that are not necessarily integrable. Using the Birkhoff coordinates of BO we establish first-order convergence in the $H^s$-norm ($s \ge 0$) while requiring only one additional derivative on the initial data $u_0 \in H^{s+1}_{0}$, significantly improving upon the regularity requirements of typical splitting methods for nonlinear dispersive equations. Furthermore, we prove that in the deep-water limit, the scheme for the ILW equation converges to the BO solution. Computational advantages of these schemes are shown in simulations: unlike classical splitting methods, our approach does not require a restrictive quadratic time step condition to nearly preserve energy over long time scales, thus rendering efficient and accurate long-time simulations feasible. As an application, we numerically explore the soliton resolution conjecture for both the ILW and a KdV--BO equation.

</details>


### [27] [Divergence-free interpolation of tangential vector fields via matrix-valued kernels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05547)
*Zhengjie Sun,Lishuo Dong,Biao Huang*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper develops divergence-free kernel interpolation methods for tangential vector fields on the unit sphere, constructing tangent-valued surface divergence-free matrix kernels from scalar radial kernels without repeated surface differential operators. It derives explicit kernel representations via vector spherical harmonics, establishes eigenvalue lower bounds and error estimates, and validates the approach numerically, including extensions to nonspherical surfaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct divergence-free kernel interpolation methods for tangential vector fields on the sphere that separate geometric divergence-free enforcement from scalar generator choice, offer lower regularity requirements than classical potential-based methods, and provide theoretical guarantees for convergence and stability.

Method: The authors construct tangent-valued, surface divergence-free matrix kernels from Euclidean scalar radial kernels without repeated surface differential operators, separating geometric constraint enforcement from scalar generator selection. They derive explicit kernel representations and Fourier multipliers using vector spherical harmonics, introduce an inverse Laplace-Beltrami construction preserving multiplier properties, and analyze interpolation matrix eigenvalues and error estimates.

Result: The paper establishes explicit kernel representations with characterized Fourier multipliers, proves a lower bound for the smallest eigenvalue of the interpolation matrix, derives pointwise and Sobolev error estimates including superconvergence for smooth targets, and confirms theoretical predictions with numerical experiments on the sphere and nonspherical surfaces.

Conclusion: The proposed divergence-free kernel construction is both theoretically sound and practically effective, offering flexibility in generator choice, reduced regularity requirements, and broad applicability beyond the sphere, while maintaining provable stability and convergence properties.

Abstract: We develop and analyze a family of divergence-free kernel interpolation methods for tangential vector fields on the unit sphere. Starting from scalar radial kernels in Euclidean space, we construct tangent-valued, surface divergence-free matrix kernels without repeatedly applying surface differential operators. The construction separates the geometric enforcement of the divergence-free constraint from the choice of scalar generator and admits lower-order variants with reduced regularity requirements compared with classical potential-based methods. Using vector spherical harmonics, we derive explicit kernel representations and characterize their Fourier multipliers. We also introduce an inverse Laplace-Beltrami construction that preserves the multipliers of the underlying scalar zonal kernel. For interpolation at scattered nodes, we establish a lower bound for the smallest eigenvalue of the interpolation matrix and derive pointwise and Sobolev error estimates, including superconvergence for targets smoother than the native space. Numerical experiments corroborate the theoretical convergence and stability results, while additional examples on nonspherical surfaces illustrate the applicability of the kernel formula beyond the sphere.

</details>


### [28] [Iterative Decoupling Methods for a Total-Pressure Formulation of Quasi-Static Electroporoelasticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05566)
*Huipeng Gu,Mingchao Cai,Jingzhi Li*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper presents a five-field reformulation of quasi-static electroporoelasticity to address poroelastic locking, along with a fully discrete monolithic scheme using BDF2 time discretization and mixed finite-element spatial discretization. An iterative decoupling method alternating between electromagnetic and poroelastic subproblems is developed, with proven geometric convergence and an explicit mesh-independent contraction factor. Numerical experiments confirm locking-free performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to address computational challenges in electroporoelasticity, particularly the issue of poroelastic locking in the nearly incompressible regime, and to develop an efficient decoupling strategy with rigorous convergence guarantees.

Method: The electroporoelasticity equations are reformulated as a five-field system using total pressure. A monolithic weak formulation with continuous stability estimate is derived. BDF2 time discretization and mixed finite-element spatial discretization yield a fully discrete monolithic scheme. An iterative decoupling method alternating between electromagnetic and poroelastic subproblems is proposed, with a proof of geometric convergence under a physical coupling condition. An algebraically equivalent reduced form is also provided.

Result: The iterative decoupling method converges geometrically to the monolithic solution with an explicit mesh-independent contraction factor. Numerical experiments verify theoretical predictions and demonstrate locking-free performance. The reduced form requires solving the electromagnetic block only once per time step.

Conclusion: The proposed five-field formulation and iterative decoupling approach provide an efficient, locking-free numerical framework for quasi-static electroporoelasticity, with rigorous theoretical guarantees and confirmed numerical performance.

Abstract: Quasi-static electroporoelasticity couples Maxwell's equations with Biot's poroelasticity through electrokinetic coupling between the electric field and the pressure gradient. By introducing the total pressure, the electroporoelasticity equations are reformulated as a five-field system to address poroelastic locking in the nearly incompressible regime. For the resulting five-field system, a monolithic weak formulation is derived, together with a continuous stability estimate. A second-order backward differentiation formula (BDF2) time discretization and a mixed finite- element spatial discretization are then introduced, yieling to a fully discrete monolithic scheme. Building on this scheme, we develop an iterative decoupling method that alternates between an electromagnetic subproblem and a poroelastic subproblem, and prove its geometric convergence to the monolithic solution with an explicit mesh-independent contraction factor under the physical coupling condition. An algebraically equivalent reduced form is also presented, in which the electromagnetic block is solved only once per time step, while the poroelastic block is solved iteratively with electric-field correction updates obtained from the pressure-gradient feedback. Numerical experiments verify the theoretical predictions and demonstrate the locking-free performance.

</details>


### [29] [Efficient higher-order multi-scale method and its convergence estimate for dynamic nonlinear hygro-thermo-mechanical coupling problems of heterogeneous structures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05580)
*Yifei Ding,Hao Dong,Jiale Linghu,Yangshuai Wang*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: A novel higher-order multi-scale (HOMS) computational framework is proposed for efficient and highly accurate simulation of nonlinear hygro-thermo-mechanical (H-T-M) coupling problems in heterogeneous structures, with rigorous error estimates, a two-stage off-line/on-line numerical algorithm, and demonstrated accuracy and efficiency in 2D/3D experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Simulating nonlinear hygro-thermo-mechanical coupling problems in heterogeneous structures is computationally expensive and challenging due to inherent nonlinearities (temperature/moisture-dependent material properties, internal heat sources and moisture sinks from chemical reactions) and high spatial inhomogeneity, motivating the need for an efficient yet high-accuracy multi-scale approach.

Method: The method combines a multi-scale asymptotic approach with Taylor series expansions to construct higher-order correction terms, derives rigorous error estimates in point-wise and integral senses, and develops a two-stage (off-line/on-line) numerical algorithm based on finite difference and finite element methods with proven convergence.

Result: Two- and three-dimensional numerical experiments demonstrate excellent numerical accuracy and robustness of the proposed HOMS approach with low computational overhead.

Conclusion: The proposed HOMS framework provides an efficient, high-accuracy, and low-cost solution for nonlinear H-T-M coupling problems in heterogeneous structures, with theoretical guarantees via rigorous error estimates and convergence proofs.

Abstract: This paper presents a novel higher-order multi-scale (HOMS) computational framework for efficient, high-accuracy, and low-cost simulation of nonlinear hygro-thermo-mechanical (H-T-M) coupling problems in heterogeneous structures. The inherent nonlinearity in the investigated model stems primarily from temperature- or moisture-dependent material properties, and this model also accounts for temperature-dependent internal heat source and moisture sink terms induced by exothermic, moisture-consuming chemical reactions (e.g., hydration). The main contributions of this work are as follows. First, a high-accuracy multi-scale asymptotic model incorporating higher-order correction terms is constructed for nonlinear H-T-M coupling problems in heterogeneous structures with highly spatial inhomogeneity, using the multi-scale asymptotic approach together with Taylor series expansions. Second, rigorous error estimates in both point-wise and integral senses are derived for the multi-scale asymptotic solutions, which theoretically demonstrate the necessity and superiority of the proposed HOMS method. Third, an efficient two-stage numerical algorithm with off-line and on-line stages is developed, based on finite difference and finite element methods, and its convergence is also proved rigorously. Finally, two- and three-dimensional numerical experiments are performed to assess the computational performance of the proposed HOMS approach, showing excellent numerical accuracy and robustness with low computational overhead.

</details>


### [30] [A priori error estimator for reduced-order models based on the higher-order Craig-Bampton method in dynamic substructuring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05625)
*Jaemin Kim,Seung-Hwan Boo*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper addresses the lack of a priori error estimators for the Higher-order Craig-Bampton (HCB) method by proposing a hierarchical estimation framework that exploits the nested Ritz subspace structure of HCB, providing both a generalized CB error estimator and a novel HCB-1 estimator.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The HCB method improves accuracy over CB at the same model dimension, but no a priori error estimator exists to predict eigenvalue errors without solving the full-order problem. If evaluating error requires the full-order solution, model reduction's purpose is defeated—this gap motivates the work.

Method: A hierarchical estimation framework exploiting the nested Ritz subspace structure of HCB, where each higher-order solution serves as a reference for the preceding order. It provides (i) a generalized CB error estimator via Rayleigh quotient perturbation analysis, and (ii) a novel HCB-1 error estimator using the HCB-2 eigensolution as a reference.

Result: Numerical examples across models of varying geometric complexity validate both estimators (the generalized CB estimator and the HCB-1 estimator).

Conclusion: The proposed hierarchical framework fills the gap of a priori error estimation for HCB, enabling accuracy assessment without solving the full-order eigenvalue problem.

Abstract: The Craig-Bampton (CB) method is a widely used dynamic substructuring technique based on component mode synthesis (CMS). The higher-order Craig-Bampton (HCB) method augments the CB basis with residual modes from a Neumann series expansion of the residual flexibility matrix, where HCB-n retains terms up to the n-th order and is reduced back to the CB size via the System Equivalent Reduction Expansion Process (SEREP), achieving improved accuracy at the same model dimension. However, assessing the accuracy of a reduced model without solving the full-order problem remains a fundamental challenge: if the full-order solution is required to evaluate the error, the purpose of model reduction is defeated. Despite the demonstrated superiority of the HCB method, no a priori error estimator (one that predicts eigenvalue errors without solving the full-order eigenvalue problem) has been proposed for it. The present work addresses this gap with a hierarchical estimation framework that exploits the nested Ritz subspace structure of the HCB method, where each higher-order solution serves as a reference for estimating the error of the preceding order. The framework provides (i) a generalized CB error estimator derived from a Rayleigh quotient perturbation analysis, and (ii) a novel HCB-1 error estimator using the HCB-2 eigensolution as a reference. Numerical examples across models of varying geometric complexity validate both estimators.

</details>


### [31] [Minimization-based polynomial corrections for high-order curved boundaries on fixed and moving domains: assessment on finite volume and discontinuous Galerkin schemes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05768)
*Mirco Ciallella,Walter Boscheri*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper introduces two novel strategies for imposing high-order boundary conditions on fixed and moving curved domains approximated with piecewise affine triangulations. The authors reformulate the Reconstruction for Off-site Data (ROD) method as simple polynomial corrections that require no linear system inversion, significantly simplifying the computational cost. The approach is developed within Runge-Kutta discontinuous Galerkin and ADER arbitrary-Lagrangian-Eulerian finite volume frameworks, demonstrating up to fifth-order convergence in 2D and 3D.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: High-order accuracy on curved domains requires simultaneously handling PDE discretization error and geometrical error. While high-order numerical methods address the former, the latter requires either high-order domain parametrization or consistent boundary condition approximation. The standard ROD method avoids curvilinear meshes but requires inverting local linear systems whose cost grows with polynomial degree and mesh refinement.

Method: The authors show that ROD-type approaches can be recast as simple polynomial corrections applicable without any linear system inversion. Two strategies are developed: one within a Runge-Kutta discontinuous Galerkin framework and one within an ADER arbitrary-Lagrangian-Eulerian finite volume framework for compressible flows on fixed and moving domains.

Result: Numerical experiments with Dirichlet and slip-wall boundary conditions demonstrate convergence analysis up to fifth order in both 2D and 3D, validating the wide applicability and accuracy of the simplified approach.

Conclusion: The reformulation of ROD as simple polynomial corrections greatly simplifies the development of minimization-based boundary treatments and reduces computational cost while maintaining high-order accuracy on curved domains without needing curvilinear meshes.

Abstract: In this work, we present two novel strategies to impose high-order boundary conditions on fixed and moving curved domains, approximated with piecewise affine triangulations. Achieving high-order accuracy on curved domains requires tackling both the PDE discretization error and the geometrical error simultaneously. While the former can be reduced by employing high-order numerical methods such as finite volume and discontinuous Galerkin, the latter demands either a high-order parametrization of the physical domain or a consistent approximation of the boundary conditions. Minimization-based approaches like the Reconstruction for Off-site Data (ROD) method allow one to skip the construction of high-order curvilinear meshes by defining high-order consistent boundary conditions on a computational boundary that does not match the physical one. The ROD approach mitigates the second-order geometrical error by retrieving a modified polynomial in each boundary cell, which enforces the boundary conditions exactly on the physical boundary. However, the standard ROD method requires the inversion of a local linear system, whose cost grows with the polynomial degree and mesh refinement. Inspired by a recent one-dimensional analysis, we show that ROD-type approaches can be recast as simple polynomial corrections, applicable without any linear system inversion. This greatly simplifies the development of minimization-based boundary treatments and reduces the associated computational cost. To prove the wide applicability of our strategy, we develop it within a Runge-Kutta discontinuous Galerkin framework and an ADER arbitrary-Lagrangian-Eulerian finite volume framework for compressible flows on fixed and moving domains. Several numerical experiments with Dirichlet and slip-wall boundary conditions are presented, with convergence analysis up to fifth order in both 2D and 3D.

</details>


### [32] [A Kernel Formula for Kinetic Fokker-Planck Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05770)
*Fuqun Han,Wuchen Li*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: The paper derives an explicit one-step kernel formula for the kinetic Fokker-Planck equation by starting from a reference dynamics with a known transition kernel and using an auxiliary weight to encode the drift perturbation. They establish local weak consistency and first-order finite-time weak convergence in a weighted phase-space setting, provide a formal variational interpretation via a regularized Wasserstein-type proximal operator, and demonstrate its utility in numerical experiments for deterministic particle schemes in kinetic sampling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain an explicit approximation of density evolution for the kinetic Fokker-Planck equation, which is typically challenging to solve directly, enabling accessible analysis and efficient numerical methods.

Method: Construct an explicit one-step kernel formula by (1) choosing a reference dynamics with an explicit transition kernel, (2) using an auxiliary weight to encode the drift perturbation, and (3) combining these to approximate the density evolution. The approach is analyzed in a weighted phase-space setting and extended to a broader class of parabolic equations.

Result: Local weak consistency and first-order finite-time weak convergence are established under suitable regularity and moment assumptions. Numerical experiments demonstrate the accuracy of the kernel approximation and its applicability to deterministic particle schemes for kinetic sampling.

Conclusion: The proposed kernel formula provides an explicit and accurate approximation for the kinetic Fokker-Planck equation, with theoretical guarantees and practical utility for kinetic sampling via particle schemes, plus a formal variational interpretation linking it to proximal-type operators.

Abstract: We derive an explicit one-step kernel formula for the kinetic Fokker-Planck equation. The construction begins with a reference dynamics admitting an explicit transition kernel and uses a carefully chosen auxiliary weight to encode the drift perturbation. The resulting kernel formula provides an explicit approximation of the density evolution. In a weighted phase-space setting, we establish local weak consistency and first-order finite-time weak convergence under suitable regularity and moment assumptions. We also give a formal variational interpretation of the formula in terms of a regularized Wasserstein-type proximal operator and present analogous constructions for a broader class of parabolic equations. Numerical experiments illustrate the accuracy of the kernel approximation and its application to deterministic particle schemes for kinetic sampling.

</details>


### [33] [A neural operator view on U-Nets for inverse imaging problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05839)
*Alexander Auras,Martin Burger,Samira Kabri,Michael Moeller,Michael Schopf-Kuester*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper studies the behavior of U-Net-style neural networks for solving ill-posed inverse problems as discretization resolution increases. It reviews neural operator approaches, compares them to classical U-Nets, and finds that while neural operator U-Nets are resolution-invariant by design, classical U-Nets are surprisingly more robust to resolution changes than expected.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Very few works have studied how neural networks for inverse problems behave in the limit where discretized ill-conditioned problems become truly ill-posed as resolution increases. Understanding this behavior is important for the reliability and generalization of these methods.

Method: The authors review neural operator learning approaches in U-Net-like architectures, use a 1D toy example for interpretability, and conduct extensive numerical experiments on limited angle CT-reconstruction comparing different neural operator U-Nets against classical U-Net baselines, including tests of resolution generalization.

Result: U-shaped neural operator architectures are resolution-invariant by design, but the classical U-Net architecture demonstrated more robustness to resolution changes than expected, challenging the presumed advantage of neural operator approaches.

Conclusion: Resolution-invariance by design does not necessarily translate to better practical performance under resolution shifts; classical U-Nets remain competitive and robust, suggesting the need for careful evaluation of architectural choices for inverse problems.

Abstract: Deep neural networks have shown great empirical success in the solution of a wide variety of ill-posed inverse problems in imaging. Yet, very few works have studied their behavior in the limit that turns the discretized ill-conditioned problems into truly ill-posed ones, i.e., for an increasing resolution of the discretization. In this work, we review common approaches to neural operator learning in architectures that resemble a U-Net, one of the most common classical architectures for inverse imaging problems. We discuss advantages and drawbacks of the respective approaches, consider a 1D toy example for improved interpretability, and present extensive numerical experiments on how different types of neural operator U-Nets can improve a first (crude) limited angle CT-reconstruction. In particular, we study how well networks trained for a certain resolution of the discretization generalize to other resolutions. Our finding is that while U-shaped neural operator architectures are by design resolution-invariant, the classical U-Net architecture seems to be more robust with respect to resolution changes than expected.

</details>


### [34] [Rothe time discretization and weak solutions for a cutoff Westervelt system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05865)
*Marvin Fritz*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper develops a fully implicit Rothe time discretization for a cutoff first-order formulation of the Westervelt equation, using enthalpy and primitive mobility variables to transform each nonlinear time step into a uniformly monotone elliptic problem, establishing well-posedness, weak solution existence, weak-strong uniqueness, consistency estimates, and numerical validation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The goal is to design a robust and theoretically sound time discretization scheme for the Westervelt equation (a nonlinear acoustic wave model) that avoids higher-order energy estimates and inverse inequalities, which are often difficult to manage in such nonlinear settings.

Method: The approach uses a fully implicit Rothe method on a cutoff first-order formulation. Key ideas include introducing the enthalpy and primitive mobility variables to convert each time step into a uniformly monotone elliptic problem, applying the Browder-Minty theorem for well-posedness, deriving discrete energy inequalities, using an Alt-Luckhaus type compactness argument for the transformed variable, and establishing consistency estimates in discrete-in-time weak norms.

Result: The scheme yields existence of weak solutions via compactness and passage to the limit, a weak-strong uniqueness principle, a conditional a posteriori criterion for cutoff inactivity, and rigorous consistency estimates for the temporal residuals. Numerical experiments demonstrate stability, near first-order temporal behavior, and inactivity of the cutoff in tested regimes.

Conclusion: The proposed Rothe scheme provides a mathematically rigorous and practically stable discretization for the cutoff Westervelt equation, with strong theoretical guarantees and confirmed numerical performance.

Abstract: We study a fully implicit Rothe time discretization for a cutoff first-order formulation of the Westervelt equation. The key ingredients are the enthalpy variable and the primitive mobility variable, which turn each nonlinear time step into a uniformly monotone elliptic problem and avoid higher-order energy estimates and inverse inequalities. For every time step, the discrete problem reduces to a monotone elliptic equation, which yields well-posedness of the Rothe scheme by the Browder-Minty theorem. We derive a discrete energy inequality, establish compactness for the transformed variable by an Alt-Luckhaus type argument, and pass to the limit to obtain existence of weak solutions for the cutoff first-order system. We prove a weak-strong uniqueness principle for the cutoff problem and formulate a conditional a posteriori criterion under which the cutoff is inactive. For sufficiently regular solutions of the forced cutoff problem, we establish consistency estimates which identify the temporal residuals produced by the Rothe approximation in the natural discrete-in-time weak norms. These estimates provide a rigorous consistency basis for the observed temporal behavior. Finally, numerical experiments illustrate the stability of the scheme, its observed near first-order temporal behavior, and inactivity of the cutoff along the computed discrete trajectories in the tested regimes.

</details>


### [35] [Reduced Basis Method for Simulating Thermal Transients in Electric Machines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05904)
*Herbert Egger,Eva-Maria Haslhofer*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper presents Reduced Basis (RB) methods for transient thermal simulation of induction motors, showing they achieve Finite Element (FE)-level accuracy, support fully automatic construction, and deliver computational efficiency comparable to Lumped Parameter Thermal Networks (LPTNs).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to overcome the trade-off between accuracy and computational cost in thermal simulation of induction motors: FE models are accurate but expensive, while LPTNs are fast but less accurate. RB methods are explored as a middle-ground solution.

Method: The authors construct RB approximations for transient thermal simulation of an induction motor and compare their performance against FE models and LPTNs, both calibrated to measurement data.

Result: Numerical results show RB models achieve FE accuracy, allow fully automatic construction, and have computational efficiency comparable to traditional LPTNs.

Conclusion: RB methods offer an attractive compromise, combining the accuracy of FE models with the computational efficiency of LPTNs, and can be constructed automatically for induction motor thermal simulation.

Abstract: Reduced Basis (RB) methods provide low-dimensional approximations of parametrized partial differential equations with controllable accuracy. We discuss the construction of RB approximations for transient thermal simulation of an induction motor and compare their performance to Finite Element (FE) models and Lumped Parameter Thermal Networks (LPTNs) after calibration to measurements. Numerical results demonstrate that RB models can achieve FE accuracy, allow for a fully automatic construction, and offer computational efficiency comparable to traditional LPTNs.

</details>


### [36] [A Thermodynamically Consistent Cahn-Hilliard-Navier-Stokes Model for Tumor Growth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06099)
*Chenyang Li,Ping Lin,Hui Yu,Haibiao Zheng*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper proposes a thermodynamically consistent phase-field model for tumor growth, coupling Cahn-Hilliard equations for tumor and nutrient dynamics with Navier-Stokes equations. A first-order MSAV-based time discretization with pressure-correction is developed, proven unconditionally energy stable, mass conservative, and achieving optimal first-order temporal error estimates, verified by numerical experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a mathematically rigorous and computationally efficient numerical framework for tumor growth modeling that maintains thermodynamic consistency and provides provable stability and convergence guarantees.

Method: An energetic variational framework is used to couple Cahn-Hilliard equations for tumor evolution and nutrient transport with incompressible Navier-Stokes equations. A first-order time discretization scheme based on the Multiple Scalar Auxiliary Variables (MSAV) approach combined with a pressure-correction strategy handles the nonlinear coupled system.

Result: The scheme is rigorously proven to be unconditionally energy stable and mass conservative. Optimal first-order temporal error estimates are established for the tumor phase-field variable, nutrient concentration, and fluid velocity. Numerical experiments confirm effectiveness, robustness, and theoretical convergence rates.

Conclusion: The proposed MSAV-based scheme provides a stable, accurate, and theoretically grounded numerical method for tumor growth simulation.

Abstract: This work develops a thermodynamically consistent phase-field model for tumor growth based on the energetic variational framework. The model couples the Cahn-Hilliard equations for tumor evolution and nutrient transport with the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations. A first-order time discretization scheme based on the Multiple Scalar Auxiliary Variables (MSAV) approach together with a pressure-correction strategy is proposed to efficiently handle the nonlinear and coupled structure of the system. The proposed scheme is rigorously proved to be unconditionally energy stable and mass conservative. Furthermore, optimal first-order temporal error estimates are established for the tumor phase-field variable, the nutrient concentration, and the fluid velocity. Finally, numerical experiments demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed method and verify the theoretical convergence rates.

</details>


### [37] [ChromOps.jl: High-order simulation and discrete forward sensitivity analysis for chromatography models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06131)
*Kristian Meyer,Maksym Ratajczyk,Christopher Rackauckas*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: ChromOps.jl is a fully differentiable Julia chromatography solver that combines high-order spatial discretization with discrete forward sensitivity analysis (DFSA), enabling efficient gradient-based parameter estimation without hand-derived gradient code.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Mechanistic chromatography models are valuable but gradient-based parameter estimation and optimization are hindered by high computational cost and the difficulty of deriving objective-function gradients manually.

Method: The paper presents ChromOps.jl, a differentiable Julia solver integrating high-order spatial discretization (FD-SBP and DG-SEM compared) with discrete forward sensitivity analysis (DFSA), using ForwardDiff.jl for automatic differentiation.

Result: Both discretizations reach theoretical convergence rates on manufactured problems; FD-SBP shows better work-precision performance for forward and dual-valued simulations. DFSA scales as approximately 1.4(1+Np) forward solves for up to 24 parameters in a single ForwardDiff.jl chunk (~1.4 forward solves per additional parameter).

Conclusion: Since primal and dual-valued solves share the same code path, DFSA provides accurate gradients of user-defined objectives without hand-derived chain-rule code, enabling accessible gradient-based parameter estimation and optimization for chromatography modeling.

Abstract: Mechanistic chromatography models are valuable for process development, but gradient-based parameter estimation and optimization can be hindered by computational cost and the effort of deriving objective-function gradients. To address this concern, a fully differentiable Julia chromatography solver, ChromOps.jl, is presented that combines high-order spatial discretization with discrete forward sensitivity analysis (DFSA). Two high-order spatial discretizations, finite difference summation-by-parts (FD-SBP) and the discontinuous Galerkin spectral element method (DG-SEM), are compared on a 6-component ion-exchange chromatography problem with steric mass action kinetics. Both attain their theoretical convergence rates on manufactured problems, while FD-SBP shows favourable work-precision performance for both forward (primal) and dual-valued simulations and is conceptually simpler to implement. DFSA scales as tDFSA approx 1.4(1+Np)tfwd for up to Np = 24 parameters propagated in a single ForwardDiff.jl chunk, i.e. about 1.4 forward solves per additional parameter. Because the primal and dual-valued solves share the same code path, DFSA provides accurate gradients of user-defined objectives without any hand-derived chain-rule code, enabling accessible gradient-based parameter estimation and optimization.

</details>


### [38] [Gradient Descent on Point Clouds and Applications in Learned Operator Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06267)
*Andreas Hauptmann,Yury Korolev,Matthew Thorpe*

Main category: math.NA

TL;DR: This paper addresses energy minimization on an unknown manifold represented only by a point cloud. The authors propose a gradient descent scheme that simultaneously estimates the manifold and minimizes the energy, with convergence guarantees to a neighborhood of a local minimizer under stability and sampling assumptions. The method is applied to learning operator corrections in inverse problems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Classical gradient descent for energy minimization assumes the manifold is known. However, many practical problems involve data living on an unknown manifold given only by point clouds. One must simultaneously estimate the manifold while minimizing the energy, which is a challenging coupled problem.

Method: The authors define a gradient descent scheme that remains in a neighborhood of the unknown manifold. The approach couples manifold estimation with energy minimization, and they provide convergence analysis under suitable stability and sampling assumptions, showing the error vanishes as time step and sampling errors go to zero.

Result: The proposed scheme converges to a neighborhood of a local minimizer, with the neighborhood size vanishing as time step and sampling errors vanish. The methodology is demonstrated on learning operator corrections in inverse problems.

Conclusion: The paper provides a principled framework for performing gradient-based optimization directly on implicitly defined manifolds from point cloud data, with theoretical guarantees and a practical application to inverse problems.

Abstract: We consider the problem of minimising an energy over an unknown manifold that is given implicitly by a point cloud. For a known manifold one can define a gradient descent scheme analogously to the classical construction in Euclidean spaces. However, when the manifold is not known one has to simultaneously estimate the manifold whilst minimising the energy. We define a gradient descent scheme which remains in a neighbourhood of the manifold and, under suitable stability and sampling assumptions, converges to a neighbourhood of a local minimiser whose size vanishes as the time step and sampling errors vanish. As an example we show the application of the methodology to learning operator corrections in inverse problems.

</details>

### [39] [Derivation of 2D Vlasov-Poisson for classical particles with Coulomb interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04104)
*Mitia Duerinckx,Pierre-Emmanuel Jabin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper provides the first rigorous derivation of the 2D Vlasov-Poisson equation for classical particles with Coulomb interactions without any microscopic cutoff, using a dual hierarchical approach combined with refined analysis of dual BBGKY hierarchies on linearized correlations via kinetic regularization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Deriving the Vlasov-Poisson equation from the N-particle Newtonian dynamics with singular Coulomb interactions has been a long-standing open problem. Previous approaches required microscopic cutoffs to handle the singular nature of the force, and removing this cutoff is a fundamental challenge in kinetic theory.

Method: The proof uses the dual hierarchical approach to mean-field limits, combined with a refined analysis of dual BBGKY hierarchies on linearized correlations. It exploits kinetic regularization effects to handle the singular interaction without cutoff.

Result: The derivation holds for arbitrary singular interaction forces K in L^{2-η}_loc with η>0 small, in any dimension, and extends to Brownian particles. It is valid globally in time as long as the mean-field solution remains sufficiently regular.

Conclusion: This resolves a major open problem by removing the microscopic cutoff in the mean-field derivation of the Vlasov-Poisson equation, with results generalizing to broader classes of singular interactions and particle types.

Abstract: We obtain the first derivation of the 2D Vlasov-Poisson equation for classical particles with Coulomb interactions without any microscopic cutoff. The proof relies on our recent dual hierarchical approach to mean-field limits, together with a refined analysis of dual BBGKY hierarchies on linearized correlations, based on kinetic regularization effects. The result holds more generally for arbitrary singular interaction forces $K\in L^{2-η}_{loc}$ with $η>0$ small enough, in any dimension, and it extends to Brownian particles. It holds globally in time as long as the mean-field solution is regular enough.

</details>


### [40] [Full-Tail Dynamical Rigidity Forced by Atomic Navier-Stokes Energy Concentration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04138)
*Hao Huang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: For smooth unforced 3D Navier-Stokes flows on the flat torus approaching a finite terminal time, this paper proves that endpoint atom formation in kinetic-energy densities forces a full dynamical rigidity. Via nested local Hodge projections and level-crossing catalogues, a backward adjoint with terminal energy concentrated at the atom is extracted, leading to uniform two-parameter saturation of the Oseen propagator, vanishing first-order dissipation, infinite delayed second-order action, and non-integrable enstrophy production in late descendants—implying a delayed second-order operator budget failure near the endpoint.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the endpoint behavior of 3D Navier-Stokes flows and determine whether the formation of kinetic-energy atoms at a terminal time imposes structural rigidity constraints on the dynamics, with implications for regularity and energy dissipation.

Method: The method uses: (1) unique endpoint measures for kinetic-energy densities, (2) same-parent full-tail dynamical rigidity from point atoms, (3) preassigned level-crossing catalogues and nested local Hodge projections to build orthogonal packets, (4) extraction of a single backward adjoint concentrating terminal energy at the atom, and (5) Cauchy saturation to lock the adjoint to late packets, giving uniform two-parameter saturation of the constrained Oseen propagator and its adjoint.

Result: The paper proves that every sufficiently late fixed-root descendant exhibits infinite delayed second-order action and non-integrable positive enstrophy production, and equivalently, that a delayed second-order operator budget determined solely by the Navier-Stokes parent must fail arbitrarily close to the terminal endpoint.

Conclusion: Endpoint atom formation in 3D Navier-Stokes flows forces a deep dynamical rigidity: the blow-up structure near the terminal time is constrained such that dissipation in the first order vanishes while higher-order (delayed second-order) dynamics saturate and fail, linking atomic concentration in kinetic energy to loss of regularity.

Abstract: Let a smooth, unforced, three-dimensional Navier--Stokes flow on the flat torus approach a finite terminal time. Its kinetic-energy densities converge, along the full time variable, to a unique endpoint measure. We prove that each point atom forces a same-parent, full-tail dynamical rigidity. A preassigned level-crossing catalogue and nested local Hodge projections produce orthogonal packets. From the entire packet tail, we extract a single backward adjoint whose terminal energy concentrates at the atom. Cauchy saturation then locks this adjoint to every sufficiently late packet and yields uniform two-parameter saturation of both the constrained Oseen propagator and its adjoint, together with vanishing first-order dissipation. Consequently, every sufficiently late fixed-root descendant has infinite delayed second-order action and non-integrable positive enstrophy production. Equivalently, a delayed second-order operator budget determined solely by the Navier--Stokes parent must fail arbitrarily close to the endpoint.

</details>


### [41] [Traveling-Wave Solutions for an Einstein-Type Material-Balance Model of the Chemotactic Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04177)
*Isanka Garli Hevage,Akif Ibragimov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A nonlinear continuum transport model is developed for localized traveling structures in a coupled two-phase medium, derived from an Einstein-type material-balance formulation combining diffusion, gradient-driven transport, and background depletion. Existence of unique one-hump traveling bands is established in the transport-dominated regime, with explicit profile representation and stability analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a general mathematical framework for chemotactic transport phenomena and specifically for the formation of oil and gas deposits, which require understanding of how localized coherent structures emerge in coupled two-phase media.

Method: Derives a nonlinear continuum transport model from an Einstein-type material-balance formulation, coupling diffusion with nonlinear gradient-driven transport and background depletion. Analyzes traveling-wave solutions, derives explicit profile representations, proves uniqueness up to translation, linearizes the perturbation operator around the traveling band, and reduces the system to a nonlinear third-order ODE.

Result: Establishes existence of coherent traveling bands in the transport-dominated regime, proves the mobile phase forms a unique one-hump profile with the background undergoing a positive bounded monotone logistic-type transition, obtains an explicit traveling profile representation, and proves finite-time perturbation bounds via maximum-principle arguments.

Conclusion: The model provides a rigorous mathematical characterization of localized traveling structures in two-phase transport, applicable to chemotaxis and subsurface oil/gas deposit formation, with complete existence, uniqueness, and stability results.

Abstract: We develop a nonlinear continuum transport model describing the formation of localized traveling structures in a coupled two-phase medium. The model is derived from an Einstein-type material-balance formulation in which displacement is generated by diffusion and by the gradient of a background-dependent transport mechanism. The resulting system couples diffusion, nonlinear gradient-driven transport, and depletion of the background phase. The proposed framework is applicable to general chemotactic transport and, in particular, to problems related to the formation of oil and gas deposits. We analyze traveling-wave solutions and establish the existence of coherent traveling bands in the transport-dominated regime. The mobile phase is shown to form a unique one-hump profile for any given reference time, while the background component undergoes a positive, bounded monotone logistic-type transition between asymptotic states. An explicit representation of the traveling profile is obtained, and uniqueness is proved up to translation. We further derive the linearized perturbation operator around the traveling band and establish finite-time perturbation bounds through a maximum-principle argument. Finally, the traveling-wave system is reduced to a nonlinear third-order ordinary differential equation for the background profile, providing an alternative characterization of the coherent structure.

</details>

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Analytical solutions to Maxwell's equations are essential for understanding causal and instantaneous behavior of electromagnetic fields, but are challenging to obtain in open-space settings with general initial values and source terms.

Method: An infinite-order estimation scheme is used, expressed as general function-to-function transformations that directly map initial values and source terms to the fields.

Result: Several case studies produce exact solutions for different initial and source configurations, demonstrating the method's validity.

Conclusion: The results provide both theoretical insight into electromagnetic field behavior and practical benchmarks for computational electromagnetics.

Abstract: Analytical solutions to Maxwell's equations are essential for understanding the causal and instantaneous behavior of electromagnetic fields, yet they are challenging to obtain in open-space settings with general initial values and source terms. This work uses an infinite-order estimation scheme that yields closed-form analytical solutions to Maxwell's equations. The scheme is expressed as general function-to-function transformations that directly map initial values and source terms to the fields. Several case studies demonstrate the method, producing exact solutions for different initial and source configurations. The results provide both theoretical insight and practical benchmarks for computational electromagnetics.

</details>


### [43] [Full-Density Degenerate Stagnation Points for Water Waves with General Vorticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04280)
*Lili Du,Chunlei Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves the nonexistence of full-density degenerate stagnation points for two-dimensional traveling gravity water waves with vorticity, using modified Weiss-type monotonicity and Almgren-type frequency formulas instead of the previously essential Bessel-type differential inequality. This yields uniform bounds for blow-ups and extends the Stokes conjecture to broader vorticity classes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to resolve the singular asymptotics of free surfaces near stagnation points in rotational water waves, specifically ruling out degenerate stagnation points beyond strict two-sided linear growth. They seek a new proof technique that circumvents the limitations of the previous Bessel-type differential inequality approach.

Method: The authors introduce modified Weiss-type monotonicity and modified Almgren-type frequency formulas, which together provide a new framework that completely avoids Bessel-type differential inequalities. These tools are applied to analyze stagnation point singularities and establish nonexistence results.

Result: The paper proves nonexistence of full-density degenerate stagnation points beyond the strict two-sided linear growth regime, obtains uniform bounds for frequency-normalized blow-ups in arbitrary dimension, and achieves strong convergence in dimension two. The Stokes conjecture is extended to a broader class of vorticity distributions.

Conclusion: The new monotonicity/frequency-based approach is a powerful alternative to previous techniques, enabling broader applicability and extending the Stokes conjecture for rotational waves to more general vorticity settings.

Abstract: In this paper, we revisit the singular asymptotics of the free surface near stagnation points for two-dimensional traveling gravity water waves with vorticity. We prove the nonexistence of full-density degenerate stagnation points beyond the strict two-sided linear growth regime.
  Our main tools are a modified Weiss-type monotonicity formula and a modified Almgren-type frequency formula. Together, they provide a new approach that completely avoids the use of a Bessel-type differential inequality, which is an essential tool used in the previous literature to prove the nonexistence of full-density degenerate stagnation points (Ann. I. H. Poincaré-AN, 29, 861--885, 2012).
  As consequences, we obtain uniform bounds for frequency-normalized blow-ups in arbitrary dimension and strong convergence in dimension two. As an application, we extend the Stokes conjecture for rotational waves to a broader class of vorticity distributions.

</details>


### [44] [Controllable subspaces and real-part observability for Schrödinger equations on $\mathbb T^d$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04357)
*Gengsheng Wang,Ming Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies internal controllability of Schrödinger equations on tori where controls act only through real parts, characterizing the maximal null-controllable subspace and showing uncontrollable directions correspond exactly to purely imaginary stationary modes. The characterization is derived via observability inequalities with sharp stationary correction terms, proven using real-part compactness-uniqueness arguments and complex-valued observability inputs with double-spectrum analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand controllability of Schrödinger equations under a physically motivated real-part constraint on controls, where full null controllability may fail, and to explicitly identify which directions are controllable versus uncontrollable.

Method: The paper derives observability inequalities with sharp stationary correction terms. For cylindrical open control regions, a real-part compactness-uniqueness argument is used. For admissible measurable product control regions in the shifted free case, the estimate is established for rough spacetime control sets via complex-valued observability inputs combined with a double-spectrum analysis from forward-backward Schrödinger evolution coupling in real-part observation.

Result: The maximal null-controllable subspace is characterized, and the uncontrollable directions are precisely the purely imaginary stationary modes. Explicit descriptions of controllable and uncontrollable directions are provided for Schrödinger equations with real-part controls.

Conclusion: Real-part controls lead to a real-linear control problem where full null controllability can fail, but the precise uncontrollable subspace can be fully identified through sharp observability estimates with stationary correction terms, applicable to both cylindrical open and rough measurable product control regions.

Abstract: We study internal controllability of Schrödinger equations on tori with controls acting only through their real parts. This real-part constraint leads to a real-linear control problem for which full null controllability may fail. We characterize the maximal null-controllable subspace and show that the uncontrollable directions are precisely given by the purely imaginary stationary modes. These results provide an explicit description of controllable and uncontrollable directions for Schrödinger equations with real-part controls.
  The characterization is obtained through observability inequalities with sharp stationary correction terms. For cylindrical open control regions, we prove such an estimate by a real-part compactness--uniqueness argument. For admissible measurable product control regions in the shifted free case, we establish the corresponding estimate for rough spacetime control sets. The proof combines complex-valued observability inputs with a double-spectrum analysis arising from the coupling of the forward and backward Schrödinger evolutions in the real-part observation.

</details>


### [45] [Morse index, Leray-Schauder degree and local uniqueness for multi-peak concentrating solutions of a fractional Schrödinger equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04380)
*Yinbin Deng,Baiping Feng,Qing Guo,Huafei Xie*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the linearized operator around positive k-peak solutions of the semiclassical fractional Schrödinger equation, showing the first k eigenvalues are negative, the next kN eigenvalues scale as ε² and are governed by the Hessians of V at the concentration points, while the rest of the spectrum is bounded away from zero. This yields the Morse index equals k plus the total number of negative Hessian eigenvalues, and nondegeneracy of all such solutions. Using modulation parametrization and Leray-Schauder degree, the paper proves uniqueness of positive solutions in each prescribed concentrating class for sufficiently small ε.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the spectral structure and Morse index of multi-peak (k-peak) solutions to the fractional Schrödinger equation, and to establish uniqueness of solutions concentrating at prescribed nondegenerate critical points of the potential V.

Method: The authors perform a complete spectral analysis of the linearized operator around k-peak solutions. They combine a unique modulation parametrization (to fix the exponentially small directions) with a Leray-Schauder degree computation to prove exact uniqueness for all sufficiently small ε, covering the entire energy-quantized class of concentrating solutions.

Result: The spectrum of the linearized operator is fully characterized: k uniformly negative eigenvalues, kN eigenvalues of order ε² determined by the Hessians D²V(ξ_j⁰), and the remaining spectrum uniformly bounded away from zero. The Morse index is exactly k plus the total number of negative Hessian eigenvalues, and all such solutions are nondegenerate. Moreover, there is exactly one positive solution in each prescribed concentrating class for small ε.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete and sharp spectral and uniqueness theory for multi-peak concentrating solutions of the semiclassical fractional Schrödinger equation, extending results beyond single-peak cases and applying to the whole energy-quantized class of solutions.

Abstract: We study positive $k$-peak solutions of the semiclassical fractional Schrödinger equation $\varepsilon^{2s}(-Δ)^s u+V(x)u=u^p$ in $\mathbb{R}^N$, concentrating at different nondegenerate critical points $ξ_1^0,\ldots,ξ_k^0$ of $V$. For every such family satisfying the natural energy quantization, we determine the complete low spectrum of the linearized operator. The first $k$ eigenvalues remain uniformly negative, the next $kN$ eigenvalues are of order $\varepsilon^2$ and are governed by the Hessians $D^2V(ξ_j^0)$, while the remaining spectrum is uniformly separated from zero. Consequently, the Morse index equals $k$ plus the total number of negative eigenvalues of these Hessians, and every such solution is nondegenerate. Combining a unique modulation parametrization with a Leray--Schauder degree computation, we further prove that, for all sufficiently small $\varepsilon$, the prescribed concentrating class contains exactly one positive solution. The result applies to the whole energy-quantized class, not only to a particular solution.

</details>


### [46] [Long-Time Dynamics and Modified Scattering for the Coulomb Vlasov--Hartree System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04402)
*Wenrui Huang,Mengyi Xie*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global well-posedness and characterizes the precise long-time dynamics of the three-dimensional Vlasov-Hartree system with Coulomb interaction, a classical-quantum model for Bose-Fermi mixtures, for small, regular, localized initial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Vlasov-Hartree system models Bose-Fermi mixtures at the interface of classical and quantum physics. Understanding its global well-posedness and long-time asymptotic behavior is essential for capturing the coupled dynamics of these mixed systems.

Method: The proof combines kinetic theory techniques (for the Vlasov equation) with vector-field methods typically used for dispersive equations (for the Hartree equation). The analysis handles the logarithmic corrections to free dynamics in both components.

Result: Global well-posedness is established for small, regular, localized initial data. The long-time dynamics exhibit logarithmic corrections: the Vlasov distribution scatters along free characteristics modified by the asymptotic bosonic profile, while the Hartree wave acquires a logarithmic phase correction from the asymptotic fermionic density. The results extend to shorter-range inverse-power interactions.

Conclusion: The coupling between the classical and quantum components remains visible at leading order in the asymptotic dynamics, manifested as logarithmic corrections to free evolution in both the Vlasov and Hartree components.

Abstract: We study the three-dimensional Vlasov--Hartree system with Coulomb interaction, a classical--quantum model for Bose--Fermi mixtures. For sufficiently small, regular, and localized initial data, we prove global well-posedness and determine the precise long-time dynamics. The coupling remains visible at leading order: the Vlasov distribution scatters along logarithmically corrected free characteristics determined by the asymptotic bosonic profile, while the Hartree wave acquires a logarithmic phase correction generated by the asymptotic fermionic density. Our proof combines techniques from kinetic theory with vector-field methods for dispersive equations and also extends to shorter-range inverse-power interactions.

</details>


### [47] [Tightness of and counterexamples to several quantum estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04411)

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
