Result: The paper establishes that the aforementioned quantum inequalities are tight (optimal), and provides new insights into the quantum Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture for anti-commuting Pauli strings.

Conclusion: The results confirm the optimality of several key quantum inequalities and shed light on open conjectures in quantum Boolean function analysis and quantum complexity theory.

Abstract: We prove here several tightness results for such quantum inequalities as the comparison of operator norm and product norm of $d$-local hamiltonians, Bohnenblust--Hille inequality for $d$-local hamiltonians and for quantum Fourier entropy-influence conjecture, we also discuss the quantum Aaronson--Ambainis conjecture in a special case of anti-commuting Pauli strings.

</details>


### [48] [Optimal Rigidity and Classification Results for the $k$-Hessian Equation of Lane--Emden Type](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04422)
*Wei Dai,Jingze Fu,Changfeng Gui,Guolin Qin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes optimal Liouville theorems and classification results for the k-Hessian Lane-Emden equation, closing the gap in the exponent range (p_-, p_*) and proving that the critical Hessian-Sobolev exponent p_* is the sharp Liouville threshold. For the critical case p=p_*, nontrivial solutions are classified as Aubin-Talenti type bubbles under various assumptions, providing fully nonlinear counterparts to classical results of Gidas-Spruck, Gidas-Ni-Nirenberg, and Caffarelli-Gidas-Spruck.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine the sharp Liouville threshold for the k-Hessian Lane-Emden equation. Previous results by Phuc-Verditsky and Ou covered p in (0,k] and (k,p_-], leaving a gap for p in (p_-, p_*). This paper aims to close this gap and establish the optimal exponent p_* as the critical threshold, along with classification results at the critical case.

Method: The authors prove optimal Liouville theorems via analysis of both C^2 solutions and Hessian-measure weak solutions. For the critical case p=p_*, they employ classification techniques to show nontrivial solutions must be Aubin-Talenti type bubbles, requiring different assumptions depending on the dimension n relative to 4k (unconditional for 2k<n≤4k, boundedness for n=4k+1,4k+2, and integral growth or pointwise asymptotic conditions for larger n and the limiting case n=2k).

Result: (1) Optimal Liouville theorem proving any nonnegative C^2 entire solution must be identically zero for p_-<p<p_*. (2) Same result for locally bounded Hessian-measure weak solutions. (3) Identification of p_* as the sharp Liouville threshold since radial positive solutions exist for p≥p_*. (4) Classification at p=p_*: nontrivial solutions must be Aubin-Talenti type bubbles under dimension-dependent assumptions.

Conclusion: The sharp Liouville threshold for the k-Hessian Lane-Emden equation is the critical Hessian-Sobolev exponent p_*. The paper completely resolves the open gap in the exponent range and provides classification results at the critical exponent, establishing fully nonlinear counterparts to the classical Liouville and classification theorems.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish optimal Liouville theorems and classification results for the \(k\)-Hessian Lane--Emden equation \[
  σ_k(-D^2u)=u^p\quad\text{in }\R^n,\qquad
  -D^2u\in\overline{Γ_k},\qquad u\geq 0, \] where \(2\leq k<\frac{n}{2}\) and $p>0$. Let $p_- = \frac{nk}{n-2k}$ and the critical Hessian--Sobolev exponent $p_* = \frac{(n+2)k}{n-2k}$. Phuc and Verbitsky proved nonexistence of positive solutions for \(k<p\leq p_-\), while Ou subsequently covered the cases \(p\in(0,k]\). We completely closed this gap and proved the optimal Liouville theorem for any \(p_-<p<p_*\): any nonnegative \(C^2\) entire solution must be identically zero. We also prove the optimal Liouville theorem for nonnegative locally bounded Hessian-measure weak solutions. This identifies the critical exponent \(p_*\) as the sharp Liouville threshold, since radial positive solutions exist for \(p\geq p_*\).
  For the critical case \(p=p_*\), we proved that nontrivial nonnegative \(C^2\) entire solution must be the Aubin-Talenti type bubble without any assumptions for \(2k<n\leq 4k\), under boundedness assumption for $n=4k+1$ and $4k+2$, and under some appropriate integral growth conditions or pointwise asymptotic behavior assumptions for any \(n>4k\) and the limiting case $n=2k$. In particular, we provide a fully nonlinear counterparts of the classical Liouville and classification theorems of Gidas--Spruck, Gidas--Ni--Nirenberg, and Caffarelli--Gidas--Spruck.

</details>


### [49] [Large-time behavior and grow-up rates of inhomogeneous semilinear heat equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04440)
*Kenta Kumagai,Yusuke Oka*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a semilinear heat equation in the unit ball with exponential nonlinearity and an inhomogeneous term f. When f exceeds a threshold, the bifurcation structure changes from the known f=0 case, eliminating the grow-up phenomenon. The authors determine sharp grow-up rates for N≥11 and find a dimension-specific log-log correction term that appears only for N=11.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how the presence of an inhomogeneous term f qualitatively changes the bifurcation structure and large-time behavior of solutions to the semilinear heat equation, which is known to undergo a critical transition at dimension N=10 in the homogeneous case.

Method: The paper analyzes the bifurcation structure of the stationary problem and connects it to the large-time behavior of the heat equation. It determines sharp grow-up rates for dimensions N≥11, with careful analysis of the threshold case N=11 where a log-log correction term appears.

Result: Once f exceeds a threshold, the bifurcation structure changes to a type not seen in the f=0 case, which causes the grow-up phenomenon to disappear. Sharp grow-up rates are established for N≥11, and a novel dimension-specific log-log correction term emerges uniquely for N=11 in the threshold case.

Conclusion: The inhomogeneous term f can fundamentally alter the bifurcation structure and suppress the grow-up phenomenon beyond a threshold, with quantitatively precise rates characterized for high dimensions, including a new N=11-specific correction term.

Abstract: We consider the semilinear heat equation in the unit ball with the exponential nonlinearity and an inhomogeneous term $f$. When $f=0$, it is known that the bifurcation structure of the stationary problem undergoes a qualitative change at the critical dimension $N=10$. This change affects the large-time behavior of solutions to the heat equation, and in particular, the grow-up phenomenon occurs for $N\ge 10$.
  In this paper, we show that once $f$ exceeds a threshold, the bifurcation structure changes to a type that does not appear in the case $f=0$. The change in the bifurcation structure leads to the disappearance of the grow-up phenomenon beyond the threshold. Moreover, we provide a quantitative characterization of this transition by determining the sharp grow-up rates for $N\ge 11$. In particular, we identify a new dimension-specific phenomenon in the threshold case: a log-log type correction term emerges in the grow-up rate only for $N=11$.

</details>


### [50] [Liouville-type theorems for coupled-drift Monge-Ampère equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04478)
*Ling Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies entire solutions and periodic correctors for a coupled-drift Monge-Ampère equation, providing sharp classification of solvability regimes for the whole-space problem and existence/uniqueness results for the periodic cell problem, with a novel scalar maximum-principle argument valid in all dimensions n≥2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the classification of entire smooth strictly convex solutions to the coupled-drift Monge-Ampère equation, and to establish existence and uniqueness results for the corresponding periodic cell problem, which is important for homogenization theory and the study of asymptotic behavior of solutions.

Method: The paper introduces a scalar maximum-principle argument valid in every dimension n≥2 to handle the null case a≠0, a·b=0. It analyzes the structure of solutions via the drift term a·Du and linear term b·x, and connects whole-space solutions to periodic correctors through asymptotic analysis.

Result: For V≡0: (i) all solutions are quadratic when a=b=0; (ii) no solutions exist when a≠0 and a·b≤0; (iii) non-quadratic solutions exist when a=0,b≠0 or a·b>0. For periodic V: existence and uniqueness of the cell problem is established, and any asymptotically quadratic solution must satisfy b=Aa, with bounded remainder forcing the solution to be the quadratic-periodic corrector up to a constant.

Conclusion: The paper achieves a complete classification of solvability for the coupled-drift Monge-Ampère equation on the whole space and provides a rigorous link between entire solutions and periodic correctors, establishing that the drift and linear terms must satisfy the constraint b=Aa for asymptotically quadratic solutions, which are then uniquely characterized.

Abstract: We study entire solutions and periodic correctors for the coupled-drift Monge-Ampère equation \[
  \det D^2u
  =
  \exp\{-a\cdot Du+b\cdot x+V(x)-c_0\},
  \quad
  D^2u>0. \] For \(V\equiv0\), we obtain a sharp classification of the whole-space solvability regimes: all entire smooth strictly convex solutions are quadratic when \(a=b=0\); no such solution exists when \(a\neq0\) and \(a\cdot b\le0\); and non-quadratic entire solutions exist when \(a=0\) and \(b\neq0\), or when \(a\cdot b>0\). The main new ingredient is a scalar maximum-principle argument valid in every dimension \(n\ge2\), which proves that the null case \(a\neq0\), \(a\cdot b=0\) admits no entire smooth strictly convex solution.
  For periodic \(V\), we establish the existence and uniqueness for the drifted cell problem \[
  \det(A+D^2ψ)
  =
  \exp\{-a\cdot Dψ+V-c_A\},
  \quad
  A+D^2ψ>0
  \quad\text{on }\mathbb T^n. \] We also prove that any asymptotically quadratic entire solution must satisfy \(b=Aa\). If its remainder is bounded, then the solution is the corresponding quadratic-periodic corrector up to an additive constant.

</details>


### [51] [Stochastic Keller Segel System with Porous Medium Diffusion and Nonlinear Chemotactic Sensitivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04614)
*Yiming Jiang,Haohang Li,Yawei Wei*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a stochastic Keller-Segel system on a bounded 1D domain with porous medium diffusion and nonlinear chemotactic sensitivity, establishing global existence of martingale solutions, uniform a priori estimates, and non-negativity preservation under the condition m ≥ 2a+1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand cell aggregation in complex environments where density-dependent diffusion, nonlinear chemotactic sensing, and random environmental fluctuations interact, and to determine conditions under which the system has well-defined global solutions despite the challenges of degenerate diffusion and stochastic noise.

Method: The proof combines a decoupled auxiliary system, energy estimates, and a stochastic Schauder-Tychonoff fixed point argument to handle degenerate diffusion, nonlinear drift, and stochastic perturbations simultaneously.

Result: For a ≥ 1 and m ≥ 2a+1, global existence of martingale solutions, uniform a priori estimates, and preservation of non-negativity are established. The condition m ≥ 2a+1 quantifies the balance needed between nonlinear chemotactic aggregation and porous-medium diffusion.

Conclusion: Stronger sensing response (larger a) requires stronger diffusion (larger m) to prevent excessive aggregation, revealing a quantitative balance condition for well-posedness of the stochastic aggregation model.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate a stochastic Keller--Segel system with porous medium diffusion and nonlinear chemotactic sensitivity on a bounded one-dimensional domain. The model describes cell aggregation in complex environments, where the dispersal of cells is governed by density-dependent diffusion $Δu^{[m]}$, reflecting the combined effects of porous media and population crowding, and the perception of chemoattractants follows Stevens' power law, leading to the nonlinear chemotactic sensitivity $\nabla\cdot(u\nabla v^{[a]})$. In addition, random environmental fluctuations are incorporated through multiplicative noise $u\,dW(t)$, which represents stochastic perturbations in population dynamics. For $a\geq1$ and $m\geq2a+1$, we establish the global existence of martingale solutions, uniform a priori estimates, and preservation of non-negativity. The condition $m\geq2a+1$ reveals a balance between nonlinear chemotactic aggregation and porous-medium diffusion: stronger sensing response requires stronger diffusion to prevent excessive aggregation. The proof combines a decoupled auxiliary system, energy estimates, and a stochastic Schauder--Tychonoff fixed point argument to overcome the difficulties caused by degenerate diffusion, nonlinear drift, and stochastic perturbations.

</details>


### [52] [Sharp regularity for the periodic Camassa--Holm equation in critical Triebel--Lizorkin spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04619)
*Wenhai Shan,Xiao-Song Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes sharp well-posedness and norm inflation results for the Camassa-Holm equation in critical Triebel-Lizorkin spaces on the torus. Local Hadamard well-posedness is proven at the endpoint p=1 (for q<∞), while norm inflation is demonstrated for p>1 and all q. The authors also complement existing results in critical Besov and higher-regularity Triebel-Lizorkin spaces.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To achieve a complete and sharp understanding of well-posedness for the Camassa-Holm equation in critical Triebel-Lizorkin spaces on the periodic domain, filling gaps where local well-posedness may or may not hold depending on the function space parameters.

Method: Positive results use a Lipschitz stability theorem for the periodic Green operator under degree-one Lagrangian flows. Negative results (norm inflation) are based on a nested smooth atomic construction on the torus, adapted from its real-line counterpart.

Result: Local Hadamard well-posedness holds at the endpoint p=1 with 1≤q<∞ in the critical space F^{1+1/p}_{p,q}(𝕋). Norm inflation occurs for 1<p<∞ and 1≤q≤∞. Results in critical Besov and higher-regularity Triebel-Lizorkin spaces are also complemented.

Conclusion: The critical regularity threshold for the Camassa-Holm equation on the torus exhibits a sharp dichotomy: well-posedness at p=1 (for finite q) and norm inflation for all p>1, establishing the exact boundary of well-posedness in the critical Triebel-Lizorkin scale.

Abstract: We establish a sharp well-posedness and norm inflation theory for the Camassa--Holm equation in critical Triebel--Lizorkin $F^{1+1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})$. At the endpoint $p=1$, we prove local Hadamard well-posedness for $1\le q<\infty$. In contrast, we prove norm inflation for $1<p<\infty$ and $1\le q\le\infty$. We also complement the local well-posedness in the critical Besov spaces and higher-regularity Triebel--Lizorkin spaces. The positive results rely on a Lipschitz stability theorem for the periodic Green operator under degree-one Lagrangian flows. The negative result is based on a nested smooth atomic construction on the torus, adapted from its real-line counterpart.

</details>


### [53] [Convergence Analysis of a Finite-Volume Scheme for a Microglia--Amyloid Chemotaxis Model with Measure-Valued Vascular Boundary Sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04785)
*Elmahdi Erraji*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a parabolic-parabolic chemotaxis system modeling microglial recruitment toward an amyloid-β signal in Alzheimer's disease, with a Radon measure-valued Neumann boundary influx and nonlocal sensing. The authors introduce a weak-solution concept, construct a fully implicit upwind finite-volume scheme, and prove its convergence to a nonnegative weak solution of the continuous problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide rigorous mathematical analysis for a chemotaxis model of microglial recruitment in Alzheimer's disease, which involves a low-regularity boundary measure source that complicates the analysis and requires a carefully designed solution concept and numerical scheme.

Method: The authors develop a weak-solution framework suited to the low regularity from the boundary measure source. They discretize the system using a fully implicit upwind finite-volume scheme, with the boundary source discretized via its exact mass on each boundary face-time cell. They then establish existence, positivity, and uniform estimates (mass, energy, discrete-gradient, compactness) and prove subsequential convergence to a weak solution.

Result: Existence and positivity of discrete solutions are established, along with uniform mass, energy, discrete-gradient, and compactness estimates. Subsequential convergence of the discrete solutions to a nonnegative weak solution of the continuous chemotaxis problem is proven.

Conclusion: The paper provides the first rigorous convergence analysis of a finite-volume approximation for this chemotaxis model with measure-valued boundary influx, confirming well-posedness in the weak sense while opening a numerical pathway for simulating microglial dynamics in Alzheimer's disease settings.

Abstract: We study a parabolic-parabolic chemotaxis system motivated by microglial recruitment toward an amyloid-$β$-associated signal in Alzheimer's disease. The signal is subject to a nonnegative Radon measure-valued Neumann influx on a vascular portion of the boundary, while microglial cells respond to a nonlocal spatial average of the signal. For a fixed sensing length $σ>0$, the chemotactic velocity is $b_σ[v]=\nabla K_σ[v]$. For every fixed $σ>0$, the nonlocal operator maps finite signal mass into a bounded spatially Lipschitz velocity field. We introduce a weak-solution concept adapted to the low regularity induced by the boundary measure and construct a fully implicit upwind finite-volume approximation in which the boundary source is discretized through its exact mass on each boundary face-time cell. We establish existence and positivity of discrete solutions, together with uniform mass, energy, discrete-gradient, and compactness estimates. Finally, we prove subsequential convergence of the discrete solutions toward a nonnegative weak solution of the continuous problem.

</details>


### [54] [The IBVP for the Navier-Stokes equations in the half-space in a class of weighted Lebesgue spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04621)
*Angelica Pia Di Feola,Vittorio Pane*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the initial-boundary value problem for Navier-Stokes equations in a half-space with initial data in a weighted Lebesgue space, where weights are products of powers of distances from finitely many fixed points. The authors prove local existence and uniqueness of smooth solutions, derive L^q-estimates for q>n, and establish spatial asymptotic behavior of the velocity field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The work extends the weighted Lebesgue space framework introduced by Di Feola and Pane for the Stokes system to the full Navier-Stokes initial-boundary value problem, and generalizes results obtained by Maremonti and Pane for the Cauchy problem to the half-space setting.

Method: The analysis builds on the weighted Lebesgue space framework with product-of-powers weights defined from distances to finitely many fixed points, extending the previous Stokes system analysis to the Navier-Stokes equations and adapting the Cauchy problem results of Maremonti and Pane to the initial-boundary value problem in the half-space.

Result: The paper proves local existence and uniqueness of smooth solutions, derives L^q-estimates with q>n, and establishes the spatial asymptotic behavior of the velocity field.

Conclusion: The paper completes and extends the prior analysis of the Stokes system to the nonlinear Navier-Stokes initial-boundary value problem in the half-space, providing existence, uniqueness, L^q-estimates, and asymptotic decay results under weighted Lebesgue initial data.

Abstract: We study the initial-boundary value problem for the Navier-Stokes equations in the half-space with initial data belonging to a suitable weighted Lebesgue space. More precisely, we consider a weighted Lebesgue space associated with a weight function defined as a product of powers of the distances from finitely many fixed points. This framework was introduced in a previous work by A. P. Di Feola and V. Pane (J. Math. Anal. Appl. 558 (2026), 130390) for the study of the initial-boundary value problem for the Stokes system. The present paper completes that analysis and, at the same time, generalizes to the initial-boundary value problem the results obtained by Maremonti and Pane (J. Math. Fluid Mech. 27 (2025), Art. 2) for the Navier-Stokes Cauchy problem. We prove the existence (local) and uniqueness of a smooth solution and derive $L^q$-estimates, with $q>n$, together with the spatial asymptotic behavior of the velocity field.

</details>


### [55] [Stochastic Two-Species Chemotaxis Competition: Global Well Posedness and Continuous Dependence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04636)
*Yiming Jiang,Haohang Li,Yawei Wei*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global well-posedness for a stochastic two-species competition Keller-Segel model on bounded domains in R^n (n=1,2), establishing unique global adapted nonnegative mild solutions when self-damping exponents exceed two, along with continuous dependence in probability on initial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Two species responding to the same chemical signal in a chemotaxis-competition system face simultaneous challenges from chemotactic movement, interspecific competition, and environmental noise. Understanding whether such stochastic systems admit global solutions is essential for modeling realistic biological populations under uncertainty.

Method: The authors analyze a stochastic two-species chemotaxis-competition system with nonlinear competition and multiplicative noise. They control L^p estimates containing sign-indefinite terms (arising from coupled chemotactic fluxes and stochastic terms) using parabolic estimates for the signal equation, superquadratic self-damping, and stochastic convolution estimates. Global well-posedness is proven via mild solution theory with adaptedness and nonnegativity.

Result: When self-damping exponents exceed two, the system admits a unique global adapted nonnegative mild solution. The solution exhibits continuous dependence in probability on admissible initial data over finite time intervals, showing that small initial population perturbations lead to small solution perturbations in probability.

Conclusion: The paper establishes global well-posedness for the stochastic two-species Keller-Segel competition model under the stated assumptions, with the superquadratic self-damping condition (exponent > 2) playing a crucial role in controlling the solutions and ensuring stability with respect to initial data.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate a stochastic two species competition Keller--Segel model. Chemotactic movement, interspecific competition, and environmental fluctuations may act simultaneously when two species respond to the same chemical signal. We consider a stochastic two-species chemotaxis--competition system on a bounded smooth domain $\mathcal O\subset\mathbb R^n$, $n\in\{1,2\}$. Both species contribute to the production of the signal and move along its gradient; their population equations also include nonlinear competition and multiplicative noise. If the self-damping exponents exceed two, we prove that the system has a unique global adapted nonnegative mild solution. We also establish continuous dependence in probability on admissible initial data over finite time intervals. The results prove the global well-posedness under the stated assumptions and we still show that small changes in the initial populations, in probability, to small changes in the solution. Due to the two coupled chemotactic fluxes and the stochastic terms, the $L^p$ estimates contain terms with no fixed sign. They are controlled by parabolic estimates for the signal equation, superquadratic self-damping, and stochastic convolution estimates.

</details>


### [56] [Almost sure local well-posedness for the nonlinear Schrödinger equations on $\Bbb T^d$ with non-algebraic nonlinearity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04643)
*Yongming Luo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes almost sure local well-posedness for the Cauchy problem of the nonlinear Schrödinger equation on the torus with random initial data and general non-algebraic power-type nonlinearities, covering all spatial dimensions and the full mass-supercritical range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extend existing random data well-posedness results for NLS from low-dimensional algebraic nonlinearities to general non-algebraic power-type nonlinearities in all spatial dimensions and the entire mass-supercritical regime allowed by the natural condition 0 < s_c < 1+a.

Method: Develops a frequency-gaining probabilistic refinement of the Galilean bilinear estimates by Kwak and Kwon. The gauge decomposition introduces three new term types (mean-free coefficient, opposite-phase interaction, scalar remainder), controlled via new resonance counting and large deviation arguments, with closure through a phase-adapted two-component contraction.

Result: Establishes almost sure local well-posedness in every spatial dimension for the whole mass-supercritical range 0 < s_c < 1+a. For the energy-critical case, extends prior low-dimensional algebraic results (Nahmod--Staffilani, Yue) to all dimensions d ≥ 3, including higher-dimensional non-algebraic models.

Conclusion: The probabilistic refinement of Galilean bilinear estimates, combined with new resonance counting and large deviation arguments, successfully overcomes the challenges of non-algebraic nonlinearities in the random setting, achieving dimension-independent local well-posedness results for the full admissible mass-supercritical range.

Abstract: We study the Cauchy problem for the nonlinear Schrödinger equation on $\mathbb T^d$ with random initial data and a general non-algebraic power-type nonlinearity. We establish almost sure local well-posedness in every spatial dimension and for the whole mass-supercritical range allowed by the natural condition $0<s_{\mathrm c}<1+a$. The main new ingredient is a frequency-gaining probabilistic refinement of the Galilean bilinear estimates recently developed by Kwak and Kwon \cite{KwakKwon}. In the random setting, the gauge decomposition gives rise to three new types of terms: a mean-free coefficient, an opposite-phase interaction, and a scalar remainder. We control them by new resonance counting and large deviation arguments, and close the local theory through a phase-adapted two-component contraction. In the energy-critical case, our result extends the low-dimensional algebraic theories of Nahmod--Staffilani \cite{NahmodStaffilani15} and Yue \cite{Yue21} to every dimension $d\geq3$, including the higher-dimensional non-algebraic models.

</details>


### [57] [Global existence of solutions for nonlinear damped wave equations in an exterior domain with nonlinearities of derivative type](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04671)
*Tuan Anh Dao,Dinh Van Duong,Masahiro Ikeda*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes the global (in time) existence of small data solutions to the semi-linear damped wave equation with derivative-type power nonlinearity in 2D exterior domains, a result not previously achieved. The proof uses a judiciously constructed time-weighted function space and nonlinear estimates compatible with fractional powers of the Dirichlet Laplacian tied to semigroup decay structure. Sharp large-time behavior estimates for the time derivative of solutions are also provided.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study of semi-linear damped wave equations with derivative-type nonlinearities in exterior domains has not been addressed in prior work. Establishing global existence of small data solutions in 2D exterior domains fills a gap in the literature, and understanding the large time behavior of solutions is important for theoretical completeness.

Method: The proof constructs a judicious time-weighted function space and derives nonlinear estimates that are compatible with fractional powers of the Dirichlet Laplacian, exploiting the semigroup decay structure associated with the damped wave equation in exterior domains.

Result: Global (in time) existence of small data solutions is proven for the semi-linear damped wave equation with power nonlinearity of derivative type in 2D exterior domains. Additionally, sharp estimates for the large time behavior of the time derivative of the obtained global solutions are derived.

Conclusion: The paper successfully establishes previously unavailable global existence results for small data solutions in 2D exterior domains and provides sharp large-time decay estimates for the time derivative, contributing new analytical tools for this class of problems.

Abstract: In this paper, we are interested in considering the semi-linear damped wave equation with the power nonlinearity of derivative type in $2$D exterior domains to indicate the global (in time) existence of small data solutions, which has never appeared in previous studies. Our proof is based on constructing a judicious time-weighted function space and demonstrating nonlinear estimates compatible with fractional powers of the Dirichlet Laplacian linked to the semigroup decay structure. Moreover, the large time behavior of the time derivative of the obtained global solutions and its sharp estimate are also discussed in this paper.

</details>


### [58] [Inverse Geometric Diffraction by a Cone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04675)
*Gang Bao,Xi Chen,Shuai Lu,Kuangmiao Xiong*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper develops a rigorous inverse scattering theory for recovering a strictly convex conical obstacle in R³ using diffraction coefficients (lens data) and arrival times of diffracted waves, reducing the problem to identifying reflected wavefronts on the sphere S² and recovering the obstacle via broken geodesics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish the first rigorous inverse theory corresponding to the Geometrical Theory of Diffraction (GTD), addressing the inverse problem of recovering conical obstacles from diffracted wave measurements in reflection shadow regions.

Method: The approach incorporates: (1) using lens data/arrival times to determine the cone tip location; (2) using diffraction coefficients to reconstruct cone shape; (3) reducing inverse diffraction by a cone in R³ to identifying reflected wavefronts in S²; (4) constructing the Hadamard parametrix for half waves near the wavefront; (5) using topological properties of broken geodesics on S² for obstacle recovery.

Result: The framework successfully exploits analytic and geometric structures of diffracted wave fields from GTD and provides a complete reconstruction procedure: tip location from arrival data and cone shape from diffraction coefficients.

Conclusion: This work establishes the first rigorous inverse theory corresponding to GTD, linking diffraction phenomena to geometric inverse problems on the sphere through broken geodesics.

Abstract: Consider the inverse problem of recovering a strictly convex conical obstacle in $\mathbb{R}^3$ from the diffraction coefficients along with arrival directions (lens data) or arrival times of diffracted waves. The incident wave is a spherical pulse emanating from a point, and the measurements of diffracted waves are taken at an arbitrarily sized receiver placed within the reflection shadow. Specifically, the lens data or arrival times determine the location of the tip, whereas the diffraction coefficients reconstruct the shape of the cone. Since diffraction coefficients are described by half waves over the complement of the cone base in $\mathbb{S}^2$, we reduce inverse diffraction by a cone in $\mathbb{R}^3$ to identifying the reflected wavefront in $\mathbb{S}^2$ and recovering the obstacle using reflected rays on the sphere. The former is accomplished by constructing the Hadamard parametrix for half waves near the wavefront, whereas the latter relies on the topological properties of broken geodesics on $\mathbb{S}^2$. The framework developed in this paper exploits the analytic and geometric structures of diffracted wave fields characterized in the Geometrical Theory of Diffraction, and establishes, for the first time, a rigorous inverse theory corresponding to GTD.

</details>


### [59] [On the absence of point defects in biaxial Landau--de Gennes models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04908)
*Haotong Fu,Huaijie Wang,Wei Wang,Zhifei Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves that local minimizers of a sextic-potential Landau–de Gennes energy for nematic liquid crystals, in the small-elastic-constant limit, converge to harmonic maps into a biaxial vacuum manifold that have no interior point singularities, using a geometric identification with a rescaled Berger metric.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the regularity of energy-minimizing configurations in biaxial nematic liquid crystals and to determine whether interior point defects can exist in the biaxial setting—contrasting with the uniaxial theory where such defects are known to occur.

Method: The proof identifies the lifted Frobenius metric on the universal cover S^3 with a rescaled Berger metric, constructs a smooth variation field adapted to the Hopf direction, and derives a quantitative instability estimate that contradicts the shifted stability inequality inherited from local minimality.

Result: The limiting harmonic map Q_0 has no interior point singularities, ruling out interior point defects in the biaxial setting. The result is sharp, as point defects are known to exist in the uniaxial theory.

Conclusion: The authors establish the absence of interior point singularities for energy-minimizing harmonic maps into the biaxial vacuum manifold, replacing round-sphere test fields with a target-specific construction, and thereby sharpening the regularity theory for biaxial nematic liquid crystals.

Abstract: We study local minimizers of a sextic-potential Landau--de Gennes energy for nematic liquid crystals in the small-elastic-constant limit. Under the uniform energy and $L^\infty$ bounds, these minimizers converge to a locally energy-minimizing harmonic map $\mathbf{Q}_0$ into a biaxial vacuum manifold. The main result of this paper is that such a limiting map $\mathbf{Q}_0$ has no interior point singularities. The proof relies on a geometric identification of the lifted Frobenius metric on the universal cover $\mathbb{S}^3$ with a rescaled Berger metric. For a hypothetical tangent cone at a point singularity, its link is a nonconstant harmonic two-sphere into the Berger sphere. We construct a smooth variation field adapted to the Hopf direction and show that it induces a quantitative instability estimate, in contradiction with the shifted stability inequality inherited from local minimality. This replaces the usual round-sphere test fields by a target-specific construction and rules out interior point defects in the biaxial setting. The result is sharp in view of the well-known existence of point defects in the uniaxial theory.

</details>


### [60] [Modulational spectrum of infinite-depth hydroelastic Stokes waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04938)
*Ting-Yang Hsiao,Zirui Li,Ye Zhang,Chengbin Zhu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper determines the complete local Bloch spectrum bifurcating from the origin for small-amplitude periodic hydroelastic Stokes waves in infinite depth, under gravity, surface tension, and elastic bending. It constructs real-analytic Stokes-wave branches, decouples the four eigenvalues from the defective zero eigenvalue into Benjamin–Feir and long-wave pairs, derives a sharp modulational stability criterion, and identifies a bounded stability island from elastic bending.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand modulational (Benjamin–Feir) instability of periodic hydroelastic Stokes waves in infinite depth, which arises from a defective zero eigenvalue of the linearized system—a problem that is singular compared to the finite-depth case and not previously analyzed in full Bloch-spectrum detail.

Method: Analytic spectral perturbation theory combined with Hamiltonian-reversible reductions to decouple four eigenvalues emerging from the defective zero eigenvalue; explicit discriminant analysis for the Benjamin–Feir pair; asymptotic analysis of the long-wave pair scaling; phase-diagram construction in the surface-tension-bending parameter plane.

Result: (1) Long-wave pair remains purely imaginary with a singular scale O(√|μ|). (2) Benjamin–Feir pair governed by an explicit discriminant whose sign gives a sharp stability/instability criterion. (3) A bounded stability island exists, generated by elastic bending. (4) In the unstable region (away from drift degeneracy), Benjamin–Feir branches form a local figure-eight. (5) Zero-bending limits recover known gravity/capillary results; the infinite-depth problem is singular due to the O(√|μ|) scale.

Conclusion: The complete local Bloch spectrum for infinite-depth hydroelastic Stokes waves is characterized exactly, providing a sharp modulational stability criterion and revealing the singular nature of the infinite-depth limit, with elastic bending capable of stabilizing the waves in a bounded parameter region.

Abstract: We determine the complete local Bloch spectrum bifurcating from the origin for small-amplitude periodic hydroelastic Stokes waves in infinite depth, under the combined effects of gravity, surface tension, and elastic bending. Away from the Wilton-type resonance set, we construct a real-analytic Stokes-wave branch and analyze the four eigenvalues emerging from the defective zero eigenvalue of the linearized hydroelastic Euler system. Using analytic spectral perturbation theory and Hamiltonian-reversible reductions, we decouple them into a Benjamin--Feir pair and a long-wave pair. The long-wave pair remains purely imaginary and has the singular scale $\cO(\sqrt{|μ|})$, whereas the Benjamin--Feir pair is governed by an explicit discriminant whose leading sign yields a sharp criterion for modulational stability and instability. We derive the exact non-resonant phase diagram in the surface-tension-bending parameter plane and identify a bounded stability island generated by elastic bending. In the unstable region, and away from a drift degeneracy, the Benjamin--Feir branches form a local figure-eight curve. In the zero-bending limit, the reduced coefficients recover the known deep-water gravity and gravity-capillary results, while the change from the finite-depth $\cO(|μ|)$ long-wave scale to $\cO(\sqrt{|μ|})$ shows that the infinite-depth problem is singular.

</details>


### [61] [Recovery of time-dependent coefficients for the convection-diffusion equation on conformally transversally anisotropic manifolds from partial data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04970)
*Boya Liu,Anamika Purohit*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper addresses an inverse problem for the convection-diffusion equation on conformally transversally anisotropic (CTA) manifolds of dimension at least three, proving that a partial input-output operator uniquely recovers both the time-dependent convection term and density coefficient up to a natural gauge, under the assumption that attenuated geodesic ray transforms are injective on the transversal manifold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to extend uniqueness results for inverse coefficient problems in convection-diffusion equations from simpler geometries to more general (CTA) manifolds, using partial boundary data rather than full data, which is more physically realistic.

Method: The analysis uses CTA manifold geometry (conformal embeddings into a product of the real line and a transversal manifold) combined with injectivity assumptions on attenuated geodesic ray transforms of one-forms and functions to establish uniqueness of the recovered coefficients from the partial input-output operator.

Result: The paper proves that both the time-dependent convection term and the density coefficient are uniquely determined (up to a natural gauge) from partial boundary measurements on CTA manifolds of dimension at least three.

Conclusion: The inverse problem is well-posed in terms of uniqueness on CTA manifolds when the transversal manifold admits injective attenuated geodesic ray transforms, providing a general framework for recovering multiple coefficients from partial data.

Abstract: We study an inverse problem of recovering a time-dependent convection term and density coefficient of the convection-diffusion equation from partial data on a certain type of compact Riemannian manifold of dimension at least three. We prove that the knowledge of a partial input-output operator determines both coefficients uniquely up to a natural gauge. Our geometric setting is conformally transversally anisotropic manifolds, that is, compact Riemannian manifolds with boundary that are conformally embedded into a product of the Euclidean line and a transversal manifold. Additionally, we assume that the attenuated geodesic ray transforms of one-forms and functions are both injective on the transversal manifold.

</details>


### [62] [Nondegeneracy and Morse Index of Ginzburg--Landau Vortices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04976)
*Manuel del Pino,Yong Liu,Monica Musso,Juncheng Wei,Wen Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that the standard degree-two and degree-three vortex solutions of the Ginzburg-Landau equation are nondegenerate and computes their Morse indices using explicit bounds on the modulus of these solutions and a comparison argument.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Understanding the nondegeneracy and Morse indices of vortex solutions is fundamental for stability analysis and the structure of the solution space of the Ginzburg-Landau equation. While degree-one vortices are well understood, this work extends the rigorous analysis to higher-degree solutions.

Method: The proof uses novel explicit upper and lower bounds on the modulus of the degree-two and degree-three vortex solutions, combined with a comparison argument to establish nondegeneracy and compute Morse indices.

Result: The degree-two and degree-three standard vortex solutions are proven to be nondegenerate, and their Morse indices are explicitly computed.

Conclusion: The approach is expected to generalize to higher-degree vortex solutions, providing a pathway for analyzing the nondegeneracy of all vortex solutions in the Ginzburg-Landau equation.

Abstract: We prove that the standard degree-two and degree-three vortex solutions of the Ginzburg-Landau equation are nondegenerate. Their Morse indices are also computed. The proof relies on new explicit upper and lower bounds of the modulus of these solutions and a comparison argument. It is expected that our method can be generalized to study higher degree solutions.

</details>


### [63] [A spectral approach to the narrow escape problem in two-dimensional domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05007)
*Louis Carillo,Tony Lelièvre,Thomas Normand,Urbain Vaes*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the exit time and exit point distribution of Brownian motion in a 2D domain with reflecting boundaries and small disjoint exit windows, showing that under the quasi-stationary distribution starting condition, a precise asymptotic description of the exit event can be obtained.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the law of exit events in confined domains with small escape regions, which is relevant to many physical and biological applications where particles escape through narrow openings.

Method: The authors study Brownian motion in a two-dimensional domain with reflecting boundary conditions except for small disjoint exit windows, and analyze the exit time and exit point distribution in the limit of infinitesimally small windows, assuming the process starts from the quasi-stationary distribution.

Result: A precise description of the exit event is obtained in the limit of infinitely small exit windows.

Conclusion: The paper provides an exact asymptotic characterization of the exit time and exit point for Brownian motion escaping through small windows under quasi-stationary starting conditions.

Abstract: We study the law of the exit time and exit point of a Brownian motion in a two-dimensional domain with reflecting boundary conditions, except on small disjoint exit windows through which the stochastic process can escape the domain. In the limit of infinitely small exit windows, it is natural to assume that the process starts from the quasi-stationary distribution. In this setting, we obtain a precise description of the exit event.

</details>


### [64] [A point-free theory of quantitative homogenization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05077)
*Thuyen Dang,Yuliya Gorb,Silvia Jiménez Bolaños*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper introduces a purely operator-theoretic framework for quantitative homogenization that avoids traditional large-scale spatial regularity and probabilistic assumptions, using algebraic structures of multiscale operators and Hilbert space geometry to derive explicit norm resolvent estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional homogenization theory relies heavily on large-scale spatial regularity and probabilistic assumptions. The authors seek a point-free, operator-theoretic approach inspired by Tartar's vision that can unify different homogenization settings (stationary, non-stationary, periodic, quasi-periodic, stochastic) under a single abstract framework.

Method: The framework uses an orthogonal decomposition of the state space where the effective macroscopic dynamics and abstract corrector emerge naturally via a Schur complement. To quantify convergence rates, a frequency-splitting technique is introduced, and a generalized Sylvester equation is solved to control the commutator between the differential structure and oscillatory material properties.

Result: The framework unifies stationary, non-stationary, periodic, quasi-periodic, and stochastic homogenization. The physical distinctions between media types and their convergence rates are entirely characterized by the behavior of the spectral measure of the microscopic derivative operator near zero frequency.

Conclusion: The abstract operator-theoretic perspective provides a unified quantitative homogenization theory where the spectral measure near zero frequency plays the central role in determining convergence rates across all media types.

Abstract: We introduce a purely operator-theoretic framework for quantitative
  homogenization that bypasses the traditional reliance on large-scale
  spatial regularity and probabilistic assumptions. Inspired by
  Tartar's vision of a point-free theory, we derive explicit
  norm resolvent estimates using only the algebraic structure of
  multiscale operators and the abstract geometry of Hilbert spaces. In
  this framework, the effective macroscopic dynamics and the abstract
  corrector emerge naturally from an orthogonal decomposition of the
  state space, governed algebraically by a Schur complement. To
  quantify the convergence rate, we introduce a frequency-splitting
  technique and solve a generalized Sylvester equation that controls
  the commutator between the differential structure and the highly
  oscillatory material properties.
  This abstract perspective unifies stationary, non-stationary,
  periodic, quasi-periodic, and stochastic homogenization. We
  demonstrate that the physical distinctions between these media--and
  their respective convergence rates--are entirely captured by the
  behavior of the spectral measure of the microscopic derivative
  operator near zero frequency.

</details>


### [65] [Counterexamples to Schiffer's Conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05114)
*Gonzalo Cao-Labora,Jaume de Dios Pont*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper disproves both the Schiffer conjecture and Pompeiu's problem in R^2 by constructing infinitely many planar domains that are not balls but satisfy the required conditions. The construction uses N-fold symmetric domains with sufficiently large N, based on a relaxed problem where N is treated as a real number, combined with bifurcation theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to resolve two long-standing open conjectures (Schiffer and Pompeiu) in the plane, which have been open for decades and are fundamental to spectral geometry and integral geometry. Disproving them requires careful construction of counterexamples.

Method: The approach involves a novel strategy of relaxing the problem by allowing N (the symmetry order) to be any real number, rather than just natural numbers. Bifurcation theory is applied to this relaxed problem, and it is shown that the local bifurcation branch size can be made independent of N. This enables branches starting near integers to reach integer values of N, yielding counterexamples to both conjectures.

Result: The authors construct infinitely many planar domains that are not balls but satisfy the Schiffer condition (constant Neumann eigenfunction at the boundary) and the Pompeiu condition (nonzero functions integrating to zero over all rigid motions). These domains are N-fold symmetric with N sufficiently large.

Conclusion: Both the Schiffer conjecture and Pompeiu's problem are disproved in R^2, providing definitive answers to these long-standing open problems in the plane.

Abstract: The Schiffer conjecture states that if a smooth domain $Ω\subset \mathbb{R}^n$ admits a Neumann eigenfunction of the Laplacian which is constant at the boundary, then the domain is a ball. It is intimately related to Pompeiu's problem, stating that if a nonzero function integrates zero over any rigid motion of $Ω$, then $Ω$ is a ball.
  We disprove both conjectures in $\mathbb{R}^2$, constructing infinitely many planar domains $Ω$ which are not balls and satisfy the conditions above. Our domains are $N$-fold symmetric, with $N$ sufficiently large. Our approach is based on a novel strategy of considering a relaxed problem where $N$ can be any real number (which corresponds to the Schiffer problem only when $N$ is a natural number). We then apply bifurcation theory to this relaxed problem, showing that the size of the local bifurcation branch can be taken independently of $N$. This result allows us to conclude that branches starting with $N$ sufficiently close to an integer reach integer values of $N$.

</details>


### [66] [Derivation of 2D Vlasov-Poisson for classical particles with Coulomb interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04104)
*Mitia Duerinckx,Pierre-Emmanuel Jabin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper presents the first derivation of the 2D Vlasov-Poisson equation for classical particles with Coulomb interactions without any microscopic cutoff, using a dual hierarchical approach to mean-field limits combined with refined analysis of dual BBGKY hierarchies on linearized correlations that exploits kinetic regularization effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The derivation of mean-field limits for singular interaction potentials, particularly Coulomb interactions, has been a long-standing challenge. Prior results required microscopic cutoffs to handle the singularity, and removing this limitation is a fundamental open problem in kinetic theory and mathematical physics.

Method: The proof combines the authors' recent dual hierarchical approach to mean-field limits with a refined analysis of dual BBGKY hierarchies on linearized correlations. The key innovation is exploiting kinetic regularization effects to control the singular interaction without needing any cutoff.

Result: The result provides the first cutoff-free derivation of 2D Vlasov-Poisson for Coulomb interactions, and extends more generally to arbitrary singular interaction forces $K \in L^{2-\eta}_{loc}$ with small $\eta>0$ in any dimension, as well as to Brownian particles. The result holds globally in time provided the mean-field solution remains sufficiently regular.

Conclusion: The derivation succeeds without microscopic cutoffs by leveraging kinetic regularization within the dual BBGKY framework, establishing a significant milestone in rigorous mean-field limits for singular interactions.

Abstract: We obtain the first derivation of the 2D Vlasov-Poisson equation for classical particles with Coulomb interactions without any microscopic cutoff. The proof relies on our recent dual hierarchical approach to mean-field limits, together with a refined analysis of dual BBGKY hierarchies on linearized correlations, based on kinetic regularization effects. The result holds more generally for arbitrary singular interaction forces $K\in L^{2-η}_{loc}$ with $η>0$ small enough, in any dimension, and it extends to Brownian particles. It holds globally in time as long as the mean-field solution is regular enough.

</details>


### [67] [Full-Tail Dynamical Rigidity Forced by Atomic Navier-Stokes Energy Concentration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04138)
*Hao Huang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that for a smooth unforced 3D Navier-Stokes flow approaching a finite terminal time on the flat torus, each point atom in the endpoint kinetic-energy density measure forces a full-tail dynamical rigidity. This rigidity leads to uniform two-parameter saturation of the constrained Oseen propagator and its adjoint, vanishing first-order dissipation, and consequently infinite delayed second-order action with non-integrable enstrophy production for sufficiently late descendants, implying a delayed second-order operator budget determined by the parent must fail near the endpoint.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the fine structure of potential singularity formation in 3D Navier-Stokes equations near a terminal time, particularly how energy concentration at endpoint atoms imposes rigid dynamical constraints on the flow's full tail behavior.

Method: The authors use a preassigned level-crossing catalogue combined with nested local Hodge projections to construct orthogonal packets. From the packet tail, a single backward adjoint is extracted whose terminal energy concentrates at the atom. Cauchy saturation is then applied to lock this adjoint to late packets, yielding uniform two-parameter saturation results for both the constrained Oseen propagator and its adjoint.

Result: Every sufficiently late fixed-root descendant has infinite delayed second-order action and non-integrable positive enstrophy production. A delayed second-order operator budget determined solely by the Navier-Stokes parent must fail arbitrarily close to the endpoint.

Conclusion: The endpoint energy atom forces a strong rigidity on the entire tail dynamics, leading to unavoidable singular behavior expressed through operator saturation and unbounded delayed second-order quantities.

Abstract: Let a smooth, unforced, three-dimensional Navier--Stokes flow on the flat torus approach a finite terminal time. Its kinetic-energy densities converge, along the full time variable, to a unique endpoint measure. We prove that each point atom forces a same-parent, full-tail dynamical rigidity. A preassigned level-crossing catalogue and nested local Hodge projections produce orthogonal packets. From the entire packet tail, we extract a single backward adjoint whose terminal energy concentrates at the atom. Cauchy saturation then locks this adjoint to every sufficiently late packet and yields uniform two-parameter saturation of both the constrained Oseen propagator and its adjoint, together with vanishing first-order dissipation. Consequently, every sufficiently late fixed-root descendant has infinite delayed second-order action and non-integrable positive enstrophy production. Equivalently, a delayed second-order operator budget determined solely by the Navier--Stokes parent must fail arbitrarily close to the endpoint.

</details>


### [68] [Traveling-Wave Solutions for an Einstein-Type Material-Balance Model of the Chemotactic Transport](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04177)
*Isanka Garli Hevage,Akif Ibragimov*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops a nonlinear continuum transport model for localized traveling structures in a coupled two-phase medium, derived from an Einstein-type material-balance formulation with diffusion and gradient-driven transport. It proves existence and uniqueness of coherent traveling bands, derives explicit profile representations, establishes perturbation bounds via maximum-principle arguments, and reduces the system to a third-order ODE for the background profile.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a mathematical framework for understanding localized traveling structures in two-phase coupled media, applicable to chemotactic transport and the formation of oil and gas deposits.

Method: A nonlinear continuum transport model is derived from an Einstein-type material-balance formulation coupling diffusion, nonlinear gradient-driven transport, and background depletion. Traveling-wave solutions are analyzed, with explicit one-hump profile representations for the mobile phase and monotone logistic transitions for the background. Linearized perturbation operators and maximum-principle bounds are derived, and the system is reduced to a nonlinear third-order ODE.

Result: Existence of coherent traveling bands is established in the transport-dominated regime. A unique one-hump mobile-phase profile exists for any reference time, the background transitions monotonically and boundedly between asymptotic states, uniqueness holds up to translation, and finite-time perturbation bounds are proven. An alternative ODE characterization of the coherent structure is also provided.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides rigorous mathematical guarantees for traveling band formation in coupled two-phase media, with applicability to chemotactic systems and hydrocarbon deposit formation.

Abstract: We develop a nonlinear continuum transport model describing the formation of localized traveling structures in a coupled two-phase medium. The model is derived from an Einstein-type material-balance formulation in which displacement is generated by diffusion and by the gradient of a background-dependent transport mechanism. The resulting system couples diffusion, nonlinear gradient-driven transport, and depletion of the background phase. The proposed framework is applicable to general chemotactic transport and, in particular, to problems related to the formation of oil and gas deposits. We analyze traveling-wave solutions and establish the existence of coherent traveling bands in the transport-dominated regime. The mobile phase is shown to form a unique one-hump profile for any given reference time, while the background component undergoes a positive, bounded monotone logistic-type transition between asymptotic states. An explicit representation of the traveling profile is obtained, and uniqueness is proved up to translation. We further derive the linearized perturbation operator around the traveling band and establish finite-time perturbation bounds through a maximum-principle argument. Finally, the traveling-wave system is reduced to a nonlinear third-order ordinary differential equation for the background profile, providing an alternative characterization of the coherent structure.

</details>


### [69] [Analytical solutions of 1D Maxwell's equations via infinite-order expansions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04212)
*David Wei Ge*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper presents an infinite-order estimation scheme that yields closed-form analytical solutions to Maxwell's equations in open-space settings with general initial values and source terms, expressed as function-to-function transformations mapping initial conditions and sources directly to electromagnetic fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Analytical solutions to Maxwell's equations are essential for understanding causal and instantaneous behavior of electromagnetic fields, but they are challenging to obtain in open-space settings with general initial values and source terms.

Method: The authors use an infinite-order estimation scheme that produces closed-form analytical solutions, expressed as general function-to-function transformations that directly map initial values and source terms to the fields.

Result: Several case studies demonstrate the method, producing exact solutions for different initial and source configurations.

Conclusion: The results provide both theoretical insight into electromagnetic field behavior and practical benchmarks for computational electromagnetics.

Abstract: Analytical solutions to Maxwell's equations are essential for understanding the causal and instantaneous behavior of electromagnetic fields, yet they are challenging to obtain in open-space settings with general initial values and source terms. This work uses an infinite-order estimation scheme that yields closed-form analytical solutions to Maxwell's equations. The scheme is expressed as general function-to-function transformations that directly map initial values and source terms to the fields. Several case studies demonstrate the method, producing exact solutions for different initial and source configurations. The results provide both theoretical insight and practical benchmarks for computational electromagnetics.

</details>


### [70] [Full-Density Degenerate Stagnation Points for Water Waves with General Vorticity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04280)
*Lili Du,Chunlei Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper revisits singular asymptotics near stagnation points for 2D traveling gravity water waves with vorticity, proving the nonexistence of full-density degenerate stagnation points beyond strict two-sided linear growth. It introduces modified Weiss-type and Almgren-type frequency formulas, avoiding the Bessel-type differential inequality used in prior work, and yields uniform bounds for frequency-normalized blow-ups, strong convergence in 2D, and an extended Stokes conjecture for a broader class of vorticity distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To prove the nonexistence of full-density degenerate stagnation points in traveling gravity water waves with vorticity without relying on the Bessel-type differential inequality that was essential in previous literature, thereby obtaining stronger convergence results and extending the Stokes conjecture.

Method: The authors develop a modified Weiss-type monotonicity formula and a modified Almgren-type frequency formula. These tools provide a new analytical framework that completely avoids the use of a Bessel-type differential inequality previously required for proving the nonexistence of full-density degenerate stagnation points.

Result: The paper establishes the nonexistence of full-density degenerate stagnation points beyond the strict two-sided linear growth regime. It obtains uniform bounds for frequency-normalized blow-ups in arbitrary dimension and strong convergence in dimension two. As an application, the Stokes conjecture for rotational waves is extended to a broader class of vorticity distributions.

Conclusion: The new monotonicity and frequency formulas offer a more robust and general approach to stagnation point analysis in water waves, yielding stronger analytical results and extending the applicability of the Stokes conjecture to wider classes of vorticity distributions.

Abstract: In this paper, we revisit the singular asymptotics of the free surface near stagnation points for two-dimensional traveling gravity water waves with vorticity. We prove the nonexistence of full-density degenerate stagnation points beyond the strict two-sided linear growth regime.
  Our main tools are a modified Weiss-type monotonicity formula and a modified Almgren-type frequency formula. Together, they provide a new approach that completely avoids the use of a Bessel-type differential inequality, which is an essential tool used in the previous literature to prove the nonexistence of full-density degenerate stagnation points (Ann. I. H. Poincaré-AN, 29, 861--885, 2012).
  As consequences, we obtain uniform bounds for frequency-normalized blow-ups in arbitrary dimension and strong convergence in dimension two. As an application, we extend the Stokes conjecture for rotational waves to a broader class of vorticity distributions.

</details>


### [71] [Controllable subspaces and real-part observability for Schrödinger equations on $\mathbb T^d$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04357)
*Gengsheng Wang,Ming Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies internal controllability of Schrödinger equations on tori where controls act only through real parts, characterizing the maximal null-controllable subspace and showing uncontrollable directions are precisely the purely imaginary stationary modes, via observability inequalities with sharp stationary correction terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-part-only controls arise naturally in physical settings where the imaginary part of the wave function cannot be directly actuated. This constraint turns the control problem into a real-linear one where full null controllability may fail, so it is important to understand precisely which directions are controllable and which are not.

Method: The characterization is obtained through observability inequalities with sharp stationary correction terms. For cylindrical open control regions, a real-part compactness-uniqueness argument is used. For admissible measurable product control regions in the shifted free case, the estimate is established for rough spacetime control sets using complex-valued observability inputs combined with a double-spectrum analysis arising from the coupling of forward and backward Schrödinger evolutions in the real-part observation.

Result: The paper characterizes the maximal null-controllable subspace and shows that the uncontrollable directions are exactly the purely imaginary stationary modes, providing an explicit description of controllable and uncontrollable directions for Schrödinger equations with real-part controls.

Conclusion: The paper fully characterizes controllability for Schrödinger equations with real-part controls on tori, giving explicit conditions for when null controllability holds, and establishes sharp observability estimates including for rough spacetime control sets in the shifted free case.

Abstract: We study internal controllability of Schrödinger equations on tori with controls acting only through their real parts. This real-part constraint leads to a real-linear control problem for which full null controllability may fail. We characterize the maximal null-controllable subspace and show that the uncontrollable directions are precisely given by the purely imaginary stationary modes. These results provide an explicit description of controllable and uncontrollable directions for Schrödinger equations with real-part controls.
  The characterization is obtained through observability inequalities with sharp stationary correction terms. For cylindrical open control regions, we prove such an estimate by a real-part compactness--uniqueness argument. For admissible measurable product control regions in the shifted free case, we establish the corresponding estimate for rough spacetime control sets. The proof combines complex-valued observability inputs with a double-spectrum analysis arising from the coupling of the forward and backward Schrödinger evolutions in the real-part observation.

</details>


### [72] [Morse index, Leray-Schauder degree and local uniqueness for multi-peak concentrating solutions of a fractional Schrödinger equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04380)
*Yinbin Deng,Baiping Feng,Qing Guo,Huafei Xie*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes positive k-peak solutions of the semiclassical fractional Schrödinger equation concentrating at nondegenerate critical points of the potential V. The authors determine the complete low spectrum of the linearized operator, show the Morse index equals k plus the total number of negative Hessian eigenvalues, prove nondegeneracy, and establish uniqueness of positive solutions in the prescribed concentrating class via Leray-Schauder degree theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the spectral properties, Morse index, and uniqueness of multi-peak solutions for the semiclassical fractional Schrödinger equation, which are fundamental for stability analysis and constructing more complex solutions (e.g., via gluing or bifurcation arguments).

Method: The paper combines spectral analysis of the linearized operator using Hessian matrices of V at the concentration points, applies a unique modulation parametrization, and uses a Leray-Schauder degree computation to prove uniqueness and nondegeneracy for the entire energy-quantized class.

Result: The first k eigenvalues are uniformly negative; the next kN eigenvalues are of order ε² governed by the Hessians D²V(ξⱼ⁰); the remaining spectrum is uniformly separated from zero. The Morse index equals k plus the total number of negative Hessian eigenvalues, and exactly one positive solution exists in the prescribed concentrating class for small ε.

Conclusion: Every energy-quantized positive k-peak solution is nondegenerate with a fully determined low spectrum and Morse index, and the prescribed concentrating class contains a unique positive solution for sufficiently small ε.

Abstract: We study positive $k$-peak solutions of the semiclassical fractional Schrödinger equation $\varepsilon^{2s}(-Δ)^s u+V(x)u=u^p$ in $\mathbb{R}^N$, concentrating at different nondegenerate critical points $ξ_1^0,\ldots,ξ_k^0$ of $V$. For every such family satisfying the natural energy quantization, we determine the complete low spectrum of the linearized operator. The first $k$ eigenvalues remain uniformly negative, the next $kN$ eigenvalues are of order $\varepsilon^2$ and are governed by the Hessians $D^2V(ξ_j^0)$, while the remaining spectrum is uniformly separated from zero. Consequently, the Morse index equals $k$ plus the total number of negative eigenvalues of these Hessians, and every such solution is nondegenerate. Combining a unique modulation parametrization with a Leray--Schauder degree computation, we further prove that, for all sufficiently small $\varepsilon$, the prescribed concentrating class contains exactly one positive solution. The result applies to the whole energy-quantized class, not only to a particular solution.

</details>


### [73] [Long-Time Dynamics and Modified Scattering for the Coulomb Vlasov--Hartree System](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04402)
*Wenrui Huang,Mengyi Xie*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global well-posedness and precise long-time dynamics for the three-dimensional Vlasov-Hartree system with Coulomb interaction, a classical-quantum model for Bose-Fermi mixtures, showing logarithmic corrections to free dynamics for both the Vlasov distribution and Hartree wave.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the long-time behavior of the Vlasov-Hartree system modeling Bose-Fermi mixtures, where the coupling between classical and quantum components remains visible at leading order, requiring a rigorous mathematical analysis of global well-posedness and asymptotic dynamics.

Method: Combines techniques from kinetic theory with vector-field methods for dispersive equations, proving global well-posedness for sufficiently small, regular, localized initial data, and extends the analysis to shorter-range inverse-power interactions.

Result: Proves global well-posedness and determines precise long-time dynamics: the Vlasov distribution scatters along logarithmically corrected free characteristics determined by the asymptotic bosonic profile, while the Hartree wave acquires a logarithmic phase correction from the asymptotic fermionic density.

Conclusion: The coupling in the Vlasov-Hartree system remains visible at leading order through logarithmic corrections in the asymptotic dynamics of both components, and the proof framework extends to shorter-range inverse-power interactions.

Abstract: We study the three-dimensional Vlasov--Hartree system with Coulomb interaction, a classical--quantum model for Bose--Fermi mixtures. For sufficiently small, regular, and localized initial data, we prove global well-posedness and determine the precise long-time dynamics. The coupling remains visible at leading order: the Vlasov distribution scatters along logarithmically corrected free characteristics determined by the asymptotic bosonic profile, while the Hartree wave acquires a logarithmic phase correction generated by the asymptotic fermionic density. Our proof combines techniques from kinetic theory with vector-field methods for dispersive equations and also extends to shorter-range inverse-power interactions.

</details>


### [74] [Tightness of and counterexamples to several quantum estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04411)
*Haonan Zhang,Joseph Slote,Alexander Volberg*

<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish optimal (tight) bounds for fundamental quantum inequalities, showing that previously known bounds cannot be improved, thereby completing the theoretical understanding of these inequalities.

Method: The paper employs mathematical proofs to demonstrate tightness for multiple quantum inequalities: comparing operator norm versus product norm of d-local Hamiltonians, the Bohnenblust-Hille inequality for d-local Hamiltonians, and the quantum Fourier entropy-influence conjecture. It also analyzes the quantum Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture in the special case of anti-commuting Pauli strings.

Result: The paper establishes tightness results for the mentioned quantum inequalities, proving that the bounds in these inequalities are optimal, and provides insights into the Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture specifically for anti-commuting Pauli strings.

Conclusion: The authors prove that several key quantum inequalities are tight, resolving the optimality of these bounds and shedding light on the quantum Aaronson-Ambainis conjecture in a restricted setting.

Abstract: We prove here several tightness results for such quantum inequalities as the comparison of operator norm and product norm of $d$-local hamiltonians, Bohnenblust--Hille inequality for $d$-local hamiltonians and for quantum Fourier entropy-influence conjecture, we also discuss the quantum Aaronson--Ambainis conjecture in a special case of anti-commuting Pauli strings.

</details>


### [75] [Optimal Rigidity and Classification Results for the $k$-Hessian Equation of Lane--Emden Type](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04422)
*Wei Dai,Jingze Fu,Changfeng Gui,Guolin Qin*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes optimal Liouville theorems and classification results for the k-Hessian Lane-Emden equation, completely closing the gap in the nonexistence range and identifying the critical Hessian-Sobolev exponent p_* as the sharp Liouville threshold. It also classifies solutions at the critical case as Aubin-Talenti type bubbles, providing fully nonlinear counterparts of classical Liouville and classification theorems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Previous work by Phuc-Verlbitsky and Ou established nonexistence for different ranges of p, leaving a gap (p_- to p_*). The paper aims to close this gap and establish the sharp Liouville threshold, along with classification results at the critical exponent.

Method: The authors prove optimal Liouville theorems for both C^2 solutions and locally bounded Hessian-measure weak solutions. They complete the gap by proving nonexistence for p_- < p < p_*, and classify critical case solutions (p = p_*) as Aubin-Talenti type bubbles under various assumptions depending on dimension n.

Result: They close the gap completely, proving optimal Liouville theorems for p_- < p < p_* showing any nonnegative solution must be zero. The critical exponent p_* is identified as the sharp threshold since radial positive solutions exist for p ≥ p_*. At p = p_*, they classify solutions as Aubin-Talenti bubbles without assumptions for 2k < n ≤ 4k, under boundedness for n = 4k+1, 4k+2, and under growth/asymptotic conditions for n > 4k and n = 2k.

Conclusion: The paper provides fully nonlinear counterparts of classical Liouville and classification theorems by Gidas-Spruck, Gidas-Ni-Nirenberg, and Caffarelli-Gidas-Spruck for the k-Hessian Lane-Emden equation, establishing the sharp critical exponent p_* as the Liouville threshold.

Abstract: In this paper, we establish optimal Liouville theorems and classification results for the \(k\)-Hessian Lane--Emden equation \[
  σ_k(-D^2u)=u^p\quad\text{in }\R^n,\qquad
  -D^2u\in\overline{Γ_k},\qquad u\geq 0, \] where \(2\leq k<\frac{n}{2}\) and $p>0$. Let $p_- = \frac{nk}{n-2k}$ and the critical Hessian--Sobolev exponent $p_* = \frac{(n+2)k}{n-2k}$. Phuc and Verbitsky proved nonexistence of positive solutions for \(k<p\leq p_-\), while Ou subsequently covered the cases \(p\in(0,k]\). We completely closed this gap and proved the optimal Liouville theorem for any \(p_-<p<p_*\): any nonnegative \(C^2\) entire solution must be identically zero. We also prove the optimal Liouville theorem for nonnegative locally bounded Hessian-measure weak solutions. This identifies the critical exponent \(p_*\) as the sharp Liouville threshold, since radial positive solutions exist for \(p\geq p_*\).
  For the critical case \(p=p_*\), we proved that nontrivial nonnegative \(C^2\) entire solution must be the Aubin-Talenti type bubble without any assumptions for \(2k<n\leq 4k\), under boundedness assumption for $n=4k+1$ and $4k+2$, and under some appropriate integral growth conditions or pointwise asymptotic behavior assumptions for any \(n>4k\) and the limiting case $n=2k$. In particular, we provide a fully nonlinear counterparts of the classical Liouville and classification theorems of Gidas--Spruck, Gidas--Ni--Nirenberg, and Caffarelli--Gidas--Spruck.

</details>


### [76] [Large-time behavior and grow-up rates of inhomogeneous semilinear heat equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04440)
*Kenta Kumagai,Yusuke Oka*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a semilinear heat equation in the unit ball with exponential nonlinearity and an inhomogeneous term f.
