When f exceeds a threshold, the bifurcation structure changes to a novel type not present when f=0, causing the grow-up phenomenon to disappear. The authors characterize this transition sharply for N≥11, discovering that at the threshold case, N=11 uniquely exhibits a log-log correction term in the grow-up rate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: For the case f=0, it is known that the bifurcation structure of the stationary problem changes qualitatively at the critical dimension N=10, which affects large-time behavior of solutions—specifically, grow-up phenomena occur for N≥10. The authors seek to understand how introducing an inhomogeneous term f modifies this picture, including whether grow-up behavior persists and how it transitions.

Method: The paper analyzes the bifurcation structure of the stationary problem when the inhomogeneous term f exceeds a threshold, and then links this structural change to the large-time behavior of the heat equation. They derive sharp grow-up rates for dimensions N≥11, with special attention to the threshold case, where a dimension-specific analysis is required for N=11.

Result: Once f surpasses a threshold, the bifurcation structure changes to a type not seen in the f=0 case, and the grow-up phenomenon disappears. Sharp grow-up rates are determined for N≥11, and a new log-log correction term in the grow-up rate is identified exclusively for N=11 at the threshold.

Conclusion: The inhomogeneous term f can fundamentally alter the dynamics of the semilinear heat equation beyond what is possible in the homogeneous case, eliminating grow-up behavior and revealing a novel dimension-specific correction (log-log term) unique to N=11 at the threshold.

Abstract: We consider the semilinear heat equation in the unit ball with the exponential nonlinearity and an inhomogeneous term $f$. When $f=0$, it is known that the bifurcation structure of the stationary problem undergoes a qualitative change at the critical dimension $N=10$. This change affects the large-time behavior of solutions to the heat equation, and in particular, the grow-up phenomenon occurs for $N\ge 10$.
  In this paper, we show that once $f$ exceeds a threshold, the bifurcation structure changes to a type that does not appear in the case $f=0$. The change in the bifurcation structure leads to the disappearance of the grow-up phenomenon beyond the threshold. Moreover, we provide a quantitative characterization of this transition by determining the sharp grow-up rates for $N\ge 11$. In particular, we identify a new dimension-specific phenomenon in the threshold case: a log-log type correction term emerges in the grow-up rate only for $N=11$.

</details>


### [77] [Liouville-type theorems for coupled-drift Monge-Ampère equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04478)
*Ling Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies entire solutions and periodic correctors for the coupled-drift Monge-Ampère equation. It provides a sharp classification of whole-space solvability regimes (quadratic solutions only when a=b=0; no solutions when a≠0 and a·b≤0; non-quadratic solutions exist in other cases), proves a new scalar maximum-principle argument ruling out the null case a≠0, a·b=0, and establishes existence/uniqueness for the drifted cell problem with periodic V, including a rigidity result linking asymptotically quadratic solutions to correctors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to understand the coupled-drift Monge-Ampère equation, which generalizes the classical Monge-Ampère equation by incorporating a gradient drift term (-a·Du) and a linear potential (b·x). Understanding when whole-space solutions exist and their structure is fundamental for applications in optimal transport, geometric optics, and homogenization, where periodic correctors play a key role.

Method: The main technical contribution is a scalar maximum-principle argument valid in every dimension n≥2, which is used to prove that the null case (a≠0, a·b=0) admits no entire smooth strictly convex solution. For the periodic cell problem, they establish existence and uniqueness of solutions via variational/elliptic techniques. For the rigidity results, they use asymptotic analysis showing that asymptotically quadratic solutions must satisfy b=Aa, and bounded-remainder solutions coincide with the quadratic-periodic corrector up to an additive constant.

Result: For V≡0, the classification is: (i) all entire smooth strictly convex solutions are quadratic when a=b=0; (ii) no such solution exists when a≠0 and a·b≤0; (iii) non-quadratic entire solutions exist when a=0 and b≠0, or when a·b>0. For periodic V, existence and uniqueness of the drifted cell problem is established, and rigidity results show asymptotically quadratic solutions must satisfy b=Aa, with bounded-remainder solutions being the periodic corrector up to a constant.

Conclusion: The paper completes the sharp classification of solvability regimes for the coupled-drift Monge-Ampère equation and provides a rigorous theory for periodic correctors. The new maximum-principle argument resolves the previously open null case and is a versatile tool applicable in all dimensions. The results connect to homogenization theory for Monge-Ampère equations with drifts.

Abstract: We study entire solutions and periodic correctors for the coupled-drift Monge-Ampère equation \[
  \det D^2u
  =
  \exp\{-a\cdot Du+b\cdot x+V(x)-c_0\},
  \quad
  D^2u>0. \] For \(V\equiv0\), we obtain a sharp classification of the whole-space solvability regimes: all entire smooth strictly convex solutions are quadratic when \(a=b=0\); no such solution exists when \(a\neq0\) and \(a\cdot b\le0\); and non-quadratic entire solutions exist when \(a=0\) and \(b\neq0\), or when \(a\cdot b>0\). The main new ingredient is a scalar maximum-principle argument valid in every dimension \(n\ge2\), which proves that the null case \(a\neq0\), \(a\cdot b=0\) admits no entire smooth strictly convex solution.
  For periodic \(V\), we establish the existence and uniqueness for the drifted cell problem \[
  \det(A+D^2ψ)
  =
  \exp\{-a\cdot Dψ+V-c_A\},
  \quad
  A+D^2ψ>0
  \quad\text{on }\mathbb T^n. \] We also prove that any asymptotically quadratic entire solution must satisfy \(b=Aa\). If its remainder is bounded, then the solution is the corresponding quadratic-periodic corrector up to an additive constant.

</details>


### [78] [Stochastic Keller Segel System with Porous Medium Diffusion and Nonlinear Chemotactic Sensitivity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04614)
*Yiming Jiang,Haohang Li,Yawei Wei*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes global existence of martingale solutions, uniform a priori estimates, and non-negativity preservation for a stochastic Keller-Segel system with porous medium diffusion and nonlinear chemotactic sensitivity on a bounded 1D domain, under the condition m ≥ 2a + 1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand cell aggregation in complex environments where both density-dependent (porous medium) diffusion and nonlinear chemotactic sensing are present, while accounting for random environmental fluctuations through multiplicative noise—a combination of features that introduces significant mathematical difficulties for existing theory.

Method: The proof combines a decoupled auxiliary system, energy estimates, and a stochastic Schauder-Tychonoff fixed point argument to overcome difficulties from degenerate diffusion, nonlinear drift, and stochastic perturbations.

Result: For a ≥ 1 and m ≥ 2a + 1, global existence of martingale solutions, uniform a priori estimates, and preservation of non-negativity are established. The condition m ≥ 2a + 1 reveals a necessary balance between chemotactic aggregation strength and porous-medium diffusion strength.

Conclusion: Stronger chemotactic sensing requires correspondingly stronger porous-medium diffusion to prevent excessive aggregation, and the established solution theory provides a rigorous foundation for this stochastic Keller-Segel model in one dimension.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate a stochastic Keller--Segel system with porous medium diffusion and nonlinear chemotactic sensitivity on a bounded one-dimensional domain. The model describes cell aggregation in complex environments, where the dispersal of cells is governed by density-dependent diffusion $Δu^{[m]}$, reflecting the combined effects of porous media and population crowding, and the perception of chemoattractants follows Stevens' power law, leading to the nonlinear chemotactic sensitivity $\nabla\cdot(u\nabla v^{[a]})$. In addition, random environmental fluctuations are incorporated through multiplicative noise $u\,dW(t)$, which represents stochastic perturbations in population dynamics. For $a\geq1$ and $m\geq2a+1$, we establish the global existence of martingale solutions, uniform a priori estimates, and preservation of non-negativity. The condition $m\geq2a+1$ reveals a balance between nonlinear chemotactic aggregation and porous-medium diffusion: stronger sensing response requires stronger diffusion to prevent excessive aggregation. The proof combines a decoupled auxiliary system, energy estimates, and a stochastic Schauder--Tychonoff fixed point argument to overcome the difficulties caused by degenerate diffusion, nonlinear drift, and stochastic perturbations.

</details>


### [79] [Sharp regularity for the periodic Camassa--Holm equation in critical Triebel--Lizorkin spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04619)
*Wenhai Shan,Xiao-Song Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes a sharp well-posedness and norm inflation theory for the Camassa-Holm equation in critical Triebel-Lizorkin spaces on the torus, proving Hadamard well-posedness at p=1 and norm inflation for 1<p<∞.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the sharp well-posedness boundary for the Camassa-Holm equation in critical Triebel-Lizorkin spaces on the torus, complementing existing results in Besov spaces and higher-regularity settings.

Method: Uses a Lipschitz stability theorem for the periodic Green operator under degree-one Lagrangian flows for positive results, and a nested smooth atomic construction on the torus (adapted from the real line) for the negative norm inflation results.

Result: Local Hadamard well-posedness is proven at the endpoint p=1 for 1≤q<∞, while norm inflation is proven for 1<p<∞ and 1≤q≤∞, yielding a sharp dichotomy at the critical regularity.

Conclusion: The Camassa-Holm equation exhibits a sharp well-posedness/norm inflation dichotomy in critical Triebel-Lizorkin spaces on the torus, with the p=1 endpoint being the only well-posed regime.

Abstract: We establish a sharp well-posedness and norm inflation theory for the Camassa--Holm equation in critical Triebel--Lizorkin $F^{1+1/p}_{p,q}(\mathbb{T})$. At the endpoint $p=1$, we prove local Hadamard well-posedness for $1\le q<\infty$. In contrast, we prove norm inflation for $1<p<\infty$ and $1\le q\le\infty$. We also complement the local well-posedness in the critical Besov spaces and higher-regularity Triebel--Lizorkin spaces. The positive results rely on a Lipschitz stability theorem for the periodic Green operator under degree-one Lagrangian flows. The negative result is based on a nested smooth atomic construction on the torus, adapted from its real-line counterpart.

</details>


### [80] [Convergence Analysis of a Finite-Volume Scheme for a Microglia--Amyloid Chemotaxis Model with Measure-Valued Vascular Boundary Sources](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04785)
*Elmahdi Erraji*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a parabolic-parabolic chemotaxis system modeling microglial recruitment toward amyloid-β signals in Alzheimer's disease, where the signal has a measure-valued Neumann boundary influx and microglia respond to a nonlocal spatial average of the signal. The authors introduce a weak-solution framework and construct a fully implicit upwind finite-volume scheme that discretizes the boundary source via exact mass on boundary face-time cells, establishing existence, positivity, uniform estimates, and convergence to a nonnegative weak solution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a rigorous mathematical and numerical framework for a chemotaxis system arising in Alzheimer's disease modeling, where the boundary signal source is a low-regularity Radon measure and the sensing mechanism is nonlocal.

Method: The authors introduce a weak-solution concept adapted to the low regularity from the boundary measure, discretize using a fully implicit upwind finite-volume scheme with exact mass discretization of the boundary source on each boundary face-time cell, and analyze the discrete solutions for mass, energy, discrete-gradient, and compactness properties.

Result: Existence and positivity of discrete solutions are established, along with uniform mass, energy, discrete-gradient, and compactness estimates. Subsequential convergence of discrete solutions toward a nonnegative weak solution of the continuous problem is proven.

Conclusion: The proposed finite-volume scheme converges to a nonnegative weak solution of the parabolic-parabolic chemotaxis system with measure-valued boundary influx, providing a robust numerical tool for simulating microglial recruitment in Alzheimer's disease.

Abstract: We study a parabolic-parabolic chemotaxis system motivated by microglial recruitment toward an amyloid-$β$-associated signal in Alzheimer's disease. The signal is subject to a nonnegative Radon measure-valued Neumann influx on a vascular portion of the boundary, while microglial cells respond to a nonlocal spatial average of the signal. For a fixed sensing length $σ>0$, the chemotactic velocity is $b_σ[v]=\nabla K_σ[v]$. For every fixed $σ>0$, the nonlocal operator maps finite signal mass into a bounded spatially Lipschitz velocity field. We introduce a weak-solution concept adapted to the low regularity induced by the boundary measure and construct a fully implicit upwind finite-volume approximation in which the boundary source is discretized through its exact mass on each boundary face-time cell. We establish existence and positivity of discrete solutions, together with uniform mass, energy, discrete-gradient, and compactness estimates. Finally, we prove subsequential convergence of the discrete solutions toward a nonnegative weak solution of the continuous problem.

</details>


### [81] [The IBVP for the Navier-Stokes equations in the half-space in a class of weighted Lebesgue spaces](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04621)
*Angelica Pia Di Feola,Vittorio Pane*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the initial-boundary value problem for the Navier-Stokes equations in a half-space, using weighted Lebesgue spaces with weights defined as products of powers of distances from finitely many fixed points. The authors prove local existence and uniqueness of smooth solutions, derive L^q-estimates for q>n, and establish the spatial asymptotic behavior of the velocity field.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to extend previous results on the Stokes system (by Di Feola and Pane) and the Navier-Stokes Cauchy problem (by Maremonti and Pane) to the initial-boundary value problem for Navier-Stokes equations in a half-space with weighted Lebesgue space initial data.

Method: The paper employs a weighted Lebesgue space framework where the weight function is a product of powers of distances from finitely many fixed points. This generalizes the analysis previously developed for the Stokes system to the full Navier-Stokes initial-boundary value problem in the half-space.

Result: The authors prove the local existence and uniqueness of smooth solutions, derive L^q-estimates with q>n, and establish the spatial asymptotic behavior of the velocity field for the Navier-Stokes initial-boundary value problem in the half-space.

Conclusion: The paper completes the analysis of the Stokes system study by extending it to the Navier-Stokes equations, and generalizes the Cauchy problem results to the initial-boundary value problem, establishing well-posedness and asymptotic properties of solutions in weighted Lebesgue spaces.

Abstract: We study the initial-boundary value problem for the Navier-Stokes equations in the half-space with initial data belonging to a suitable weighted Lebesgue space. More precisely, we consider a weighted Lebesgue space associated with a weight function defined as a product of powers of the distances from finitely many fixed points. This framework was introduced in a previous work by A. P. Di Feola and V. Pane (J. Math. Anal. Appl. 558 (2026), 130390) for the study of the initial-boundary value problem for the Stokes system. The present paper completes that analysis and, at the same time, generalizes to the initial-boundary value problem the results obtained by Maremonti and Pane (J. Math. Fluid Mech. 27 (2025), Art. 2) for the Navier-Stokes Cauchy problem. We prove the existence (local) and uniqueness of a smooth solution and derive $L^q$-estimates, with $q>n$, together with the spatial asymptotic behavior of the velocity field.

</details>


### [82] [Stochastic Two-Species Chemotaxis Competition: Global Well Posedness and Continuous Dependence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04636)
*Yiming Jiang,Haohang Li,Yawei Wei*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global well-posedness for a stochastic two-species competition Keller-Segel model, establishing the existence of a unique global adapted nonnegative mild solution and continuous dependence in probability on initial data when self-damping exponents exceed two.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how chemotactic movement, interspecific competition, and environmental fluctuations interact simultaneously when two species respond to the same chemical signal, and to establish rigorous mathematical foundations for this stochastic system.

Method: The approach combines parabolic estimates for the signal equation, superquadratic self-damping, and stochastic convolution estimates to control L^p estimates that contain terms with no fixed sign due to two coupled chemotactic fluxes and stochastic terms.

Result: The system has a unique global adapted nonnegative mild solution when self-damping exponents exceed two, with continuous dependence in probability on admissible initial data over finite time intervals.

Conclusion: Global well-posedness is established under the stated assumptions, and small changes in initial populations lead to small changes in the solution in probability.

Abstract: In this paper, we investigate a stochastic two species competition Keller--Segel model. Chemotactic movement, interspecific competition, and environmental fluctuations may act simultaneously when two species respond to the same chemical signal. We consider a stochastic two-species chemotaxis--competition system on a bounded smooth domain $\mathcal O\subset\mathbb R^n$, $n\in\{1,2\}$. Both species contribute to the production of the signal and move along its gradient; their population equations also include nonlinear competition and multiplicative noise. If the self-damping exponents exceed two, we prove that the system has a unique global adapted nonnegative mild solution. We also establish continuous dependence in probability on admissible initial data over finite time intervals. The results prove the global well-posedness under the stated assumptions and we still show that small changes in the initial populations, in probability, to small changes in the solution. Due to the two coupled chemotactic fluxes and the stochastic terms, the $L^p$ estimates contain terms with no fixed sign. They are controlled by parabolic estimates for the signal equation, superquadratic self-damping, and stochastic convolution estimates.

</details>


### [83] [Almost sure local well-posedness for the nonlinear Schrödinger equations on $\Bbb T^d$ with non-algebraic nonlinearity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04643)
*Yongming Luo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper establishes almost sure local well-posedness for the nonlinear Schrödinger equation (NLS) on the torus with random initial data and general non-algebraic power-type nonlinearities, covering the full mass-supercritical range for all spatial dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend almost sure local well-posedness results for NLS with random initial data beyond algebraic nonlinearities and beyond low dimensions, addressing the gap in the literature which previously only covered low-dimensional algebraic cases.

Method: The authors develop a frequency-gaining probabilistic refinement of the Galilean bilinear estimates from Kwak and Kwon. The random gauge decomposition introduces three new term types (mean-free coefficient, opposite-phase interaction, scalar remainder), which are controlled via new resonance counting and large deviation arguments. The local theory is closed through a phase-adapted two-component contraction.

Result: Almost sure local well-posedness is established in every spatial dimension for the full mass-supercritical range allowed by 0 < s_c < 1+a. In the energy-critical case, the result extends prior low-dimensional algebraic theories (Nahmod–Staffilani, Yue) to all dimensions d ≥ 3, including higher-dimensional non-algebraic models.

Conclusion: The paper significantly generalizes the scope of random-data NLS local well-posedness, covering general nonlinearities and all dimensions through new probabilistic refinements of bilinear estimates.

Abstract: We study the Cauchy problem for the nonlinear Schrödinger equation on $\mathbb T^d$ with random initial data and a general non-algebraic power-type nonlinearity. We establish almost sure local well-posedness in every spatial dimension and for the whole mass-supercritical range allowed by the natural condition $0<s_{\mathrm c}<1+a$. The main new ingredient is a frequency-gaining probabilistic refinement of the Galilean bilinear estimates recently developed by Kwak and Kwon \cite{KwakKwon}. In the random setting, the gauge decomposition gives rise to three new types of terms: a mean-free coefficient, an opposite-phase interaction, and a scalar remainder. We control them by new resonance counting and large deviation arguments, and close the local theory through a phase-adapted two-component contraction. In the energy-critical case, our result extends the low-dimensional algebraic theories of Nahmod--Staffilani \cite{NahmodStaffilani15} and Yue \cite{Yue21} to every dimension $d\geq3$, including the higher-dimensional non-algebraic models.

</details>


### [84] [Global existence of solutions for nonlinear damped wave equations in an exterior domain with nonlinearities of derivative type](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04671)
*Tuan Anh Dao,Dinh Van Duong,Masahiro Ikeda*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global existence of small data solutions for the semi-linear damped wave equation with derivative-type power nonlinearity in 2D exterior domains, and establishes sharp large-time estimates for the time derivative of these solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The semi-linear damped wave equation with derivative-type power nonlinearity in 2D exterior domains has not been studied before. The authors aim to fill this gap by establishing global (in time) existence of small data solutions, which is a previously open problem in this setting.

Method: The proof relies on constructing a judicious time-weighted function space and demonstrating nonlinear estimates that are compatible with fractional powers of the Dirichlet Laplacian, which are linked to the semigroup decay structure of the equation.

Result: Global (in time) existence of small data solutions is established for the semi-linear damped wave equation with derivative-type nonlinearity in 2D exterior domains. Furthermore, the large time behavior of the time derivative of the obtained solutions and its sharp estimate are also derived.

Conclusion: The paper successfully provides global existence results for a previously unstudied problem in 2D exterior domains, complete with sharp large-time estimates for the time derivative of solutions, thereby extending the theory to this new setting.

Abstract: In this paper, we are interested in considering the semi-linear damped wave equation with the power nonlinearity of derivative type in $2$D exterior domains to indicate the global (in time) existence of small data solutions, which has never appeared in previous studies. Our proof is based on constructing a judicious time-weighted function space and demonstrating nonlinear estimates compatible with fractional powers of the Dirichlet Laplacian linked to the semigroup decay structure. Moreover, the large time behavior of the time derivative of the obtained global solutions and its sharp estimate are also discussed in this paper.

</details>


### [85] [Inverse Geometric Diffraction by a Cone](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04675)
*Gang Bao,Xi Chen,Shuai Lu,Kuangmiao Xiong*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops a rigorous inverse scattering theory for recovering a strictly convex conical obstacle in R^3 from diffraction data (lens data or arrival times), using the Geometrical Theory of Diffraction framework to connect diffraction coefficients to the cone's geometry, and proving uniqueness of reconstruction via spherical wavefront identification and broken geodesics on S^2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to establish, for the first time, a rigorous inverse theory corresponding to the Geometrical Theory of Diffraction (GTD). While GTD provides a well-developed forward model for diffracted waves, no rigorous inverse theory existed to recover obstacle geometry from diffraction data.

Method: The approach reduces the 3D inverse diffraction problem by a cone to identifying a reflected wavefront on S^2, then recovering the obstacle via reflected rays on the sphere. The wavefront identification is achieved by constructing a Hadamard parametrix for half waves near the wavefront, while obstacle recovery relies on topological properties of broken geodesics on S^2. Lens data or arrival times determine the cone tip; diffraction coefficients reconstruct the cone shape.

Result: The framework exploits analytic and geometric structures of diffracted wave fields in GTD and establishes the first rigorous inverse theory for GTD, showing that diffraction coefficient data and arrival information uniquely determine the conical obstacle.

Conclusion: Inverse diffraction by a cone in R^3 is fully solvable in principle: tip location comes from timing/lens data, while cone shape is recoverable from diffraction coefficients, with the entire procedure rigorously justified via spherical wavefront parametrix construction and broken geodesic analysis.

Abstract: Consider the inverse problem of recovering a strictly convex conical obstacle in $\mathbb{R}^3$ from the diffraction coefficients along with arrival directions (lens data) or arrival times of diffracted waves. The incident wave is a spherical pulse emanating from a point, and the measurements of diffracted waves are taken at an arbitrarily sized receiver placed within the reflection shadow. Specifically, the lens data or arrival times determine the location of the tip, whereas the diffraction coefficients reconstruct the shape of the cone. Since diffraction coefficients are described by half waves over the complement of the cone base in $\mathbb{S}^2$, we reduce inverse diffraction by a cone in $\mathbb{R}^3$ to identifying the reflected wavefront in $\mathbb{S}^2$ and recovering the obstacle using reflected rays on the sphere. The former is accomplished by constructing the Hadamard parametrix for half waves near the wavefront, whereas the latter relies on the topological properties of broken geodesics on $\mathbb{S}^2$. The framework developed in this paper exploits the analytic and geometric structures of diffracted wave fields characterized in the Geometrical Theory of Diffraction, and establishes, for the first time, a rigorous inverse theory corresponding to GTD.

</details>


### [86] [On the absence of point defects in biaxial Landau--de Gennes models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04908)
*Haotong Fu,Huaijie Wang,Wei Wang,Zhifei Zhang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies local minimizers of a sextic-potential Landau-de Gennes energy for nematic liquid crystals in the small-elastic-constant limit, proving that the limiting harmonic map Q0 into a biaxial vacuum manifold has no interior point singularities, using a geometric identification with a rescaled Berger metric and a target-specific instability construction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand whether interior point defects can arise in the biaxial setting of nematic liquid crystals in the small-elastic-constant limit, and to settle this question rigorously by establishing regularity of limiting harmonic maps into the biaxial vacuum manifold.

Method: The proof identifies the lifted Frobenius metric on the universal cover S^3 with a rescaled Berger metric. For a hypothetical tangent cone at a point singularity with a nonconstant harmonic two-sphere link, the authors construct a smooth variation field adapted to the Hopf direction, producing a quantitative instability estimate that contradicts the shifted stability inequality from local minimality—replacing round-sphere test fields with target-specific constructions.

Result: The authors prove that the limiting map Q0 has no interior point singularities, establishing regularity in the biaxial setting via a contradiction argument based on instability induced by a Hopf-direction variation field.

Conclusion: The result is sharp since point defects are known to exist in the uniaxial theory, distinguishing the biaxial case and ruling out interior singularities where such defects might otherwise occur.

Abstract: We study local minimizers of a sextic-potential Landau--de Gennes energy for nematic liquid crystals in the small-elastic-constant limit. Under the uniform energy and $L^\infty$ bounds, these minimizers converge to a locally energy-minimizing harmonic map $\mathbf{Q}_0$ into a biaxial vacuum manifold. The main result of this paper is that such a limiting map $\mathbf{Q}_0$ has no interior point singularities. The proof relies on a geometric identification of the lifted Frobenius metric on the universal cover $\mathbb{S}^3$ with a rescaled Berger metric. For a hypothetical tangent cone at a point singularity, its link is a nonconstant harmonic two-sphere into the Berger sphere. We construct a smooth variation field adapted to the Hopf direction and show that it induces a quantitative instability estimate, in contradiction with the shifted stability inequality inherited from local minimality. This replaces the usual round-sphere test fields by a target-specific construction and rules out interior point defects in the biaxial setting. The result is sharp in view of the well-known existence of point defects in the uniaxial theory.

</details>


### [87] [Modulational spectrum of infinite-depth hydroelastic Stokes waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04938)
*Ting-Yang Hsiao,Zirui Li,Ye Zhang,Chengbin Zhu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper determines the complete local Bloch spectrum bifurcating from the origin for small-amplitude periodic hydroelastic Stokes waves in infinite depth, accounting for gravity, surface tension, and elastic bending. The authors construct a real-analytic Stokes-wave branch and analyze four eigenvalues emerging from a defective zero eigenvalue, decoupling them into a Benjamin-Feir pair and a long-wave pair using analytic spectral perturbation theory and Hamiltonian-reversible reductions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To fully characterize the modulational stability and instability of small-amplitude periodic hydroelastic Stokes waves in infinite depth under combined physical effects (gravity, surface tension, elastic bending), which previously remained incomplete, particularly regarding the local Bloch spectrum and the singular nature of the infinite-depth problem.

Method: The authors use analytic spectral perturbation theory and Hamiltonian-reversible reductions to decouple the four eigenvalues emerging from the defective zero eigenvalue of the linearized hydroelastic Euler system into a Benjamin-Feir pair and a long-wave pair. They derive an explicit discriminant for the Benjamin-Feir pair and construct real-analytic Stokes-wave branches away from Wilton-type resonances.

Result: The long-wave pair remains purely imaginary with singular O(sqrt(|μ|)) scale, while the Benjamin-Feir pair's stability is governed by an explicit discriminant. They derive the exact non-resonant phase diagram, identify a bounded stability island from elastic bending, and find a local figure-eight curve for Benjamin-Feir branches in the unstable region. In the zero-bending limit, known gravity and gravity-capillary results are recovered, while the change from O(|μ|) (finite depth) to O(sqrt(|μ|)) (infinite depth) long-wave scale reveals the singularity of the infinite-depth problem.

Conclusion: The paper provides a sharp criterion for modulational stability/instability of hydroelastic Stokes waves in infinite depth, revealing a stability island due to elastic bending and highlighting the singular nature of infinite depth through the anomalous scaling of the long-wave pair.

Abstract: We determine the complete local Bloch spectrum bifurcating from the origin for small-amplitude periodic hydroelastic Stokes waves in infinite depth, under the combined effects of gravity, surface tension, and elastic bending. Away from the Wilton-type resonance set, we construct a real-analytic Stokes-wave branch and analyze the four eigenvalues emerging from the defective zero eigenvalue of the linearized hydroelastic Euler system. Using analytic spectral perturbation theory and Hamiltonian-reversible reductions, we decouple them into a Benjamin--Feir pair and a long-wave pair. The long-wave pair remains purely imaginary and has the singular scale $\cO(\sqrt{|μ|})$, whereas the Benjamin--Feir pair is governed by an explicit discriminant whose leading sign yields a sharp criterion for modulational stability and instability. We derive the exact non-resonant phase diagram in the surface-tension-bending parameter plane and identify a bounded stability island generated by elastic bending. In the unstable region, and away from a drift degeneracy, the Benjamin--Feir branches form a local figure-eight curve. In the zero-bending limit, the reduced coefficients recover the known deep-water gravity and gravity-capillary results, while the change from the finite-depth $\cO(|μ|)$ long-wave scale to $\cO(\sqrt{|μ|})$ shows that the infinite-depth problem is singular.

</details>


### [88] [Recovery of time-dependent coefficients for the convection-diffusion equation on conformally transversally anisotropic manifolds from partial data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04970)
*Boya Liu,Anamika Purohit*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper addresses an inverse problem for convection-diffusion equations on compact Riemannian manifolds of dimension ≥3, proving that the time-dependent convection term and density coefficient can be uniquely recovered from partial boundary data on conformally transversally anisotropic manifolds, up to a natural gauge, assuming injectivity of attenuated geodesic ray transforms on the transversal manifold.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to extend inverse coefficient determination results for convection-diffusion equations to more general geometric settings—specifically conformally transversally anisotropic (CTA) manifolds—and under partial data conditions, which are more realistic for practical applications than full data measurements.

Method: The approach utilizes boundary measurements encoded in a partial input-output operator and leverages the geometric structure of CTA manifolds. The proof relies on the injectivity of attenuated geodesic ray transforms of one-forms and functions on the transversal manifold to disentangle the convection term and density coefficient, establishing uniqueness up to a natural gauge transformation.

Result: The paper proves that both the time-dependent convection term and the density coefficient are uniquely determined by the partial input-output operator, up to a natural gauge, on CTA manifolds of dimension at least three with injective attenuated ray transforms on the transversal part.

Conclusion: This establishes a uniqueness result for a coupled inverse problem under partial data on a broad class of manifolds, generalizing prior work and highlighting the sufficiency of ray transform injectivity conditions for recovering convection-diffusion parameters.

Abstract: We study an inverse problem of recovering a time-dependent convection term and density coefficient of the convection-diffusion equation from partial data on a certain type of compact Riemannian manifold of dimension at least three. We prove that the knowledge of a partial input-output operator determines both coefficients uniquely up to a natural gauge. Our geometric setting is conformally transversally anisotropic manifolds, that is, compact Riemannian manifolds with boundary that are conformally embedded into a product of the Euclidean line and a transversal manifold. Additionally, we assume that the attenuated geodesic ray transforms of one-forms and functions are both injective on the transversal manifold.

</details>


### [89] [Nondegeneracy and Morse Index of Ginzburg--Landau Vortices](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04976)
*Manuel del Pino,Yong Liu,Monica Musso,Juncheng Wei,Wen Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves that the standard degree-two and degree-three vortex solutions of the Ginzburg-Landau equation are nondegenerate, and computes their Morse indices using new explicit bounds on the modulus of these solutions and a comparison argument.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Establishing nondegeneracy and computing Morse indices of vortex solutions is fundamental for understanding the qualitative structure and stability of solutions to the Ginzburg-Landau equation, which is central to superconductivity theory.

Method: The proof uses new explicit upper and lower bounds on the modulus of the degree-two and degree-three vortex solutions, combined with a comparison argument to establish nondegeneracy and compute Morse indices.

Result: The degree-two and degree-three vortex solutions are proven to be nondegenerate, and their Morse indices are explicitly computed.

Conclusion: The method is expected to be generalizable to higher-degree vortex solutions, suggesting a pathway for extending this nondegeneracy analysis beyond the cases treated here.

Abstract: We prove that the standard degree-two and degree-three vortex solutions of the Ginzburg-Landau equation are nondegenerate. Their Morse indices are also computed. The proof relies on new explicit upper and lower bounds of the modulus of these solutions and a comparison argument. It is expected that our method can be generalized to study higher degree solutions.

</details>


### [90] [A spectral approach to the narrow escape problem in two-dimensional domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05007)
*Louis Carillo,Tony Lelièvre,Thomas Normand,Urbain Vaes*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the exit time and exit point of Brownian motion in a 2D domain with reflecting boundaries and small disjoint exit windows, deriving a precise description of the exit event when starting from the quasi-stationary distribution in the limit of infinitely small windows.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain a precise asymptotic description of the escape event (both time and location) for Brownian motion exiting a 2D domain through small holes, under the natural assumption that the process starts from the quasi-stationary distribution.

Method: The study considers a two-dimensional domain with reflecting boundary conditions except for small disjoint exit windows, and analyzes the law of the exit time and exit point in the limit where the exit windows shrink to zero, assuming the process starts from the quasi-stationary distribution.

Result: A precise description of the exit event (exit time and exit point) is obtained in the limit of infinitely small exit windows.

Conclusion: The paper provides a complete characterization of the asymptotic exit behavior for Brownian motion escaping a 2D domain through multiple small windows when initiated from the quasi-stationary distribution.

Abstract: We study the law of the exit time and exit point of a Brownian motion in a two-dimensional domain with reflecting boundary conditions, except on small disjoint exit windows through which the stochastic process can escape the domain. In the limit of infinitely small exit windows, it is natural to assume that the process starts from the quasi-stationary distribution. In this setting, we obtain a precise description of the exit event.

</details>


### [91] [A point-free theory of quantitative homogenization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05077)
*Thuyen Dang,Yuliya Gorb,Silvia Jiménez Bolaños*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: A purely operator-theoretic framework for quantitative homogenization that bypasses traditional spatial regularity and probabilistic assumptions, deriving explicit norm resolvent estimates from the algebraic structure of multiscale operators and Hilbert space geometry, unifying stationary, non-stationary, periodic, quasi-periodic, and stochastic homogenization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a unified abstract framework for quantitative homogenization that does not rely on large-scale spatial regularity or probabilistic assumptions, following Tartar's vision of a point-free theory.

Method: Derives explicit norm resolvent estimates using only algebraic structure of multiscale operators and abstract Hilbert space geometry. Uses orthogonal decomposition of state space governed by a Schur complement, a frequency-splitting technique, and solves a generalized Sylvester equation controlling the commutator between differential structure and oscillatory material properties.

Result: The framework unifies stationary, non-stationary, periodic, quasi-periodic, and stochastic homogenization. Physical distinctions between media and their convergence rates are captured entirely by the behavior of the spectral measure of the microscopic derivative operator near zero frequency.

Conclusion: The abstract operator-theoretic perspective shows that all homogenization regimes can be treated within a single framework, with convergence behavior determined solely by spectral properties near zero frequency of the microscopic derivative operator.

Abstract: We introduce a purely operator-theoretic framework for quantitative
  homogenization that bypasses the traditional reliance on large-scale
  spatial regularity and probabilistic assumptions. Inspired by
  Tartar's vision of a point-free theory, we derive explicit
  norm resolvent estimates using only the algebraic structure of
  multiscale operators and the abstract geometry of Hilbert spaces. In
  this framework, the effective macroscopic dynamics and the abstract
  corrector emerge naturally from an orthogonal decomposition of the
  state space, governed algebraically by a Schur complement. To
  quantify the convergence rate, we introduce a frequency-splitting
  technique and solve a generalized Sylvester equation that controls
  the commutator between the differential structure and the highly
  oscillatory material properties.
  This abstract perspective unifies stationary, non-stationary,
  periodic, quasi-periodic, and stochastic homogenization. We
  demonstrate that the physical distinctions between these media--and
  their respective convergence rates--are entirely captured by the
  behavior of the spectral measure of the microscopic derivative
  operator near zero frequency.

</details>


### [92] [Counterexamples to Schiffer's Conjecture](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05114)
*Gonzalo Cao-Labora,Jaume de Dios Pont*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper disproves the Schiffer conjecture and Pompeiu's problem in R^2 by constructing infinitely many non-spherical planar domains with the required properties, using a novel relaxation strategy that treats N as a real number and applies bifurcation theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Schiffer conjecture and Pompeiu's problem are long-standing open problems in spectral geometry and integral geometry, respectively. Disproving them in the planar case is a significant result.

Method: The authors relax the problem by allowing N (the symmetry order) to be any real number rather than just integers. They apply bifurcation theory to this relaxed problem, showing that the local bifurcation branch size can be taken independently of N. This independence allows branches starting near integer N to reach integer values.

Result: Infinitely many planar domains that are N-fold symmetric (N sufficiently large), are not balls, yet admit Neumann eigenfunctions constant at the boundary and satisfy the Pompeiu condition.

Conclusion: Both the Schiffer conjecture and Pompeiu's problem are false in R^2, as explicit counterexamples are constructed.

Abstract: The Schiffer conjecture states that if a smooth domain $Ω\subset \mathbb{R}^n$ admits a Neumann eigenfunction of the Laplacian which is constant at the boundary, then the domain is a ball. It is intimately related to Pompeiu's problem, stating that if a nonzero function integrates zero over any rigid motion of $Ω$, then $Ω$ is a ball.
  We disprove both conjectures in $\mathbb{R}^2$, constructing infinitely many planar domains $Ω$ which are not balls and satisfy the conditions above. Our domains are $N$-fold symmetric, with $N$ sufficiently large. Our approach is based on a novel strategy of considering a relaxed problem where $N$ can be any real number (which corresponds to the Schiffer problem only when $N$ is a natural number). We then apply bifurcation theory to this relaxed problem, showing that the size of the local bifurcation branch can be taken independently of $N$. This result allows us to conclude that branches starting with $N$ sufficiently close to an integer reach integer values of $N$.

</details>


### [93] [On a two-dimensional Camassa-Holm-Zakharov-Kuznetsov equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05334)
*Xixuan Li,Kai Yan,Tiantian Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper introduces and analyzes the Camassa-Holm-Zakharov-Kuznetsov (CH-ZK) equation, a new 2D nonlinear dispersive wave model. The authors establish local well-posedness, derive a blow-up criterion, construct finite-time blow-up solutions, prove a unique continuation property, and investigate traveling wave solutions including peaked and smooth solitary waves with a rigidity theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to develop and analyze a new two-dimensional nonlinear dispersive wave model that combines the nonlinear structure of the Camassa-Holm equation with the transverse Laplacian dispersion of the Zakharov-Kuznetsov equation, thereby extending the understanding of wave phenomena to higher dimensions.

Method: The paper establishes local well-posedness in suitable Sobolev spaces, derives a blow-up criterion for strong solutions, constructs finite-time blow-up solutions, proves a unique continuation property, and analyzes traveling wave solutions (both peaked and smooth solitary waves) with a rigidity theorem based on wave speed magnitude.

Result: Local well-posedness is established, a blow-up criterion and finite-time blow-up strong solutions are derived, a unique continuation property is proven, and existence of peaked and smooth solitary waves is shown along with a rigidity theorem for traveling wave solutions.

Conclusion: The paper provides a comprehensive mathematical analysis of the CH-ZK equation, covering well-posedness, blow-up phenomena, unique continuation, and traveling wave classification, offering a complete theoretical foundation for this new 2D nonlinear dispersive wave model.

Abstract: This paper is devoted to a new two-dimensional nonlinear dispersive wave model named as the Camassa-Holm-Zakharov-Kuznetsov (CH-ZK) equation which combines the nonlinear structure of the Camassa-Holm equation with the transverse Laplacian dispersion of the Zakharov-Kuznetsov equation. We first establish the local well-posedness of its Cauchy problem in a suitable Sobolev space and derive a blow-up criterion for strong solutions. Then the finite-time blow-up strong solutions for the CH-ZK equation have been constructed. Moreover, we prove a unique continuation property for the solutions to the CH-ZK equation. Finally, we investigate the existence of both peaked and smooth solitary waves, and obtain a rigidity theorem for the traveling wave solutions according to the magnitude of wave speed.

</details>


### [94] [Unstable Manifolds of Stratified Euler Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05370)
*Zhiwu Lin,Yanbo Wang,Chongchun Zeng*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper constructs local stable and unstable manifolds for spectrally unstable steady states of the incompressible stratified Euler equations on d-dimensional domains, assuming a sufficient spectral gap relative to the maximal Lyapunov exponent of the background flow, using the Lyapunov-Perron method on the infinite-dimensional manifold of volume-preserving Lagrangian maps.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the nonlinear dynamics near spectrally unstable steady states of the incompressible stratified Euler equations, particularly to establish the existence of stable and unstable manifolds that characterize local dynamical behavior, which is a fundamental step toward understanding instability mechanisms in fluid flows.

Method: The proof reformulates the Euler equation as an ODE on the infinite-dimensional manifold of volume-preserving Lagrangian maps, treating density as a frozen Lagrangian parameter and as the weight in the L^2 metric. Then the Lyapunov-Perron method is applied to construct local stable and unstable manifolds, requiring an exponential dichotomy of the linearized equation with a spectral gap larger than the maximal Lyapunov exponent of the background flow.

Result: Local stable and unstable manifolds are constructed for spectrally unstable steady states under the assumption of exponential dichotomy with a sufficiently large spectral gap relative to the maximal Lyapunov exponent. Applications to two-dimensional steady flows are discussed.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a robust framework for constructing invariant manifolds for unstable steady states of the stratified Euler equations, with the key technical innovation being the Lagrangian formulation that allows treating the density in a frozen manner while using it as a weight in the metric. This provides a foundation for further nonlinear instability analysis in stratified fluid dynamics.

Abstract: We consider a spectrally unstable steady state $(ρ_0,v_0)$ of the incompressible stratified Euler equations on a class of $d$-dimensional domains. Assuming that the linearized equation admits an exponential dichotomy with a reasonably large spectral gap relative to the maximal Lyapunov exponent of the background steady flow $v_0$, we construct the local stable and unstable manifolds of $(ρ_0,v_0)$. The proof is based on the Lyapunov--Perron method after reformulating the Euler equation as an ODE on the infinite-dimensional manifold of volume-preserving Lagrangian maps, with the density treated as a frozen Lagrangian parameter as well as the weight in the $L^2$ metric. We also discuss some applications to two-dimensional steady flows.

</details>


### [95] [Sharp $H^2$-regularity in dimensions $N\geq 5$ and beyond for two classes of elliptic problems with critical unbounded coefficients](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05383)
*Cristian Cazacu,Adelina Călina*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes sharp parameter thresholds for H²-regularity of weak solutions to two classes of λ-parametric elliptic problems with critical unbounded perturbations in dimensions N≥5. For the first problem with the singular perturbation (x·∇v)/|x|², H² regularity holds for all λ < (N-2)/2, fully extending previous results. For the second problem with v/|x|² perturbation, H² regularity holds for λ > -N(N-4)/4 and fails for λ in (-(N-2)²/4, -N(N-4)/4], sharply extending the Kato perturbation theory range. The proofs rely on newly developed sharp second-order Hardy-Rellich type inequalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to precisely characterize the H²-regularity of weak solutions to elliptic problems with critical singular perturbations involving the origin. While well-posedness in H₀¹ is known via the Hardy inequality, the question of which parameter ranges guarantee H²-regularity was not fully resolved, particularly for positive λ in the first problem (previously only λ≤0 was covered) and for the full sharp range in the second problem (previously only a restricted symmetric interval was known via Kato perturbation theory).

Method: The authors study two λ-parametric elliptic problems in a bounded C²-domain containing the origin. For the first problem, they prove H² regularity for all λ < (N-2)/2 using new sharp second-order Hardy-Rellich type inequalities tailored to the operator. For the second problem, they establish both a positive result (H² regularity for λ > -N(N-4)/4) and a negative result (failure of H² regularity for λ in the range (-(N-2)²/4, -N(N-4)/4]), thereby determining the sharp threshold. The developed Hardy-Rellich inequalities for the involved elliptic operators are essential to these proofs.

Result: For the first problem (-Δv + λ(x·∇v)/|x|² = f), any solution v ∈ H₀¹(Ω) belongs to H²(Ω) for any λ < (N-2)/2, provided f ∈ L²(Ω), fully extending the previous result of Kim and Tsai (which covered λ≤0). For the second problem (-Δv + λv/|x|² = f), H² regularity holds for any λ > -N(N-4)/4 and fails for any λ ∈ (-(N-2)²/4, -N(N-4)/4], sharply extending the previously known range (-N(N-4)/4, N(N-4)/4) obtained via Kato perturbation theory.

Conclusion: The paper provides sharp and complete characterizations of H²-regularity thresholds for both classes of elliptic problems with critical unbounded perturbations. The results fully extend the first problem to all admissible λ values and give an exact threshold for the second problem, improving upon previous results from both Kim-Tsai and Kato's perturbation theory. The newly developed sharp second-order Hardy-Rellich type inequalities are the key technical tools enabling these sharp results.

Abstract: We establish sharp parameter thresholds governing $H^2$ -regularity of weak solutions in $H_0^1$ for two classes of elliptic problems with critical unbounded perturbations, in dimensions $N\geq 5$. More precisely, we consider two distinct $λ$-parametric elliptic problems $ - Δv + λ\frac{x\cdot \nabla v}{|x|^{2}} =f$ and $-Δv + λ\frac{v}{|x|^2}=f$ posed in a bounded $C^2$-domain $Ω\subset \mathbb{R}^N$ containing the origin $x=0$. We observe that the singular perturbations $\frac{x\cdot \nabla v}{|x|^{2}}$ and $\frac{v}{|x|^2}$ are homogeneous operators of order 2 consistent with the scaling of the Laplacian. In view of the Hardy inequality the problems are well-posed in $H_0^1(Ω)$ for $λ<\frac{N-2}{2}$ and $λ>-\frac{(N-2)^2}{4}$ respectively.
  The main results are as follows. For the first problem we show that any solution $v\in H_0^1(Ω)$ belongs to $H^2(Ω)$ for any $λ< \frac{N-2}{2}$ provided $f\in L^2(Ω)$. This fully extends the previous $H^2$ regularity properties obtained by Kim and Tsai in \cite{Kim-Tsai} for $λ\leq 0$. For the second problem we show that $H^2$ regularity holds for any $λ>- \frac{N(N-4)}{4}$ and fails for any $λ\in \left(-\frac{(N-2)^2}{4},-\frac{N(N-4)}{4}\right]$. This extends sharply the range of $λ\in \left(-\frac{N(N-4)}{4}, \frac{N(N-4)}{4}\right)$ obtained when applying the Kato perturbation theory in \cite{Kato}. In addition, we develop sharp second order Hardy-Rellich type inequalities for the involved elliptic operators which are essential in the above proofs.

</details>


### [96] [Truncations for fractional Laplacians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05433)
*Egor Ignatev,Alexander I. Nazarov,Pavel Nichitenko,Artur Tursunbaev*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves and significantly generalizes a conjecture by Nazarov regarding the spectral fractional Dirichlet Laplacian: for s in (1, 3/2), the quadratic form strictly increases when a sign-changing function u is replaced by its absolute value |u| on a bounded Lipschitz domain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The conjecture by A.I. Nazarov concerned the behavior of the spectral fractional Dirichlet Laplacian quadratic form under taking absolute values of sign-changing functions—a question with implications for the study of fractional eigenvalue problems and sharp constants in related inequalities.

Method: The authors prove and widely generalize Nazarov's conjecture using analytic techniques for the spectral fractional Laplacian, establishing strict monotonicity of the quadratic form Q_s^SP under the map u ↦ |u| for s ∈ (1, 3/2) on bounded Lipschitz domains.

Result: The conjecture is proven and broadly generalized: for s ∈ (1, 3/2), Q_s^SP[|u|] < Q_s^SP[u] strictly holds whenever u ∈ H̃^s(Ω) changes sign in Ω, where Ω is a bounded Lipschitz domain.

Conclusion: The strict increase of the spectral fractional Dirichlet Laplacian quadratic form under the absolute value map is confirmed and extended beyond the original conjecture's scope, settling an open problem for the range s ∈ (1, 3/2).

Abstract: Let $Ω\subset\mathbb R^n$ be a bounded Lipschitz domain. We prove and widely generalize a conjecture of A.\,I.~Nazarov \cite{Naz21}: for $s\in(1,\frac 32)$ the quadratic form $Q^{\rm SP}_s[u]$ of the spectral fractional Dirichlet Laplacian strictly increases under the map $u\mapsto|u|$ provided $u\in\tilde H^s(Ω)$ changes sign in $Ω$.

</details>


### [97] [Analysis of a coupled magneto-quasistatic--mechanical model of an eddy current brake](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05606)
*Anna Fischer,Kaja Krhač,Timo Reis,Alexander Wierzba*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a coupled magneto-quasistatic model for an eddy current brake, formulated as a nonlinear infinite-dimensional differential-algebraic system with structural (rather than material-based) nonlinearity arising from the electromechanical feedback loop between the magnetic field and rotor angular momentum. The authors introduce a finite-dissipation solution concept and prove global existence and uniqueness of weak solutions for given initial data (magnetic field and angular momentum) and inputs (coil current and external torque).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to provide a rigorous mathematical analysis of the eddy current brake model, which involves a closed electromechanical feedback loop coupling Maxwell's equations with rotor dynamics. The challenge is the structural nonlinearity in the differential-algebraic system, requiring a suitable solution concept and existence/uniqueness guarantees for physically meaningful initial data and inputs.

Method: The authors formulate the coupled model as a nonlinear infinite-dimensional differential-algebraic system, where nonlinearity stems from the feedback coupling (angular momentum → velocity → motional eddy currents → Lorentz torque → rotor). They introduce a finite-dissipation solution concept tailored to handle this structural nonlinearity, and employ analytical techniques to establish global existence and uniqueness of weak solutions.

Result: Global existence and uniqueness of weak solutions are proven for the coupled magneto-quasistatic eddy current brake model, with well-posedness established for initial data comprising the magnetic field and angular momentum, and for inputs consisting of the supplied coil current and externally applied torque.

Conclusion: The finite-dissipation solution concept provides a rigorous framework for analyzing the nonlinear differential-algebraic system governing the eddy current brake, establishing a solid mathematical foundation for this electromechanical coupled problem.

Abstract: We study a coupled magneto-quasistatic model for an eddy current brake. The model leads to a nonlinear infinite-dimensional differential-algebraic system in which the nonlinearity is structural rather than caused by nonlinear material laws. It is generated by the closed electromechanical feedback loop: the angular momentum determines the velocity of the conducting disk, the velocity and the magnetic field generate motional eddy currents, and these currents induce a Lorentz torque acting back on the rotor. We introduce a finite-dissipation solution concept and prove global existence and uniqueness of weak solutions for initial data given by the magnetic field and the angular momentum, and for inputs given by the supplied coil current and the externally applied torque.

</details>


### [98] [Time-periodic solutions of the Vlasov-Poisson-Boltzmann system with a general external force in $\mathbb{R}^3$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05637)
*Renjun Duan,Jinkai Ni*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves global existence and asymptotic stability of small time-periodic solutions to the Vlasov-Poisson-Boltzmann system with non-potential time-periodic external forces in R^3, using a hybrid function space combining low-frequency Besov framework with high-frequency energy estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The time-periodic problem for the VPB system with non-potential external forces remains open. Previous approaches treated the nonlinear Vlasov force as a generic source term at low frequencies, which fails to close the estimates needed for well-posedness. A structural understanding of the Vlasov force's role at low frequencies is needed.

Method: The authors construct a hybrid function space combining the low-frequency Besov framework (for the forced Boltzmann equation) with control of the self-consistent electric field. The key novelty is treating the nonlinear Vlasov force at low frequencies by exploiting the Poisson equation and macroscopic balance laws to recover structural cancellation required for VPB semi-group estimates. This is combined with high-frequency energy estimates and weighted microscopic propagation. Serrin's method is used to obtain the unique time-periodic solution.

Result: Global existence of small solutions is proved in the hybrid function space. Asymptotic stability of solutions driven by the same force is established, yielding a unique time-periodic solution with matching period. As a corollary, existence and stability of stationary solutions for time-independent external forces are obtained.

Conclusion: The paper closes the time-periodic well-posedness theory for the VPB system with non-potential external forces by exploiting the structural cancellation of the Vlasov force at low frequencies, extending the theory to both time-periodic and stationary settings.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the time-periodic problem for the Vlasov-Poisson-Boltzmann (VPB) system with a given time-periodic external force in the whole space $\mathbb{R}^3$. The force is allowed to be non-potential. Around the global Maxwellian, we prove the global existence of small solutions in a hybrid function space that combines the low-frequency Besov framework for the forced Boltzmann equation with a corresponding control of the self-consistent electric field. The main novelty lies in the treatment of the nonlinear Vlasov force $-\nabla_xφ\cdot \nabla_vf + \frac{1}{2}(v \cdot \nabla_xφ)f$ at low frequencies. Rather than treating it as a generic source term, we exploit the Poisson equation and macroscopic balance laws to recover the structural cancellation required for the VPB semi-group estimates, which combined with high-frequency energy estimates and weighted microscopic propagation, yields a closed global well-posedness theory. We further prove the asymptotic stability of small solutions driven by the same force. When the external force is time-periodic, Serrin's method yields a unique time-periodic solution with the same period, together with its stability. As a direct consequence, our result also gives the existence and stability of stationary solutions when the external force is time-independent.

</details>


### [99] [On dynamic stability of energetically stable equilibria of the Navier-Stokes-Korteweg flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05654)
*Yoshikazu Giga,Naoto Kajiwara,Kazuyuki Tsuda*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves exponential stability of energetically stable equilibria for the Navier-Stokes-Korteweg equations in bounded domains or periodic cells, even when pressure is non-monotone (allowing two-phase equilibria). For isolated equilibria, exponential stability holds in dimensions ≤ 3; for non-isolated equilibria, solutions converge to another equilibrium exponentially fast. The proof uses a generalized stability principle by Prüss, Wilke, and Simonett (2013), without smallness assumptions on equilibria.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Navier-Stokes-Korteweg equations model two-phase flows with non-monotone pressure, allowing non-constant equilibria. Understanding the stability of such equilibria is critical for the qualitative theory, but prior work imposed restrictive assumptions such as monotone pressure or smallness conditions on equilibria.

Method: The authors use a Hilbert space framework and apply a (generalized) stability principle due to J. Prüss, M. Wilke, and G. Simonett (2013). They verify that energetically stable, non-degenerate isolated equilibria satisfy the conditions of this principle, yielding exponential stability. For non-isolated equilibria, they show convergence to another equilibrium with exponential rate.

Result: (i) Isolated energetically stable non-degenerate equilibria are exponentially stable in space dimensions ≤ 3 under isothermal Navier-Stokes-Korteweg flows. (ii) For non-isolated equilibria, any global-in-time solution near an energetically stable equilibrium converges exponentially fast to possibly another equilibrium. No smallness assumptions on the equilibria are required.

Conclusion: The stability theory for Navier-Stokes-Korteweg equations is significantly extended to non-monotone pressure regimes (two-phase scenarios) without smallness restrictions, establishing exponential stability via a generalized stability principle.

Abstract: We consider the Navier-Stokes-Korteweg equations in a bounded domain or a periodic cell. The pressure considered in this paper may not be monotone with respect to the density so that there exist non-constant equilibria allowing two-phases. Using a simple Hilbert space framework, we prove that if an isolated equilibrium is energetically stable and non- degenerate, it is exponentially stable under the isothermal Navier-Stokes-Korteweg flows when the space dimension is less than or equal to three. For non-isolated case, we prove that a global-in-time solution near an energetically stable equilibrium converges to possibly another equilibrium exponentially fast. No smallness assumptions on equilibria are imposed. For the proof we apply a (generalized) stability principle due to J. Prüss, M. Wilke and G. Simonett (2013).

</details>


### [100] [Periodic Solutions to the Steady Viscous Burgers Equation: A Constructive Example](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05677)
*Quansen Jiu,Yuhan Cao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper constructs uniformly bounded periodic solutions to the steady viscous Burgers equation with periodic external force using a direct Fourier series approach, providing explicit constructive procedures and sharper convergence rates than prior work by Jauslin-Kreiss-Moser.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct periodic solutions to the steady viscous Burgers equation that are uniformly bounded with respect to viscosity, improving upon previous results both in terms of explicitness of construction and convergence rate.

Method: The approach uses a direct Fourier series method. First, starting from a special solution of the non-viscous Burgers equation with a specific force, approximate solutions to the viscous equation are solved explicitly. Second, the periodic solution is constructed by solving an initial value problem for a second-order ordinary differential equation.

Result: The approach yields an explicit constructive procedure for periodic solutions and achieves a sharper convergence rate (up to higher order) compared to Jauslin-Kreiss-Moser's result as viscosity vanishes.

Conclusion: A direct Fourier series method provides an explicit and more accurate construction of uniformly bounded periodic solutions to the viscous Burgers equation, improving over existing results.

Abstract: In this paper, we consider the periodic problem to steady viscous Burgers equation with an periodic and external force. Our main aim is to construct periodic solutions to this model which are uniformly bounded with respect to the viscosity via a direct Fourier series approach. Firstly, as an example, starting from a special solution of the non-viscous Burgers equation with a specific force, we solve the approximate solutions to the viscous Burgers equation in an explicit way. Secondly, we construct the solution to the periodic problem of the viscous Burgers equation with the external force by solving the initial value problem to a second ordinary differential equations, which is uniformly bounded with respect to the viscosity. Compared with Jauslin-Kreiss-Moser's result, our approach provides an explicit constructive procedure for the periodic solutions and yields a sharper convergence rate (up to higher order) when the viscosity vanishes.

</details>


### [101] [Local well-posedness of the boundary layer for a pseudo-plastic fluid by energy methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05693)
*Zhonger Wu,Zhong Tan*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the well-posedness of the boundary layer for a pseudo-plastic fluid using energy methods under Oleinik's monotonicity assumption, addressing two key difficulties: derivative loss (solved via Masmoudi-Wong's cancellation mechanism) and viscosity-related issues (solved via monotonicity assumptions and Faà di Bruno formula to control high-order derivatives of viscosity).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish well-posedness of the boundary layer for pseudo-plastic fluids, which is challenging due to derivative loss and difficulties caused by viscosity in the analysis.

Method: Energy methods are employed. For derivative loss, the cancellation mechanism of Masmoudi and Wong is borrowed. For viscosity difficulties, the monotonicity assumption combined with the Faà di Bruno formula provides precise control over high-order derivatives of viscosity.

Result: The main contribution is obtaining precise control over high-order derivatives of viscosity through the combination of monotonicity and Faà di Bruno formula, enabling well-posedness results for the pseudo-plastic boundary layer.

Conclusion: The paper successfully establishes well-posedness of the boundary layer for pseudo-plastic fluids under Oleinik's monotonicity assumption, with the viscosity control technique as the novel contribution.

Abstract: We study the well-posedness of the boundary layer for a pseudo-plastic fluid by energy methods under Oleinik's monotonicity assumption. We need to address two main difficulties: derivative loss and the difficulty caused by viscosity. For the first difficulty, we borrow the cancellation mechanism proposed by Masmoudi and Wong [CPAM, 2015]. For the second difficulty, we combine the monotonicity assumption and Faà di Bruno formula to obtain precise control over the high-order derivatives of viscosity, which is the main contribution of this paper.

</details>


### [102] [Mixed local and nonlocal weighted singular quasilinear elliptic problem and its associated Sobolev-type inequality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05696)
*Prashanta Garain*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies a class of mixed anisotropic and nonlocal singular quasilinear elliptic problems with Muckenhoupt weights, establishing existence and uniqueness of weak solutions via monotone approximation and variational methods, and characterizing the best constant in a related weighted Sobolev-type inequality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The combination of singular nonlinearity (blowing up near the origin) with anisotropy, weighted degeneracy (Muckenhoupt weights), and nonlocal diffusion creates significant analytical challenges that have not been addressed before. This work aims to fill this gap by establishing well-posedness and extremal results for this challenging class of problems.

Method: The authors employ a combination of monotone approximation techniques, weighted Sobolev embeddings, compactness arguments, and variational methods to establish existence and uniqueness of weak solutions under suitable assumptions on the datum.

Result: The paper proves existence and uniqueness of weak solutions, characterizes the best constant in the associated weighted mixed anisotropic and nonlocal Sobolev-type inequality, proves that this constant is attained, and shows that the normalized weak solution is the unique extremal. These results are new even in the mixed weighted Laplace case p=2.

Conclusion: The results establish a complete theory for singular quasilinear elliptic problems combining multiple difficult features (anisotropy, nonlocality, weights, singularity), and the findings are novel even in the simplified p=2 mixed weighted Laplace case.

Abstract: We consider a class of mixed anisotropic and nonlocal singular quasilinear elliptic problem associated with Muckenhoupt weights. The presence of the singular nonlinearity, which blows up near the origin along with its interaction with anisotropy, weighted degeneracy, and nonlocal diffusion creates significant analytical challenges. We employ monotone approximation, weighted Sobolev embeddings, compactness, and variational methods to establish the existence and uniqueness of weak solutions under suitable assumptions on the datum. Further, we characterize the best constant in an associated weighted mixed anisotropic and nonlocal Sobolev-type inequality, prove that it is attained, and show that the normalized weak solution is the unique extremal. These results are new even in the mixed weighted Laplace case \(p=2\).

</details>


### [103] [Positive Solutions for a One-Dimensional Minkowski-Curvature Equation with a Sign-Changing Nonlinearity under Mixed Boundary Conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05711)
*Ao Xiao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes the existence of a positive solution for a one-dimensional Minkowski-curvature equation with mixed boundary conditions and sign-changing nonlinearity, using cone theory, principal-eigenvalue comparisons, and fixed point index methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The study addresses the challenge of finding positive solutions to Minkowski-curvature equations, which arise in geometry and relativity, particularly when the nonlinearity is sign-changing and boundary conditions are mixed. Existing methods need adaptation to handle the nonstandard curvature (mean curvature operator) and the complexity introduced by sign-changing nonlinearities.

Method: The authors employ several key techniques: (1) a nonnegative linear shift combined with the Green kernel of the associated linear problem to construct an invariant cone; (2) principal-eigenvalue comparisons and fixed point index arguments to establish cone expansion near zero and compression at infinity; (3) a globally defined auxiliary equation with a first-contact argument to ensure the solution's slope is below one (the natural bound for the Minkowski curvature equation), thereby guaranteeing it solves the original problem; and (4) a directly verifiable asymptotic criterion for applications.

Result: The paper proves the existence of a positive solution to the one-dimensional Minkowski-curvature equation with mixed boundary conditions and sign-changing nonlinearity. Additionally, it derives a directly verifiable asymptotic criterion that can be applied in practice.

Conclusion: The work provides a rigorous existence result for positive solutions of Minkowski-curvature equations under general conditions, extending the applicability of cone-theoretic fixed point methods to this class of problems and offering a practical asymptotic criterion for verification.

Abstract: We establish the existence of a positive solution for a one-dimensional Minkowski-curvature equation with mixed boundary conditions and a sign-changing nonlinearity. A nonnegative linear shift and the Green kernel of the associated linear problem produce an invariant cone. Principal-eigenvalue comparisons and the fixed point index give cone expansion near zero and compression at infinity. A globally defined auxiliary equation and a first-contact argument show that the resulting solution has slope below one and hence solves the original equation. We also derive a directly verifiable asymptotic criterion.

</details>


### [104] [A note on generalized BV mean curvature flow with a critical forcing term](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05760)
*Kiichi Tashiro*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This note proves that the weak mean curvature flow with critical forcing term constructed by Liu-Tonegawa satisfies the BV-type area change formula, making it a generalized BV flow (as proposed by Stuvard-Tonegawa). The authors identify minimal conditions under which a Brakke flow satisfies the area-change formula, derive a lower bound for extinction time, and outline a compactness theorem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between Brakke flows (which lack area-change formula) and the newly proposed generalized BV flow framework by Stuvard-Tonegawa, by establishing that the Liu-Tonegawa critical forcing flow is indeed a generalized BV flow.

Method: The authors identify minimal conditions under which a Brakke flow satisfies the BV-type area-change formula, then verify these conditions for the Liu-Tonegawa weak mean curvature flow with critical forcing term. They apply this result to derive extinction time bounds and outline a compactness theorem for generalized BV flows.

Result: The Liu-Tonegawa flow with critical forcing term is shown to satisfy the BV-type area-change formula, thus qualifying as a generalized BV flow. A lower bound for extinction time is derived, and a compactness theorem is outlined.
