Conclusion: The paper strengthens the theoretical foundation of generalized BV flows by connecting them concretely to existing weak mean curvature flows with critical forcing, and provides useful quantitative estimates (extinction time) for these flows.

Abstract: In this note, we show that a weak mean curvature flow with critical forcing term obtained by Liu--Tonegawa (2024) satisfies the BV-type area change formula, that is, their flow is not only a Brakke flow but also a generalized BV flow, which is proposed by Stuvard--Tonegawa (2024). To establish this result, we identify minimal conditions under which a Brakke flow satisfies the area-change formula. As an application of our main theorem, we derive a lower bound for the extinction time of generalized BV flows with a critical forcing term. We also outline the proof of a compactness theorem for generalized BV flows.

</details>


### [105] [Optimization-Based Identification of Effective Coefficients for Wave Equations in Spatio-Temporal Metamaterials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05779)
*Christian Döding,Vishnu Raveendran,Barbara Verfürth*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper develops an optimization-based method to identify effective (homogenized) coefficients for wave equations in space-time heterogeneous media, proving convergence of the identified coefficient to the homogenized coefficient as the microscopic scale vanishes, along with convergence of the corresponding effective solution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Wave equations in heterogeneous media (e.g., spatio-temporal metamaterials) require effective macroscopic parameters, but determining these from observed wave propagation is challenging, especially when the underlying space-time-dependent coefficient is unknown.

Method: An optimization-based approach that minimizes a cost functional to identify a constant effective coefficient, extending optimization-based coefficient identification techniques from elliptic multiscale problems to wave equations. The method uses measured space-time solution data without requiring knowledge of the underlying heterogeneous coefficient.

Result: Provided a homogenized limit exists, the identified coefficient converges to the homogenized coefficient as the microscopic scale tends to zero. Convergence of the effective solution toward the heterogeneous solution is established, including strong convergence in L^2 and weak convergence of first-order space and time derivatives. Numerical experiments demonstrate performance for non-periodic space-time heterogeneous media, including cases without a known homogenized limit.

Conclusion: The optimization-based identification method successfully extends to wave equations in heterogeneous media, providing a robust way to recover effective coefficients from measurements and demonstrating convergence even in challenging non-periodic settings.

Abstract: We study the identification of effective coefficients for wave equations in heterogeneous media. Such equations arise in the modeling of spatio-temporal metamaterials, where the underlying material properties exhibit variations in both space and time. While homogenization provides effective models in the asymptotic regime of vanishing microscopic scales, determining macroscopic parameters from observations of wave propagation remains challenging. We introduce an optimization-based method that identifies a constant effective coefficient by minimizing a cost functional. The approach is designed for situations in which the underlying space-time-dependent coefficient is unknown, while the solution is available in space and time by measurements. We extend optimization-based coefficient identification techniques from elliptic multiscale problems to wave equations and prove that, provided a homogenized limit exists, the identified coefficient converges to the homogenized coefficient as the microscopic scale tends to zero. Furthermore, we establish convergence of the corresponding effective solution towards the heterogeneous solution, including strong convergence in $L^2$ and weak convergence of first-order space and time derivatives. Numerical experiments further demonstrate the performance of the method for non-periodic space-time heterogeneous media, including cases for which a homogenized limit is not known to exist.

</details>


### [106] [Vanishing capillary limit for compressible Navier-Stokes-Korteweg system in a bounded interval with large initial data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05849)
*Jiaxin Ling,Huanyao Wen,Xinhua Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper studies the vanishing capillary limit for the isentropic compressible Navier-Stokes-Korteweg system on a bounded interval, deriving an optimal convergence rate in L∞ norm globally in time for arbitrarily large initial data, rigorously recovering the isentropic compressible Navier-Stokes system.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the vanishing capillary limit from the Navier-Stokes-Korteweg system to the standard Navier-Stokes system in a bounded domain, overcoming challenges from the capillary term and boundary effects.

Method: Derives a new uniform dissipative estimate involving the third-order derivative of density and constructs suitable correctors to handle the capillary term and boundary effects in the bounded interval.

Result: Achieves optimal convergence rate of solutions in L∞ norm globally in time, valid for arbitrarily large initial data.

Conclusion: Provides a rigorous derivation of the isentropic compressible Navier-Stokes system from the Navier-Stokes-Korteweg system via the vanishing capillary limit in a bounded interval.

Abstract: In this paper, we study vanishing capillary limit for isentropic compressible Navier-Stokes-Korteweg system in a bounded interval. The main challenges focus on the capillary term and the boundary effect. A new uniform dissipative estimate in terms of the third-order derivative of density and some correctors are derived to handle such difficulties. It leads to the optimal convergence rate of the solutions in $L^\infty$ norm globally in time with arbitrarily large initial data. This work provides a rigorous derivation of the isentropic compressible Navier-Stokes system in a bounded interval from the isentropic compressible Navier-Stokes-Korteweg system via vanishing capillary limit.

</details>


### [107] [Boundary layer analysis for the 2D chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system with logarithmic sensitivity, Part I: Well-posedness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05940)
*Hui Wang,Wendong Wang,Lingling Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the boundary layer convergence of the chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system in a 2D half-space with logarithmic singularity under Navier-slip boundary conditions. The authors perform an exact asymptotic expansion with viscous coefficient ε>0, obtain partial boundary layer profiles and establish their well-posedness. They also prove local well-posedness of the supercritical chemotaxis Euler equation (ε=0) by handling the difficulty from vanishing diffusion terms.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the vanishing viscosity limit (ε→0) and boundary layer behavior for the chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system in a two-dimensional half-space, particularly under Navier-slip boundary conditions with logarithmic singularity. The goal is to rigorously analyze boundary layer convergence, which is a fundamental issue in fluid dynamics with chemotaxis coupling.

Method: The paper performs an exact asymptotic expansion of the chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system with viscous coefficient ε>0, derives partial boundary layer profiles, and proves well-posedness of these profiles. Additionally, the authors establish local well-posedness of the supercritical chemotaxis Euler equation (ε=0) by addressing the challenge posed by the absence of diffusion terms.

Result: The authors obtain partial boundary layer profiles from the asymptotic expansion of the chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system under Navier-slip boundary conditions, and establish well-posedness of these profiles. They also prove local well-posedness of solutions to the supercritical chemotaxis Euler system.

Conclusion: This is the first part of a two-part work on boundary layer convergence for the chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system in a 2D half-space. It establishes the foundation by analyzing boundary layer profiles and proving well-posedness results for both the viscous system and the inviscid (Euler) limit.

Abstract: This is the first part of a two-part work concerning the boundary layer convergence for chemotaxis-Navier-Stokes system in a two-dimensional half-space. In this paper, we investigate the chemotacxis-Navier-Stokes system with the logarithmic singularity under Navier-slip boundary conditions. More precisely, we perform an exact asymptotic expansion for the chemotaxis Navier-Stokes system with viscous coefficient $\varepsilon>0$, and obtain partial boundary layer profiles, establishing the well-posedness of the corresponding boundary layer profiles. Specially, we also establish the local well-posedness of solutions to the supercritical chemotaxis Euler equation (with $\varepsilon=0$) by overcoming the difficulty from the disappearance of diffusion terms.

</details>


### [108] [Prandtl--Batchelor and flux-expulsion selection for steady MHD flows in a disk](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05994)
*Chen Gao,Zhiwu Lin,Jianfeng Zhao*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the simultaneous vanishing-viscosity and vanishing-resistivity limits of steady incompressible MHD flows in a disk, showing convergence to an ideal MHD core with constant vorticity and out-of-plane current density, governed by a new MHD-Wood law and fully coupled flux expulsion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to understand how viscous and resistive effects vanish simultaneously in MHD flows, particularly whether a fully coupled (non-kinematic) theory can realize Prandtl-Batchelor selection and flux expulsion when the magnetic field actively influences the flow rather than being weak/passive.

Method: The proof combines: (1) a non-Alfvénic coercive theory for a periodic MHD boundary layer, (2) global matching of velocity and magnetic fields, (3) a coupled stability estimate adapted to the magnetic boundary condition, and (4) a separate conditional rigidity argument using exact viscous identities and local convergence without interior asymptotic expansions.

Result: Under assumptions (α≠0 and non-Alfvénic |α|≠|β|), solutions converge on interior subdisks to a rigidly rotating ideal MHD core. A new MHD-Wood law determines the velocity core via coupled kinetic-magnetic balance, while the magnetic core is fixed by imposed mean circulation. Zero circulation yields complete interior magnetic expulsion; nonzero circulation leaves a uniform magnetic rotation after nonaxisymmetric modes confine to a thin boundary layer.

Conclusion: This provides the first fully coupled realization of Prandtl-Batchelor selection and flux expulsion where the magnetic field actively modifies the flow, generalizing classical kinematic models. A broader single-eddy family exhibits the same core structure via a rigidity argument.

Abstract: We study the simultaneous vanishing-viscosity and vanishing-resistivity limit of steady incompressible MHD flows in a disk. The boundary velocity is a small nonaxisymmetric perturbation of a rigid rotation with mean angular speed \(α\), while the prescribed tangential magnetic trace has mean \(β\). Assuming \(α\neq0\) and the non-Alfvénic condition \(|α|\neq|β|\), we construct solutions converging on compact interior subdisks to a rigidly rotating ideal MHD core with constant vorticity and out-of-plane current density. A new MHD--Wood law determines the two core rotations from the boundary data: the velocity core is selected by a coupled kinetic--magnetic balance, whereas the magnetic core is fixed by the imposed mean circulation. Consequently, zero circulation gives complete interior magnetic expulsion, while nonzero circulation leaves a uniform magnetic rotation after the nonaxisymmetric modes are confined to a thin boundary layer. This provides a fully coupled realization of Prandtl--Batchelor selection and flux expulsion; unlike classical kinematic models, the magnetic field actively changes the flow and need not be weak. The proof combines a non-Alfvénic coercive theory for a periodic MHD boundary layer, global matching of the two fields, and a coupled stability estimate adapted to the magnetic boundary condition. A separate conditional rigidity argument, using exact viscous identities and local convergence but no interior asymptotic expansion, explains the same core structure for a broader single-eddy family.

</details>


### [109] [Exponential growth and decay in the ideal induction equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05997)
*Víctor Navarro-Fernández*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper constructs a time-periodic, divergence-free velocity field on the 3D torus made of alternating piecewise-affine shears. For large shear amplitude, all non-zero divergence-free L^p initial fields grow exponentially under the ideal induction equation. The proof uses a uniform cone condition and a bunching inequality to rule out fields in the stable bundle. For the time-reversed flow, bounded divergence-free fields exist in the 2D stable bundle and decay exponentially in L^p.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand transport and mixing mechanisms for the induction equation (magnetic field evolution) in ideal fluids, and to demonstrate that fast dynamo behavior (exponential growth) can be achieved by relatively simple, explicitly constructible flows—specifically piecewise-affine shears—without requiring complicated chaotic flows.

Method: Constructs a 3D time-periodic velocity field on the torus formed by three alternating piecewise-affine shear flows. The analysis shows a uniform cone condition holds for the time-one map of the flow, combined with a bunching inequality to exclude divergence-free fields that lie in the stable bundle almost everywhere. For the reversed velocity, the (inverse) time-one map admits nontrivial bounded divergence-free fields in its 2D stable bundle, and exponential decay in L^p is established.

Result: For sufficiently large shear amplitude: (1) every non-zero divergence-free L^p initial field grows exponentially under the ideal induction equation; (2) for the time-reversed flow, nontrivial bounded divergence-free initial configurations exist in the 2D stable bundle, and the corresponding solution decays exponentially in every L^p.

Conclusion: The constructed flow provides a rigorous example showing that the ideal induction equation can exhibit exponential growth for all non-zero divergence-free fields via a simple piecewise-affine shear construction, with the reverse flow demonstrating the symmetric decay scenario. This establishes sharp contrast between forward and backward time dynamics.

Abstract: We construct a divergence-free velocity field on the three-dimensional torus that is time-periodic and consists of three alternating piecewise-affine shears. For sufficiently large shear amplitude, every non-zero divergence-free initial field in $L^p$ grows exponentially under the ideal induction equation. The proof establishes a uniform cone condition for the time-one map, and combines it with a bunching inequality to rule out nontrivial divergence-free fields lying almost everywhere in the stable bundle. Additionally, we show that for the time-reversed velocity, whose time-one map is the inverse of the original one, there exist nontrivial, bounded, divergence-free initial configurations taking values almost everywhere in its two-dimensional stable bundle. The corresponding solution decays exponentially in every $L^p$.

</details>


### [110] [New decay estimates and Liouville type theorems for the 3D axisymmetric stationary Navier-Stokes equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06040)
*Wendong Wang,Guoxu Yang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper addresses the open Liouville problem for 3D stationary Navier-Stokes equations, improving decay estimates for axisymmetric D-solutions using a new pointwise Calderón-Zygmund estimate adapted to cylindrical geometry, and establishing new Liouville theorems without symmetry assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Liouville problem for 3D stationary Navier-Stokes equations is a longstanding open question, even restricted to axisymmetric D-solutions. The authors aim to improve existing decay estimates and establish new Liouville-type criteria.

Method: The paper uses two approaches: (i) a new pointwise Calderón-Zygmund estimate adapted to cylindrical geometry to improve decay estimates in the radial variable r, and (ii) a new approach to Liouville theorems that improves earlier axisymmetric criteria while removing symmetry assumptions.

Result: The paper proves improved decay rates for the gradient of velocity components (|∇u_r|+|∇u_z| ≲ r^{-5/4}[log(e+r)]^{5/4}) and vorticity components (|ω_r|+|ω_z| ≲ r^{-9/8}[log(e+r)]^{9/8}) for r≫1. Furthermore, it shows D-solutions are trivial if either the velocity or vorticity satisfies the stated decay bounds on cylinders.

Conclusion: The authors develop new tools—a cylindrical Calderón-Zygmund estimate and a fresh Liouville theorem approach—that yield sharper decay estimates and extend Liouville criteria beyond the axisymmetric setting, requiring only r^{-2/3} (velocity) or r^{-5/3} (vorticity) decay with logarithmic corrections.

Abstract: The Liouville problem for the three-dimensional stationary Navier--Stokes equations remains open, even for axisymmetric \(D\)-solutions. In this paper, we obtain two results based on decay in the cylindrical radial variable \(r=|x'|\).
  (i). Using a new pointwise Calderón--Zygmund estimate adapted to cylindrical geometry, we improve the decay estimates of Carrillo--Pan--Zhang (2020, JFA) and prove
  \[
  |\nabla u_r|+|\nabla u_z|
  \lesssim r^{-5/4}[\log(\mathrm e+r)]^{5/4},
  \quad
  |ω_r|+|ω_z|
  \lesssim r^{-9/8}[\log(\mathrm e+r)]^{9/8}, \quad r\gg1.
  \]
  (ii). We develop a new approach to Liouville theorems that improves the axisymmetric criteria of Wang (2019, JDE) and Zhao (2019, Nonlinear Anal.). Without any symmetry assumption, we show that a \(D\)-solution is trivial if one of the following holds:
  \[ (\mathrm a).\,\sup_{|x'|=r,\, z\in\mathbb R}
  |u(x',z)|
  \leq
  Cr^{-2/3}[\log(\mathrm e+r)]^{-γ}; \quad (\mathrm b).\,
  \sup_{|x'|=r,\, z\in\mathbb R}
  |ω(x',z)|
  \leq
  Cr^{-5/3}[\log(\mathrm e+r)]^{-γ},
  \]
  for $r\geq1$, where $γ>1/3$.

</details>


### [111] [On advective nonlocal operators: multiplicity of principal eigenpairs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06095)
*Arnaud Ducrot,Quentin Griette,Xing Liang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the existence and multiplicity of principal eigenvalues and eigenfunctions for a periodically heterogeneous nonlocal dispersal model with advection. Since the operator is resolvent-positive but not resolvent-compact, classical Krein-Rutman theory fails. The authors show uniqueness of the principal eigenvalue when advection has constant sign, but surprisingly rich behavior (unique solution, continuum of solutions, or singular measure eigenvectors) when advection changes sign. They fully characterize eigenvalues with positive (possibly measure-valued) eigenvectors and apply results to a KPP-type equation exhibiting a continuum of nontrivial stationary solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand principal eigenvalues/eigenfunctions for nonlocal dispersal operators with advection in heterogeneous periodic media, where non-compactness prevents direct application of standard spectral theory, and to reveal the qualitatively different spectral and dynamical behavior compared to classical local diffusion models.

Method: The analysis handles a resolvent-positive but non-resolvent-compact operator, distinguishing between constant-sign advection (where uniqueness of the principal eigenvalue holds) and sign-changing advection (yielding multiple regimes including continua of solutions and measure-valued eigenvectors). Spectral characterization is performed for all eigenvalues with positive eigenvectors, including Radon measure eigenvectors.

Result: For constant-sign advection, existence and uniqueness of the principal eigenvalue and normalized eigenfunction are established. For sign-changing advection, depending on coefficients, the principal eigenproblem exhibits either a unique normalized solution or a continuum of solutions with a boundary principal eigenvector carrying a singular measure component; all such eigenvalues lie in the continuous spectrum. Complete characterization of eigenvalues with positive (possibly measure-valued) eigenvectors is achieved.

Conclusion: The spectral structure of the nonlocal dispersal operator with advection is fundamentally richer than in local diffusion cases, particularly when advection changes sign. This leads to a continuum of nontrivial stationary solutions for the associated KPP-type equation, a behavior absent in classical KPP models with local diffusion.

Abstract: We study the existence and multiplicity of principal eigenvalues and eigenfunctions for a periodically heterogeneous nonlocal dispersal model with advection. The operator we consider is resolvent-positive but not resolvent-compact; therefore, the classical Krein-Rutman theory cannot be applied directly. When the advection coefficient has a constant sign, we prove the existence and uniqueness of the principal eigenvalue and the corresponding normalized eigenfunction. In sharp contrast, when the advection does not have a constant sign, the problem is more involved and leads to surprising results. Depending on the coefficients of the equation, the principal eigenproblem can either have a unique normalized solution or a continuum of solutions, at the boundary of which there exists a principal eigenvector with a singular measure component. In the latter situation, all the constructed eigenvalues are embedded in the continuous spectrum of our operator. We completely characterize the eigenvalues associated with positive eigenvectors, even when the eigenvector is a Radon measure. Finally, we discuss an application to a nonlinear KPP-type equation with nonlocal dispersal, which possesses a continuum of nontrivial stationary solutions, a different behavior from the classical KPP equation with local diffusion.

</details>


### [112] [Vanishing viscosity limit to two interacting shocks from the same family for the compressible Navier-Stokes equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06173)
*Lin-An Li,Dehua Wang,Yi Wang*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves the vanishing viscosity limit for 1D compressible Navier-Stokes equations in the challenging regime of two interacting same-family shock waves. Using the anti-derivative method before collision and a weighted relative entropy method with time-dependent shifts after collision, the authors establish global smooth solutions that converge to the entropy solution of the Euler equations with an explicit rate for small wave strengths and viscosity coefficients.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The interaction of two same-family shock waves is fundamentally different from distinct-family shock collisions (which produce two outgoing shocks). Same-family collisions generate an outgoing shock plus a rarefaction wave, occur on a larger time scale (inversely proportional to wave strength), and introduce singularities at the collision point. These features make the vanishing viscosity limit significantly harder to justify, especially because uniform estimates with respect to viscosity cannot be closed up to the collision time.

Method: (1) Before collision: apply the anti-derivative method to precisely fix the locations of both viscous shocks up to the collision point. (2) Since uniform estimates cannot be closed up to the collision time, introduce a carefully constructed approximate collision time to obtain higher-order energy bounds. (3) After collision: apply a weighted relative entropy method combined with time-dependent shifts to handle the composite wave structure (coexisting shock and rarefaction waves).

Result: For suitably small wave strengths and viscosity coefficients, the authors construct a family of global smooth solutions to the Navier-Stokes equations that converge to the entropy solution of the Euler equations with an explicit convergence rate.

Conclusion: The two-phase strategy (anti-derivative method before collision, weighted relative entropy after) successfully handles the more singular same-family shock interaction regime. The techniques are expected to be applicable to other related problems in vanishing viscosity theory.

Abstract: We investigate the vanishing viscosity limit for the one-dimensional compressible Navier-Stokes equations in the regime of two interacting shock waves from the same characteristic family of the underlying Euler equations. Unlike the interaction of two shocks from distinct families, which produces two outgoing shocks in their respective original families, the collision of two same-family shocks generates an outgoing shock in the same family together with a rarefaction wave in the other family. This configuration is more singular because the collision occurs on a larger time scale that depends inversely on the wave strength, making it challenging to justify the vanishing viscosity limit not only in the processes before and after the shock collision but, more crucially, at the collision point. Furthermore, the emergence of both shock and rarefaction waves after the collision introduces an additional difficulty. To overcome these obstacles, we first employ the anti-derivative method before the collision, which allows us to fix the locations of both viscous shocks precisely up to the collision point. However, uniform estimates with respect to the viscosity cannot be closed up to the collision time; we therefore introduce a carefully constructed approximate collision time to obtain the required higher-order energy bounds. After the collision, we apply a weighted relative entropy method, combined with time-dependent shifts, to handle the composite wave structure arising from the coexistence of shock and rarefaction waves. Our main result establishes that, for suitably small wave strengths and viscosity coefficients, there exists a family of global smooth solutions to the Navier-Stokes equations that converge to the entropy solution of the Euler equations with an explicit convergence rate. The techniques are expected to be applicable to other related problems in vanishing viscosity theory.

</details>


### [113] [The energy of fractional Allen--Cahn layers in dimension one](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06226)
*Alvaro Carballeira,Damien Galant,Javier Gómez-Serrano*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the energy functional of the one-dimensional fractional Allen-Cahn layer solution for s∈(1/2,1], proving it is continuous and strictly decreasing, with explicit asymptotic expansions at both endpoints: E(s) = 2√2/3 + κ₁(1-s) + o(1-s) near s=1 and E(s) = 1/(π(s-1/2)) + O(1) near s=1/2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a sharp qualitative and quantitative description of how the energy of the fractional Allen-Cahn layer solution depends on the fractional order parameter s, filling a gap in understanding of this fundamental quantity across the interval s∈(1/2,1].

Method: The proof combines the Cabré-Sire construction of the layer solution, the Palatucci-Savin-Valdinoci minimality theorem, an identity for the s-derivative of the energy, and a computer-assisted coercivity estimate at explicit approximate layers. The strict decrease of the energy is verified using computer assistance: on an interior subinterval it is reduced to finitely many inequalities checked by interval-arithmetic computations.

Result: The paper proves the energy is continuous and strictly decreasing on s∈(1/2,1]. Explicit asymptotic expansions are obtained at both endpoints: at s=1 the energy has the form 2√2/3 + κ₁(1-s) + o(1-s) with an explicit formula for κ₁, and at s=1/2 it diverges as 1/(π(s-1/2)) + O(1).

Conclusion: The energy of the fractional Allen-Cahn layer is strictly decreasing in s, transitioning from a finite value at s=1 (classical case) to diverging to infinity as s approaches 1/2, with the rigorous proof requiring a combination of analytical techniques and computer-assisted verification.

Abstract: We study the energy $\mathcal{E}(s) := E_{s}[Φ_s]$ of the one-dimensional fractional Allen--Cahn layer solution $Φ_s$, defined as the unique odd, increasing solution of $(-Δ)^{s} Φ_s = Φ_s - Φ_s^{3}$ with $Φ_s(\pm\infty)=\pm 1$ and $Φ_s(0)=0$, for $s\in(1/2,1]$. Our main results are a sharp qualitative and quantitative description of the energy $\mathcal{E}$ on this interval. We show that the energy is continuous and strictly decreasing, and we obtain explicit asymptotic expansions at both endpoints. At the upper endpoint we prove $\mathcal{E}(s) = \frac{2\sqrt{2}}{3} + κ_1 (1-s) + o(1-s)$ with an explicit formula for $κ_1$. At the lower endpoint we prove that the energy goes to infinity as $\mathcal{E}(s)=\frac{1}{π(s-1/2)}+O(1)$. The strict decrease is proved with computer assistance. On an interior subinterval it is reduced to finitely many inequalities verified by interval-arithmetic computations. The proofs combine the Cabré--Sire construction of the layer, the minimality theorem of Palatucci--Savin--Valdinoci, an identity for the $s$ derivative of the energy, and a computer-assisted coercivity estimate at explicit approximate layers.

</details>


### [114] [Well-posedness of stochastic time-nonlocal telegraph equations with Hölder diffusion coefficient: hereditary phase-space lifting and novel generalized coupling method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06237)
*Xi Huang,Li Peng,Juan Carlos Pozo,Yong Zhou*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper establishes the first weak existence and uniqueness results in law for mild solutions to stochastic time-nonlocal telegraph equations with (PC_ε)-type kernels under multiplicative Gaussian white noise, using a new hereditary phase-space lifting framework and a generalized coupling framework with novel damping control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The stochastic time-nonlocal telegraph equation models high-frequency signal propagation in small-scale systems under stochastic fluctuations, but no well-posedness results existed for this class of equations. The nonlocal kernel structure and stochastic forcing create significant analytical challenges that require new mathematical frameworks.

Method: Two novel analytical tools are developed: (1) a hereditary phase-space lifting framework specifically designed for time-nonlocal telegraph equations, and (2) a generalized coupling framework featuring a new construction of the damping control term for velocity. These tools enable handling of the nonlocal convolution structure with the (PC_ε)-type kernel.

Result: Weak existence and uniqueness in law for mild solutions valued in L²_loc(R₊; H^δ) are proven, where δ can be arbitrarily close to min{1/2, ε/(2+ε)} from below. The admissible Hölder exponent κ lower bound is quantitatively tied to the integrability exponent ε. For the damped wave case (Dirac kernel), κ improves to any value in (3/5, 1]. The framework also handles low-regularity nonlinearities depending on both displacement and velocity.

Conclusion: This work provides the first well-posedness theory for stochastic time-nonlocal telegraph equations, with explicit regularity thresholds and Hölder exponent constraints determined by kernel integrability, and the framework generalizes to handle nonlinearities with low regularity in both displacement and velocity.

Abstract: We consider the initial-boundary value problem for the stochastic time-nonlocal telegraph equation with $(\mathcal{PC}_\varepsilon)$-type kernel $a$:
  \begin{align*}
  γ\partial_t \left( a \ast \partial_t (a \ast v)\right) =Δv-\partial_t (a \ast v)+ Ψ(v)+ Φ(v) \frac{\mathrm{d}W(t)}{\mathrm{d}t},
  \end{align*}
  where $W$ is a space-time Gaussian white noise, $Ψ$ satisfies a linear growth condition, and $Φ$ is Hölder continuous and uniformly nondegenerate. This model characterizes high-frequency signal propagation in small-scale systems under stochastic fluctuations. We develop a new hereditary phase-space lifting framework for time-nonlocal telegraph equations. In addition, we propose a novel generalized coupling framework, which features a new construction of the damping control term for the velocity. Based on these analytic tools, we prove the first results on weak existence and uniqueness in law for mild solutions, valued in $L_{loc}^2(\mathbb R_+; H^δ)$, to the stochastic nonlocal telegraph equation. The regularity index $δ$ can be arbitrarily close to $\min\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\varepsilon}{2+\varepsilon} \}$ from below, and the admissible lower bound of the Hölder exponent $κ$ is quantitatively determined by the integrability exponent $\varepsilon$. For the corresponding IBVP of the stochastic damped wave equation, obtained by replacing $a$ with the Dirac measure $δ_0$, $κ$ can be improved to any value in $(\frac{3}{5}, 1]$. More significantly, the generalized coupling framework also handles low-regularity nonlinearities depending on both displacement and velocity.

</details>


### [115] [Nonconvex Sublevel Sets For The Planar Translating Mean Curvature Equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06293)
*Jiahuan Li,Yilu Liu,Xi-Nan Ma,Guohuan Qiu*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The authors construct a smooth, bounded, uniformly convex domain in R² where the solution to the planar translating mean curvature equation (with zero Dirichlet boundary conditions) has a nonconvex sublevel set, demonstrating that convexity of the domain does not guarantee convexity of the solution's level sets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Translating solitons model type II singularities of mean-convex mean curvature flow. Understanding the geometric properties of their solutions—particularly whether convexity of the domain propagates to convexity of sublevel sets—is a fundamental question in this area.

Method: The construction combines two key ingredients: (1) a corrected near-critical grim-reaper profile, which serves as a local model near the boundary, and (2) explicit barrier functions defined on a long convex channel to control the solution globally.

Result: The authors successfully construct a smooth bounded uniformly convex domain in R² whose translating mean curvature solution has a nonconvex sublevel set, providing a counterexample to potential convexity preservation.

Conclusion: Convexity of the domain alone is insufficient to guarantee convexity of sublevel sets for the zero-Dirichlet solution of the planar translating mean curvature equation.

Abstract: Translating solitons arise as models for type~II singularities of mean-convex mean curvature flow. We construct a smooth bounded uniformly convex domain \(\Om\Subset\R^2\) such that the zero-Dirichlet solution of the planar translating mean curvature equation has a nonconvex sublevel set. The construction is based on a corrected near-critical grim-reaper profile and explicit barriers on a long convex channel.

</details>


### [116] [Sub-critical well-posedness for the intermediate nonlinear Schrödinger equation on the line](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06298)
*Andreia Chapouto,Justin Forlano,Thierry Laurens*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper studies the well-posedness theory for the intermediate nonlinear Schrödinger equation (INLS), proving local well-posedness in H^s for any s>0 (covering the full scaling-subcritical range) and global well-posedness for integrable models with 0<s<1/2 for small L^2-norm initial data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors aim to improve upon their previous local well-posedness result for INLS (previously only s>1/4) by extending it to the full scaling-subcritical range (any s>0). They also seek to establish global well-posedness results for integrable models, expanding the theory for these equations.

Method: The approach uses a gauge transformation, derivation of a closed system for four auxiliary variables, and nonlinear smoothing estimates, notably NOT relying on the completely integrable nature of the equations. For global results, the argument relies on a novel family of conserved quantities derived from a Lax pair discovered in prior work.

Result: Local well-posedness is proven for any s>0, covering the full scaling-subcritical range, including the continuum Calogero-Moser equation without chirality assumption. Global well-posedness is established for 0<s<1/2 for initial data with small L^2-norm in integrable models, with applicability whenever L^2-equicontinuous sets are preserved by the flow.

Conclusion: The paper significantly advances the well-posedness theory for INLS by extending local well-posedness to the full scaling-subcritical range and establishing global well-posedness for integrable cases. The small-data restriction in the global result could be removed by an a-priori equicontinuity result.

Abstract: We continue our study of the well-posedness theory for the intermediate nonlinear Schrödinger equation (INLS). Firstly, we prove that INLS is locally well-posed in $H^s (\mathbb{R})$ for any $s>0$. This improves on our previous result of local well-posedness for any $s>\frac 14$, and covers the full scaling-subcritical range for INLS. In particular, we also obtain the local well-posedness for the continuum Calogero-Moser equation without chirality assumption in the full scaling-subcritical range. Our method relies on a gauge transformation, the derivation of a closed system for four auxiliary variables, and nonlinear smoothing estimates, but not on the completely integrable nature of these equations.
  Secondly, for the integrable models, we prove global well-posedness for $0<s<\frac12$ for initial data with small $L^2$-norm. Moreover, we show that our global well-posedness result applies whenever $L^2$-equicontinuous sets are preserved by the flow, and so the small-data restriction would be removed by an a-priori equicontinuity result. Our argument relies on a novel family of conserved quantities based upon the Lax pair we discovered in our prior work.

</details>


### [117] [A General Aubry-Mather Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06344)
*Nassif Ghoussoub*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper presents the front matter of a monograph that develops a general Aubry-Mather theory for a class of non-linear operators called Kantorovich operators, using skew-linear entropies (a form of optimal mass transport) as a substitute for duality, thereby extending ergodic theory and Aubry-Mather theory beyond Hamiltonian dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Kantorovich operators are ubiquitous across analysis, probability, dynamical systems, mathematical economics, and finance, yet they lack adjoints and their ergodic theory is underdeveloped compared to Markov operators. The author aims to extend classical ergodic theory and Aubry-Mather theory to this broad class of operators.

Method: The monograph replaces operator-measure duality (impossible due to lack of adjoint) with a convex functional on pairs of probability distributions called a skew-linear entropy, which generalizes optimal mass transport. Ergodic theory is then developed around this structure, yielding minimal measures, a Mather constant, weak KAM solutions, and an Aubry set for arbitrary Kantorovich operators.

Result: The result is a comprehensive ergodic theory for Kantorovich operators that generalizes classical theories involving Markov operators, free-energy transfers, and the Hopf-Lax-Oleinik semi-group, extending Aubry-Mather theory well beyond its Hamiltonian origin.

Conclusion: A general Aubry-Mather theory can be constructed for Kantorovich operators using skew-linear entropies, generalizing substantial portions of classical ergodic theory and dynamical systems theory to a broad class of non-linear operators.

Abstract: This paper reproduces the front matter --- preface, overview and table of contents --- of a monograph by the author, submitted for publication under the title {\it Skew Linear Entropies and Kantorovich Operators: A General Aubry-Mather Theory}. The book isolates a class of non-linear operators, which we call {\it Kantorovich operators}, that are ubiquitous in analysis, probability, dynamical systems, mathematical economics and finance. We develop aspects of their ergodic theory in a way that extends classical ones involving Markov operators, free-energy transfers, or the Hopf--Lax--Oleinik semi-group. Having no adjoint, the duality between such an operator and measures is carried instead via a convex functional on {\it pairs} of probability distributions --- a source and a target --- which we call a {\it skew-linear entropy}, and which is a general form of optimal mass transport. The extensive overview reproduced here describes the resulting ergodic theory, in which minimal measures, a Mather constant, weak KAM solutions and an Aubry set are attached to an arbitrary Kantorovich operator, extending Aubry--Mather theory well beyond its origins in Hamiltonian dynamics.

</details>


### [118] [On generalised d'Alembert-type integral representations for damped wave equations on the quarter-plane](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06355)
*Andreas Chatziafratis,Claudio Giorgi,Alain Miranville,Federico Zullo*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper extends the Fokas unified transform method to hyperbolic-parabolic problems on the semi-infinite interval, rigorously constructing closed-form a posteriori solutions for the forced Maxwell-Cattaneo-Vernotte (damped wave/telegrapher's) equation on the spatiotemporal quarter-plane. The authors prove regularity, boundary, asymptotic, and well-posedness theorems, and reveal compatibility conditions guaranteeing smooth solutions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously construct and verify new closed-form solutions for the forced Maxwell-Cattaneo-Vernotte equation on a semi-infinite domain, extending the Fokas unified transform method beyond its original scope (elliptic PDEs and evolution equations with polynomial dispersion relations) to hyperbolic-parabolic problems—a first-time extension that addresses substantial analytic challenges from the semi-unbounded domain.

Method: The modern complex-analytic unified transform method of Fokas is extended for the first time to hyperbolic-parabolic problems on the semi-infinite interval. The approach involves constructing explicit integral representations of solutions, appropriately interpreting oscillatory integral terms, and proving theorems on regularity, boundary properties, asymptotic behavior, and well-posedness—revealing compatibility conditions between initial, boundary, and forcing data at the origin.

Result: New closed-form analytical solutions are rigorously constructed and verified for the forced Maxwell-Cattaneo-Vernotte equation on the quarter-plane, accompanied by theorems establishing regularity, boundary and asymptotic properties, and well-posedness. Compatibility conditions at the origin are identified that guarantee smooth solutions across the entire domain.

Conclusion: The explicit integral representations are directly useful for numerical benchmarking, for exploring connections with continuum mechanics, mathematical physics, biology, and natural sciences, and for investigating well-posedness of nonlinear counterparts.

Abstract: We rigorously construct and verify a posteriori new closed-form solutions for the forced Maxwell-Cattaneo-Vernotte equation (also broadly known as the damped wave equation, hyperbolic heat, and telegrapher's equation on lossy transmission lines) posed on the spatiotemporal quarter-plane with general initial and boundary data in classical function spaces. For this purpose, the modern complex-analytic unified transform method of Fokas (originally developed for elliptic PDE and evolution equations with polynomial dispersion relations) is here, for the first time, extended for analysis of hyperbolic-parabolic problems on the semi-infinite interval. Importantly, we then establish theorems which pertain to regularity, boundary and asymptotic properties of the new analytical formulae as well as to well-posedness of the addressed boundary-value problems. Notably, the nature and generality of problems considered, combined with the semi-unboundedness of the domain, induce substantial analytic challenges which demand delicate treatment, both in appropriately interpreting oscillatory integral terms of the solution formulae and in proving the proposed results. In this process, crucially, certain compatibility conditions, between initial, boundary and forcing data at the origin, are revealed, which guarantee the existence of a smooth solution across the whole domain of interest. Our explicit integral representations are of direct utility for numercal benchmarking purposes, for exploring connections with modelling in continuum mechanics, mathematical physics, biology and the natural sciences, and for the investigation of well-posedness for nonlinear counterparts too.

</details>


### [119] [Quasiconvexity for the Dacorogna--Marcellini Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06367)
*Giuseppe Bruno,Federico Pasqualotto*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: The paper proves that the planar Dacorogna–Marcellini energy is quasiconvex if and only if it is rank-one convex (i.e., |γ| ≤ 2/√3), using a monotonicity property along the componentwise heat flow. This establishes quasiconvexity-rank-one convexity equivalence for a class of left/right rotation-invariant quartic polynomials on 2×2 matrices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: A longstanding open question in the calculus of variations is whether quasiconvexity and rank-one convexity are equivalent for nonconvex energies. The Dacorogna–Marcellini energy is a canonical example where this equivalence was conjectured but unproven, and resolving it provides insight into a broader class of rotation-invariant polynomial energies.

Method: The proof exploits a monotonicity property of the energy functional along the componentwise heat flow. By tracking how the energy behaves under this flow, the authors derive a sharp condition—|γ| ≤ 2/√3—under which quasiconvexity holds, matching the known rank-one convexity threshold.

Result: The paper proves that the Dacorogna–Marcellini energy is quasiconvex exactly when |γ| ≤ 2/√3, coinciding with its rank-one convexity range. As a corollary, it establishes that for homogeneous quartic polynomials on 2×2 matrices invariant under left and right rotations, quasiconvexity is equivalent to rank-one convexity.

Conclusion: The result resolves the quasiconvexity question for the Dacorogna–Marcellini energy and extends the equivalence to a general class of rotation-invariant quartic polynomials, advancing the understanding of when quasiconvexity and rank-one convexity coincide.

Abstract: We prove that the planar Dacorogna--Marcellini energy $f_γ(A)=|A|^4-2γ|A|^2\det A$ is quasiconvex exactly when it is rank-one convex, i.e. if and only if $|γ|\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt3}$. The proof uses a monotonicity property of the energy functional along the componentwise heat flow. As a corollary of our method, we show that for homogeneous quartic polynomials on $2 \times 2$ matrices invariant by left and right rotation, quasiconvexity is equivalent to rank one convexity.

</details>


### [120] [Global weak solutions to the Cahn-Hilliard equation with degenerate mobility and singular diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06371)
*Monica Conti,Andrea Giorgini,Greta Ricchi*

Main category: math.AP

TL;DR: This paper proves the existence of global weak solutions for the Cahn-Hilliard equation with degenerate mobility and singular diffusion in 3D convex domains, deriving novel L⁴(0,T;H²(Ω)) estimates for both the solution u and φ(u)=arcsin(u) without using the classical entropy-based approach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Cahn-Hilliard equation with degenerate mobility and singular diffusion models phase separation in polymer blends described by Flory-Huggins-de Gennes free energy. Establishing existence of global weak solutions in 3D is a mathematically challenging open problem, particularly when the classical entropy-based techniques are insufficient or inapplicable.

Method: The authors analyze the initial-boundary value problem for the Cahn-Hilliard equation in three-dimensional bounded, smooth, convex domains. They develop a new analytical framework that avoids the classical entropy-based approach and instead derives direct L⁴(0,T;H²(Ω)) estimates for both the solution u and the transformed function φ(u)=arcsin(u), which is used to handle the singular diffusion at pure phases.

Result: The paper establishes the existence of suitable global weak solutions, assuming only that the initial datum has finite energy. The key technical achievement is obtaining L⁴(0,T;H²(Ω)) regularity estimates for both u and φ(u) via a novel method that does not rely on entropy-based arguments.

Conclusion: The study provides a new proof of global weak solution existence for this degenerate/singular Cahn-Hilliard model in 3D, overcoming limitations of classical entropy-based methods and opening new avenues for analyzing such complex phase-field models.

Abstract: We study the initial-boundary value problem for the Cahn-Hilliard equation with degenerate mobility and singular diffusion at pure phases. This model describes the dynamics of phase separation in polymer blends with associated Flory-Huggins-de Gennes free energy. We prove the existence of suitable global weak solutions in three-dimensional bounded, smooth, and convex domains, assuming that the initial datum has finite energy. A key novelty of our analysis is the derivation of $L^4(0,T; H^2(Ω))$ estimates for both the solution $u$ and the function $φ(u)=\arcsin(u)$, obtained without relying on the classical entropy-based approach.

</details>

### [121] [From populations to absolute binding affinities in molecular simulations: exact volumetric terms and practical estimators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04834)
*Davide Mandelli,Emiliano Ippoliti,Charles Plate*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a statistical-mechanics framework for computing equilibrium binding constants K in the dilute limit, deriving a general expression relating K to relative populations of bound and unbound states. The framework makes the volumetric term explicit, accounts for volume restraints exactly, and is applied to two host-guest/ligand-protein complexes, showing that common single-bin estimators deviate by ~1 kcal/mol.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a principled, first-principles framework for computing absolute binding constants from molecular simulations, making the volumetric contribution explicit and resolving ambiguities in defining bound states.

Method: Derives a general statistical-mechanics expression relating K to relative populations of bound/unbound states, enabling exact accounting of volume restraints under the assumption of a homogeneous unbound state. Estimators are computed from histograms of reaction coordinates with well-defined state boundaries.

Result: Applied to cucurbit[7]uril/1-adamantanol and galactonate-DgoT complexes; shows commonly used single-bin estimators deviate by ~1 kcal/mol from the theoretically correct value in both systems.

Conclusion: By anchoring the bound-state definition to experimentally resolvable states, the theory converts a hidden assumption into a controlled input, providing a principled route to absolute binding affinities from molecular simulations.

Abstract: We present a statistical-mechanics framework for computing equilibrium binding constants $K$ in the dilute limit. From first principles, we derive a general expression relating $K$ to the relative populations of the bound and unbound states. Its transparency has twofold advantage: it makes the origin of the unbound-state volumetric term explicit, and it allows one to track exactly how an imposed volume restraint propagates through the expression. This makes $K$ directly computable, as restrained simulations can account for the volumetric contribution exactly, under the physically mild assumption of a homogeneous unbound state. The resulting estimators are computable from histograms of any suitably defined reaction coordinate, and determine unambiguously how the boundaries of the thermodynamic states of interest must be defined. We apply our framework to the cucurbit[7]uril/1-adamantanol host--guest complex and the galactonate--DgoT ligand--protein complex. Our results show that commonly used single-bin estimators depart from the theoretically correct one by $\approx 1$~kcal/mol in both systems. This shift originates in the definition of the bound state: by anchoring that definition to what state-of-the-art experiments resolve, the theory turns it from a hidden assumption into a controlled input, and provides a principled route to absolute binding affinities from molecular simulations.

</details>


### [122] [From populations to absolute binding affinities in molecular simulations: exact volumetric terms and practical estimators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04834)
*Davide Mandelli,Emiliano Ippoliti,Charles Plate*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: The paper presents a statistical-mechanics framework for computing equilibrium binding constants in the dilute limit, deriving a general expression relating K to relative populations of bound and unbound states. The framework makes the volumetric term explicit and allows exact accounting for volume restraints, with estimators computable from reaction-coordinate histograms. Applied to host-guest and ligand-protein complexes, it shows common single-bin estimators deviate by ~1 kcal/mol and provides a principled route to absolute binding affinities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguities in computing binding constants from molecular simulations, particularly the hidden assumptions in defining bound states and the treatment of volumetric terms in the unbound state that lead to systematic errors in standard estimators.

Method: Derives a general expression for K from first principles relating it to relative populations of bound and unbound states, making the volumetric contribution explicit. Uses restrained simulations to account for the volumetric term exactly under the assumption of a homogeneous unbound state, with estimators computed from histograms of reaction coordinates.

Result: Applied to cucurbit[7]uril/1-adamantanol and galactonate-DgoT complexes, showing commonly used single-bin estimators deviate by approximately 1 kcal/mol from the theoretically correct value in both systems.

Conclusion: By anchoring the bound-state definition to what experiments resolve, the theory transforms hidden assumptions into controlled inputs, providing a principled route to absolute binding affinities from molecular simulations.

Abstract: We present a statistical-mechanics framework for computing equilibrium binding constants $K$ in the dilute limit. From first principles, we derive a general expression relating $K$ to the relative populations of the bound and unbound states. Its transparency has twofold advantage: it makes the origin of the unbound-state volumetric term explicit, and it allows one to track exactly how an imposed volume restraint propagates through the expression. This makes $K$ directly computable, as restrained simulations can account for the volumetric contribution exactly, under the physically mild assumption of a homogeneous unbound state. The resulting estimators are computable from histograms of any suitably defined reaction coordinate, and determine unambiguously how the boundaries of the thermodynamic states of interest must be defined. We apply our framework to the cucurbit[7]uril/1-adamantanol host--guest complex and the galactonate--DgoT ligand--protein complex. Our results show that commonly used single-bin estimators depart from the theoretically correct one by $\approx 1$~kcal/mol in both systems. This shift originates in the definition of the bound state: by anchoring that definition to what state-of-the-art experiments resolve, the theory turns it from a hidden assumption into a controlled input, and provides a principled route to absolute binding affinities from molecular simulations.

</details>


### [123] [Multiphysics tritium transport modelling of the ARC breeding blanket with FESTIM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05398)
*James Dark,Arpan Sircar,Jin Whan Bae,Jørgen S. Dokken,Rémi Delaporte-Mathurin*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents a fully open-source, component-scale multiphysics framework for modelling tritium transport in an ARC liquid immersion blanket, coupling neutron transport (OpenMC), thermal hydraulics (OpenFOAM), and hydrogen isotope transport (FESTIM). Steady-state simulations predict ~243 mg tritium inventory with ~30 min steady-state throughput, showing turbulence-enhanced diffusion dominates transport with localisation in stagnation regions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Accurate prediction of tritium behaviour in molten salt breeding blankets is essential for the design and safe operation of ARC-class fusion reactors, yet previous analyses lacked high-fidelity spatial resolution at the component scale.

Method: A fully open-source multiphysics framework coupling OpenMC (neutron transport), OpenFOAM (thermal hydraulics), and FESTIM (hydrogen isotope transport), with dedicated tools enabling direct transfer of spatially resolved fields between solvers. Steady-state simulations assume zero inlet concentration.

Result: Total tritium inventory of ~243 mg with the blanket reaching steady-state tritium throughput in ~30 min (similar order to previous system-level estimates). Tritium transport is dominated by turbulence-enhanced diffusion, with strong localisation in flow stagnation regions. Sensitivity analyses show inventories are governed primarily by the numerical stabilisation scheme, with modest dependence on the turbulent Schmidt number.

Conclusion: The proposed workflow provides a transparent and extensible basis for high-fidelity analysis of tritium transport in ARC-class breeding blankets.

Abstract: Accurate prediction of tritium behaviour in molten salt breeding blankets is essential for the design and safe operation of ARC-class fusion reactors. This work presents a fully open-source, component-scale multiphysics framework for modelling tritium transport in an ARC liquid immersion blanket. Neutron transport, thermal hydraulics, and hydrogen isotope transport are coupled using OpenMC, OpenFOAM, and FESTIM, leveraging dedicated tools enabling direct transfer of spatially resolved fields between solvers. Assuming a zero inlet concentration, steady-state simulations predict a total tritium inventory of approximately 243 mg, with the blanket reaching steady-state tritium throughput within approximately 30 min, which is of a similar order to previous system-level estimates. The results show that tritium transport is dominated by turbulence-enhanced diffusion, with strong localisation in flow stagnation regions and reduced accumulation in highly turbulent zones. Sensitivity analyses indicate that predicted inventories are governed primarily by the numerical stabilisation scheme, with only a modest dependence on the turbulent Schmidt number. The proposed workflow provides a transparent and extensible basis for high-fidelity analysis of tritium transport in ARC-class breeding blankets.

</details>


### [124] [Data-driven reconstruction of dynamical systems using Takens' Theorem, manifold learning, and universal function approximators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05477)
*Maximilian Topel,Andrew L. Ferguson*

Main category: physics.comp-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces TAR (TAkens Reconstruction), an algorithmic framework that combines Takens' Delay Embedding Theorem, manifold learning, and universal function approximators to reconstruct full-dimensional dynamical systems from low-dimensional time series observations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While embedding theorems provide theoretical guarantees about the relationship between low-dimensional observations and full-dimensional system states, they do not offer practical guidance on choosing observables, constructing embeddings, or learning the mapping back to the full state. This gap prevents their broad application.

Method: The TAR framework integrates three components: (1) Takens' Delay Embedding Theorem for theoretical grounding, (2) manifold learning techniques for embedding construction, and (3) universal function approximators to learn the mapping between the embedding space and the full-dimensional state.

Result: TAR successfully reconstructs: (1) predator-prey ecological dynamics across diverse embedding time scales, (2) the protein Villin's molecular dynamics using multiple time delays to handle multiple characteristic time scales, and (3) Vanguard S&P 500 trade records, revealing underlying dynamical phenomenologies with accurate short-horizon return predictions without full-dimensional market data.

Conclusion: The TAR framework provides a practical, validated methodology for reconstructing arbitrary dynamical systems from low-dimensional time series, with an open-source software package released to enable broad application.

Abstract: Embedding theorems can be used to provide theoretical guarantees about the relation between low-dimensional observations of a system and its full-dimensional state and dynamics. Such theorems do not, however, provide guidance on observable choice, embedding construction, or methodologies to learn the mapping between the embedding and full-dimensional state. In this work, we develop an algorithmic framework, TAkens Reconstruction (TAR), to analyze and reconstruct arbitrary dynamical systems from low-dimensional time series using an integration of Takens' Delay Embedding Theorem, manifold learning techniques, and universal function approximators. We validate TAR in applications to a variety of simulated and observed dynamical systems and use it to investigate how delay vector structure impacts reconstruction accuracy. In an ecological system, we show that simple predator-prey dynamics can be reconstructed with observations taken over a wide variety of embedding time scales. In molecular dynamics simulations of the protein Villin, we demonstrate how including multiple time delays of the same observable series can be used to improve reconstruction of systems with multiple characteristic time scales. In the trade record of Vanguard S&P 500, we show how the approach exposes underlying dynamical phenomenologies in the data and accurate return predictions over short time horizons without access to full-dimensional market observations. We develop and release an open-source software package to enable the application of TAR to arbitrary dynamical systems.

</details>
