### [125] [Computational Methods and GPU Acceleration in Plasma Physics: A Empirical Analysis of arXiv Publications and Research Trends](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04171)
*Jeremy J. Williams,Anders Brostrom,Stefano Markidis*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: This study analyzes 5,522 computational plasma physics papers (2010-2025) from arXiv using text mining and regression models, finding that MHD papers have longer abstracts than PIC/gyrokinetic papers, abstract length increases modestly with team size, and PIC/gyrokinetic methods have grown in prevalence and are strongly linked to GPU adoption—revealing a decoupling between methodological verbosity and method prevalence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: There is a lack of field-wide bibliometric evidence on how computational method choice, team size, and GPU adoption shape research outputs in computational plasma physics, despite the field's heavy reliance on HPC methods like PIC, gyrokinetic, and MHD simulations.

Method: Large-scale text mining of 5,522 arXiv papers (2010-2025), using abstract length as a proxy for methodological/algorithmic complexity, analyzed via OLS, tobit, and logistic regression models to examine how abstract length and GPU mentions vary with computational method, author count, and publication year, while controlling for collaboration size and temporal trends.

Result: MHD studies have longer abstracts than PIC and gyrokinetic papers; abstract length increases modestly with team size; temporal effects show gradual changes in conciseness; PIC and gyrokinetic methods have grown in prevalence and are strongly linked to GPU adoption, indicating their computational intensity and suitability for accelerator architectures.

Conclusion: The findings reveal a decoupling between methodological verbosity and method prevalence, offering a new bibliometric perspective on the evolution of HPC-driven plasma physics research.

Abstract: Computational plasma physics increasingly relies on high-performance computing (HPC) methods, including particle-in-cell (PIC), gyrokinetic, and magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) simulations, yet field-wide evidence on how method choice, team size, and GPU adoption shape research outputs remains limited. We analyze 5,522 computational plasma physics papers published on arXiv between 2010 and 2025 using large-scale text mining, employing abstract length as a proxy for methodological and algorithmic complexity. Using ordinary least squares (OLS), tobit, and logistic regression models, we examine how abstract length and GPU mentions vary with computational method, number of authors, and publication year. Controlling for collaboration size and temporal trends, results show that MHD studies have longer abstracts than PIC and gyrokinetic papers, indicating more extensive methodological and physical exposition. Abstract length increases modestly with team size, while temporal effects suggest gradual changes in abstract conciseness over time. At the same time, PIC and gyrokinetic methods have grown substantially in relative prevalence over the past decade and are strongly linked to GPU adoption, reflecting their higher computational intensity and suitability for accelerator-based architectures. Together, these findings highlight a decoupling between methodological verbosity and method prevalence, offering a new bibliometric perspective on the evolution of HPC-driven plasma physics research.

</details>


### [126] [Polarization-resolved attosecond gamma-ray emission from few-cycle laser interactions with cone targets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04363)
*De-Sheng Zhang,Cui-Wen Zhang,Xue-Ren Hong,Feng Wan,Jian-Xing Li,Bai-Song Xie*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates the generation of linearly polarized attosecond γ-ray pulses in the MeV range using a cone target irradiated by a single few-cycle laser pulse, achieving 300 as duration with 0.78 linear polarization degree, via QED-PIC simulations that resolve spin and polarization effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generate highly polarized attosecond γ-ray pulses in the MeV range, which are needed for investigating ultrafast nuclear dynamics and polarization-dependent processes in strong-field QED.

Method: Using a cone target irradiated by a single few-cycle laser pulse, where electron layers are periodically extracted and accelerated from cone walls; these electrons interact with counter-propagating reflected attosecond fields via nonlinear Compton scattering. The interaction is modeled with 2D QED-PIC simulations resolving electron spin and photon polarization.

Result: Attosecond γ-ray pulses with 300 as equivalent duration, 0.78 linear polarization degree (overall), photon spectrum extending to 6 MeV, and 0.88 linear polarization degree in the high-energy range. Parameter scans over cone opening angle and laser-plasma coupling reveal tradeoffs among photon number, mean energy, and polarization.

Conclusion: Highly polarized attosecond γ-ray pulses in the MeV range can be generated from cone targets, and these pulses could be used for ultrafast nuclear dynamics studies and polarization-dependent strong-field QED investigations.

Abstract: Linearly polarized attosecond $γ$-ray pulses in the MeV range are generated from a cone target irradiated by a single few-cycle laser pulse. Electron layers are periodically extracted from the cone walls and subsequently accelerated. Their interaction with the counter-propagating reflected attosecond field produces high-energy photons through nonlinear Compton scattering (NCS), forming attosecond $γ$-ray pulses. We model this interaction using two-dimensional quantum electrodynamics particle-in-cell (QED-PIC) simulations that resolve electron spin and photon polarization during emission. The results show a shortest equivalent duration of $300\,\mathrm{as}$, with a corresponding linear polarization degree of 0.78. The photon spectrum extends to $6\,\mathrm{MeV}$, and the linear polarization degree in the high-energy range reaches 0.88. The linear polarization degree remains high when photons from both emission directions are collected over wide momentum-angle ranges. Scans over the cone opening angle and the coupled laser-plasma parameters reveal tradeoffs among photon number, mean photon energy, and polarization. Such highly polarized attosecond $γ$-ray pulses could be used to investigate ultrafast nuclear dynamics and polarization-dependent processes in strong-field quantum electrodynamics.

</details>


### [127] [On the relationship between the gain of Stimulated Raman Backscatter of a short-pulse laser in a plasma and the Pierce parameter for XFELs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04517)
*Xinlu Xu,Warren Mori*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates an equivalence between the convective gain of Stimulated Raman Backscatter (SRBS) in the strongly coupled regime and the Pierce parameter used in high-gain X-ray Free Electron Lasers (XFELs), establishing a direct physical correspondence between the two phenomena.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a formal connection between two seemingly distinct physical systems—plasma-based SRBS and XFELs—by leveraging the Lorentz invariance of the gain parameter, thereby enabling cross-fertilization of theoretical insights between plasma physics and accelerator/FEL physics.

Method: The authors exploit the fact that the convective gain is a Lorentz invariant. They express the Pierce parameter in terms of FEL parameters evaluated in the electron beam's rest frame, and then compare it directly to the SRBS gain within a single laser wavelength in the strongly coupled regime.

Result: The convective gain of SRBS in the strongly coupled regime is shown to be mathematically equivalent to the Pierce parameter of high-gain XFELs, demonstrating a direct correspondence between the two physical systems.

Conclusion: There is a fundamental physical equivalence between SRBS in the strongly coupled regime and high-gain XFELs in the Compton regime, as evidenced by the Lorentz-invariant gain comparison.

Abstract: It is shown that the convective gain of Stimulated Raman Backscatter (SRBS) within a single laser wavelength in the strongly coupled regime is equivalent to the Pierce parameter commonly used to describe high gain x-ray Free Electron Lasers (XFELs). The comparison utilizes the fact that the gain is a Lorentz invariant so the Pierce parameter is written in terms of the FEL parameters in the electron beam's rest frame. It is thus inferred that there is a direct correspondence of the physics between SRBS in the strong-coupled regime and high gain XFELs in the Compton regime.

</details>


### [128] [A plasma photocathode for spin-polarized electron beams using excited hydrogen halides](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04932)
*Lars Reichwein,Thomas C. Wilson,Dimitris Sofikitis,Chinmaya Singh,T. Peter Rakitzis,Bernhard Hidding,Alexander Pukhov,Liangliang Ji,Markus Büscher*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new photocathode-like scheme for producing spin-polarized electron beams in plasma-based accelerators using pre-polarized hydrogen halides, achieving ~10 pC witness beams with up to 97% polarization, rivaling conventional sources.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Spin-polarized electron beams are critical for fundamental physics and searches beyond the Standard Model. While plasma-based accelerators offer a path to higher energies, existing proposals for polarized sources are difficult to realize and achieve polarizations far below conventional sources.

Method: The scheme uses a gas of pre-polarized hydrogen halides. A VUV laser pulse and a visible laser pulse excite the halide atoms, creating a two-component ionization medium: low-threshold excited halide and high-threshold polarized hydrogen. Particle-in-cell simulations are then used to evaluate the resulting beam properties.

Result: Particle-in-cell simulations show witness beams of approximately 10 pC retaining 97% of initial polarization P0. The final polarization reaches P ≈ 68% for unaligned bonds (P0 = 70%) and P = 97% for aligned bonds (P0 = 100%), rivaling state-of-the-art conventional sources.

Conclusion: The proposed photocathode-like scheme demonstrates that plasma-based accelerators can produce highly polarized electron beams competitive with conventional sources, overcoming a key limitation of previous plasma-based approaches.

Abstract: Spin-polarized electron beams are essential tools for probing fundamental symmetries and for the search beyond the Standard Model. While plasma-based accelerators are a promising pathway towards higher-energy frontiers, they have so far failed to deliver a competitive polarized source: existing proposals are challenging to realize and achievable polarizations remain far below conventional sources. Here, we introduce a photocathode-like scheme, applied to a gas of pre-polarized hydrogen halides. A VUV and a visible laser pulse excite the halide atoms to create a two-component ionization medium, consisting of low-threshold excited halide and high-threshold polarized hydrogen. Particle-in-cell simulations show witness beams of approx. 10 pC retaining $97\%$ of the initial polarization $P_0$, reaching $P \approx 68\%$ for unaligned bonds ($P_0 = 70\%$) and $P=97\%$ for aligned bonds ($P_0 = 100\%$), rivaling state-of-the-art conventional sources.

</details>


### [129] [Computational Methods and GPU Acceleration in Plasma Physics: A Empirical Analysis of arXiv Publications and Research Trends](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04171)
*Jeremy J. Williams,Anders Brostrom,Stefano Markidis*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes 5,522 computational plasma physics papers from arXiv (2010-2025) using text mining and statistical regression models (OLS, tobit, logistic) to examine how abstract length and GPU mentions vary with computational method, team size, and publication year, finding that MHD papers have longer abstracts while PIC and gyrokinetic methods have grown in prevalence and are strongly linked to GPU adoption.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Despite the growing reliance of computational plasma physics on HPC methods (PIC, gyrokinetic, MHD), there is limited field-wide evidence on how method choice, team size, and GPU adoption shape research outputs. This motivates a large-scale bibliometric analysis to understand these relationships.

Method: The authors analyzed 5,522 arXiv papers (2010-2025) using large-scale text mining, using abstract length as a proxy for methodological complexity. They employed OLS, tobit, and logistic regression models to examine relationships between abstract length, GPU mentions, computational method, number of authors, and publication year, while controlling for collaboration size and temporal trends.

Result: MHD studies have longer abstracts than PIC and gyrokinetic papers. Abstract length increases modestly with team size and changes gradually over time. PIC and gyrokinetic methods have grown substantially in prevalence over the past decade and are strongly linked to GPU adoption.

Conclusion: The findings reveal a decoupling between methodological verbosity and method prevalence, offering a new bibliometric perspective on the evolution of HPC-driven plasma physics research.

Abstract: Computational plasma physics increasingly relies on high-performance computing (HPC) methods, including particle-in-cell (PIC), gyrokinetic, and magnetohydrodynamic (MHD) simulations, yet field-wide evidence on how method choice, team size, and GPU adoption shape research outputs remains limited. We analyze 5,522 computational plasma physics papers published on arXiv between 2010 and 2025 using large-scale text mining, employing abstract length as a proxy for methodological and algorithmic complexity. Using ordinary least squares (OLS), tobit, and logistic regression models, we examine how abstract length and GPU mentions vary with computational method, number of authors, and publication year. Controlling for collaboration size and temporal trends, results show that MHD studies have longer abstracts than PIC and gyrokinetic papers, indicating more extensive methodological and physical exposition. Abstract length increases modestly with team size, while temporal effects suggest gradual changes in abstract conciseness over time. At the same time, PIC and gyrokinetic methods have grown substantially in relative prevalence over the past decade and are strongly linked to GPU adoption, reflecting their higher computational intensity and suitability for accelerator-based architectures. Together, these findings highlight a decoupling between methodological verbosity and method prevalence, offering a new bibliometric perspective on the evolution of HPC-driven plasma physics research.

</details>


### [130] [Polarization-resolved attosecond gamma-ray emission from few-cycle laser interactions with cone targets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04363)
*De-Sheng Zhang,Cui-Wen Zhang,Xue-Ren Hong,Feng Wan,Jian-Xing Li,Bai-Song Xie*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: The paper demonstrates generation of linearly polarized attosecond γ-ray pulses in the MeV range using a cone target irradiated by a single few-cycle laser pulse. QED-PIC simulations resolve spin and polarization, showing 300 as pulses with up to 0.88 linear polarization degree and photon energies extending to 6 MeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generate highly polarized attosecond γ-ray pulses in the MeV range, which are needed for studying ultrafast nuclear dynamics and polarization-dependent processes in strong-field QED.

Method: A cone target is irradiated by a single few-cycle laser pulse, extracting electron layers from the cone walls that are accelerated and interact with a counter-propagating reflected attosecond field via nonlinear Compton scattering. Two-dimensional QED-PIC simulations resolve electron spin and photon polarization during emission.

Result: Shortest equivalent pulse duration of 300 as with 0.78 linear polarization degree; photon spectrum extends to 6 MeV with linear polarization up to 0.88 in the high-energy range. Tradeoffs among photon number, mean energy, and polarization are found when scanning cone opening angle and laser-plasma parameters.

Conclusion: Highly polarized attosecond γ-ray pulses in the MeV range can be produced from a cone target with a single few-cycle laser, useful for investigating ultrafast nuclear dynamics and polarization-dependent strong-field QED processes.

Abstract: Linearly polarized attosecond $γ$-ray pulses in the MeV range are generated from a cone target irradiated by a single few-cycle laser pulse. Electron layers are periodically extracted from the cone walls and subsequently accelerated. Their interaction with the counter-propagating reflected attosecond field produces high-energy photons through nonlinear Compton scattering (NCS), forming attosecond $γ$-ray pulses. We model this interaction using two-dimensional quantum electrodynamics particle-in-cell (QED-PIC) simulations that resolve electron spin and photon polarization during emission. The results show a shortest equivalent duration of $300\,\mathrm{as}$, with a corresponding linear polarization degree of 0.78. The photon spectrum extends to $6\,\mathrm{MeV}$, and the linear polarization degree in the high-energy range reaches 0.88. The linear polarization degree remains high when photons from both emission directions are collected over wide momentum-angle ranges. Scans over the cone opening angle and the coupled laser-plasma parameters reveal tradeoffs among photon number, mean photon energy, and polarization. Such highly polarized attosecond $γ$-ray pulses could be used to investigate ultrafast nuclear dynamics and polarization-dependent processes in strong-field quantum electrodynamics.

</details>


### [131] [On the relationship between the gain of Stimulated Raman Backscatter of a short-pulse laser in a plasma and the Pierce parameter for XFELs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04517)
*Xinlu Xu,Warren Mori*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that the convective gain of Stimulated Raman Backscatter (SRBS) in the strongly coupled regime is mathematically equivalent to the Pierce parameter used in high-gain X-ray Free Electron Lasers (XFELs), establishing a direct physical correspondence between these two phenomena.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors seek to find a unifying theoretical framework connecting two important high-energy physics phenomena—SRBS in laser-plasma interactions and XFELs—by exploiting the Lorentz invariance of gain to reveal their underlying equivalence.

Method: The authors show that the convective gain of SRBS within a single laser wavelength in the strongly coupled regime equals the Pierce parameter of XFELs. They use the fact that gain is a Lorentz invariant to express the Pierce parameter in terms of FEL parameters in the electron beam's rest frame, enabling a direct comparison between the two systems.

Result: A direct correspondence is established between the physics of strongly-coupled SRBS and high-gain XFELs in the Compton regime, demonstrating that these two distinct physical systems share equivalent gain physics.

Conclusion: The equivalence found suggests that insights and analytical tools from one field (XFELs or SRBS) can be cross-applied to the other, potentially benefiting both research communities.

Abstract: It is shown that the convective gain of Stimulated Raman Backscatter (SRBS) within a single laser wavelength in the strongly coupled regime is equivalent to the Pierce parameter commonly used to describe high gain x-ray Free Electron Lasers (XFELs). The comparison utilizes the fact that the gain is a Lorentz invariant so the Pierce parameter is written in terms of the FEL parameters in the electron beam's rest frame. It is thus inferred that there is a direct correspondence of the physics between SRBS in the strong-coupled regime and high gain XFELs in the Compton regime.

</details>


### [132] [A plasma photocathode for spin-polarized electron beams using excited hydrogen halides](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04932)
*Lars Reichwein,Thomas C. Wilson,Dimitris Sofikitis,Chinmaya Singh,T. Peter Rakitzis,Bernhard Hidding,Alexander Pukhov,Liangliang Ji,Markus Büscher*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: The paper proposes a novel photocathode-like scheme for producing spin-polarized electron beams in plasma-based accelerators. By using VUV and visible laser pulses to ionize pre-polarized hydrogen halides, the scheme creates a two-component ionization medium that enables selective ionization of low-threshold halide atoms while preserving high polarization in hydrogen. Simulations show witness beams of ~10 pC retaining 97% of initial polarization, reaching P≈97% for aligned bonds.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Spin-polarized electron beams are crucial for fundamental symmetry probing and beyond-Standard-Model searches. While plasma-based accelerators offer promise for higher energies, existing proposals for polarized sources are difficult to realize and achieve polarizations far below conventional sources.

Method: The scheme uses a photocathode-like approach applied to pre-polarized hydrogen halides gas. A VUV laser pulse and a visible laser pulse excite the halide atoms, creating a two-component ionization medium with low-threshold excited halide atoms and high-threshold polarized hydrogen. Particle-in-cell simulations are used to validate the approach.

Result: Simulations show witness beams of approximately 10 pC that retain 97% of initial polarization P0. The scheme achieves P≈68% for unaligned bonds (P0=70%) and P=97% for aligned bonds (P0=100%), rivaling state-of-the-art conventional sources.

Conclusion: The proposed scheme provides a competitive spin-polarized electron source for plasma-based accelerators, overcoming previous limitations and achieving polarizations comparable to conventional sources.

Abstract: Spin-polarized electron beams are essential tools for probing fundamental symmetries and for the search beyond the Standard Model. While plasma-based accelerators are a promising pathway towards higher-energy frontiers, they have so far failed to deliver a competitive polarized source: existing proposals are challenging to realize and achievable polarizations remain far below conventional sources. Here, we introduce a photocathode-like scheme, applied to a gas of pre-polarized hydrogen halides. A VUV and a visible laser pulse excite the halide atoms to create a two-component ionization medium, consisting of low-threshold excited halide and high-threshold polarized hydrogen. Particle-in-cell simulations show witness beams of approx. 10 pC retaining $97\%$ of the initial polarization $P_0$, reaching $P \approx 68\%$ for unaligned bonds ($P_0 = 70\%$) and $P=97\%$ for aligned bonds ($P_0 = 100\%$), rivaling state-of-the-art conventional sources.

</details>


### [133] [Millisecond-Scale Neural Operator Surrogates for Double-Null Free-Boundary Grad-Shafranov Equilibria](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05555)
*Plamen G. Krastev*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: The paper trains a geometrically conditioned Fourier Neural Operator (FNO) to predict poloidal-flux fields in tokamak plasmas, replacing slow nonlinear Picard-iteration-based Grad-Shafranov solvers. The FNO achieves 0.05% mean relative L2 error, recovers X-points within 0.2 cm, produces physics-consistent GS residuals matching the FreeGS baseline, and runs ~640x faster on GPU (2.77 ms) with near-deterministic latency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Free-boundary Grad-Shafranov solvers are computationally expensive and have sample-dependent latency due to nonlinear Picard iteration, making them prohibitive for optimization, modeling, and control-oriented loops that require many equilibrium evaluations.

Method: A geometrically conditioned Fourier Neural Operator (FNO) is trained to learn a constrained forward map from spatial coordinates and scalar operating parameters (P_axis, I_p, f_vac) plus prescribed X-point locations to the poloidal-flux field ψ(R,Z), using a controlled family of double-null free-boundary equilibria generated by FreeGS for a fixed machine geometry and topology.

Result: The best FNO achieves 0.05% mean relative L2 error with N^-0.68 scaling; recovers both X-points within 0.2 cm and O-point to 0.03 cm; matches the FreeGS baseline in external finite-difference GS residual (2.29 vs 2.29±0.06); evaluates in 2.77 ms on GPU and 25.6 ms on CPU (640x and 69x speedups) with p95/median latency ratio of 1.01.

Conclusion: Neural-operator surrogates can deliver accurate, geometrically precise, millisecond-scale equilibrium evaluations suitable for magnetic-confinement fusion workflows within a prescribed topology and machine geometry.

Abstract: The Grad-Shafranov (GS) equation governs ideal magnetohydrodynamic equilibrium in tokamak plasmas. Free-boundary GS solvers are central to diverted-equilibrium modeling, but nonlinear Picard iteration introduces computational cost and sample-dependent latency that can become prohibitive in optimization, modeling, and control-oriented loops. Here we train a geometrically conditioned Fourier Neural Operator (FNO) to learn a constrained forward map from spatial coordinates, scalar operating parameters $(P_{\mathrm{axis}}, I_p, f_{\mathrm{vac}})$, and prescribed X-point locations to the poloidal-flux field $ψ(R,Z)$. The model is trained on a controlled family of constrained double-null free-boundary equilibria generated with \textsc{FreeGS} for a single fixed machine geometry and prescribed topology. The best model achieves a mean relative $L^2$ error of $0.05\%$, with test error following an empirical $N^{-0.68}$ power law over $N_{\mathrm{train}}\in\{500,1000,2000,5000\}$. It recovers both X-points to within $0.2$ cm and localizes the O-point to $0.03$ cm. As a physics-consistency diagnostic, the predicted fields satisfy an external finite-difference GS residual evaluation at the same level as the ground-truth fields, with mean normalized residual $2.29$, indistinguishable from the $2.29\pm0.06$ \textsc{FreeGS} baseline using the same diagnostic. The trained FNO evaluates one equilibrium in $2.77$ ms on GPU and $25.6$ ms on CPU, corresponding to speedups of ${\sim}640\times$ and ${\sim}69\times$ relative to \textsc{FreeGS} as configured here, with near-deterministic latency (p95/median $=1.01$). These results show that neural-operator surrogates can provide accurate, geometrically precise, millisecond-scale equilibrium evaluations for magnetic-confinement fusion workflows within a prescribed topology and machine geometry.

</details>


### [134] [BORAY-3D: A ray tracing code for three-dimensional magnetized plasma configurations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05667)
*Yuxuan Wang,Huasheng Xie*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: BORAY-3D is a new ray tracing code that integrates broad radio-frequency coverage (from ion-cyclotron/helicon/lower-hybrid to electron-cyclotron waves and emission), unified treatment of arbitrary 2D/3D magnetic-plasma configurations including open and closed field lines, and fully relativistic Maxwellian EC absorption into a single version, benchmarked against several established codes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing ray tracing codes typically offer either broad radio-frequency coverage in axisymmetric equilibria or 3D capability specialized only for electron-cyclotron applications. No single code combines both broad frequency coverage and full 3D capability, motivating the development of an integrated tool.

Method: The code extends the axisymmetric BORAY formulation by solving ray equations in cylindrical coordinates (r,φ,z) with a variable toroidal mode number nφ. It handles magnetic-field, density, and temperature data directly in (r,φ,z) coordinates without flux-function restrictions, allowing numerical equilibria and analytic models to be treated uniformly. Non-relativistic hot-plasma model is used for lower-hybrid, ion-cyclotron, and helicon absorption, while the relativistic model is coupled to reciprocal radiative transfer for ECE.

Result: The code is benchmarked against GENRAY for tokamak toroidal-field ripple, Raytrax and TRAVIS for W7-X, and public HSX heating and W7-X ECE results. Practical applications cover 13.56 MHz helicon waves, 50 MHz fast waves, 3.7 GHz lower-hybrid waves, and 115-220 GHz EC emission.

Conclusion: BORAY-3D successfully integrates three desirable features - broad frequency coverage, unified 2D/3D configuration handling, and relativistic EC absorption - into a single comprehensive ray tracing tool validated across multiple plasma devices.

Abstract: Ray tracing codes are useful tools for studying electromagnetic wave propagation and absorption using the geometrical-optics approximation. Existing codes commonly provide either broad radio-frequency coverage in axisymmetric equilibria or three-dimensional capability specialized for electron-cyclotron (EC) applications. BORAY-3D integrates three desirable features in a single version. First, it has a broad frequency range of validity regime from ion-cyclotron, helicon and lower-hybrid waves to EC waves and emission. Second, it provides a unified treatment of arbitrary two- and three-dimensional magnetic-plasma configurations, including both closed and open field-line regions. Third, it incorporates fully relativistic Maxwellian EC absorption. The code extends the axisymmetric BORAY formulation by solving the ray equations in cylindrical coordinates $(r,φ,z)$ while allowing the toroidal mode number $n_φ$ to vary. Magnetic-field, density and temperature data are directly described in $(r,φ,z)$ coordinates without the restriction of flux functions, so that numerical equilibria and analytic field models can be handled in the same form. The non-relativistic hot-plasma model inherited from BORAY is used for lower-hybrid, ion-cyclotron and helicon absorption, whereas the relativistic model is coupled to reciprocal radiative transfer for electron cyclotron emission (ECE). Practical applications include 13.56 MHz helicon and 50 MHz fast waves, a 3.7 GHz lower-hybrid wave, and 115--220 GHz EC emission. BORAY-3D has been systematically benchmarked against GENRAY for tokamak toroidal-field ripple, Raytrax and TRAVIS for W7-X, as well as public HSX heating and W7-X ECE results.

</details>


### [135] [SafeDivertor: Faithful Divertor Heat Flux Reconstruction from Macroscopic Plasma State Signals via Time-Frequency Prior Exploitation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05669)
*Hao Si,Zehua Chen,Qingquan Yang,Xiao Wang,Dengdi Sun,Wanli Lyu,Gaoting Chen,Guosheng Xu,Hang Su,Jin Tang,Jun Zhu*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces a new online-oriented signal-based paradigm for reconstructing time-resolved radial divertor heat-flux profiles from multi-source macroscopic plasma-state signals during discharge, bypassing conventional IR-based inversion that requires post-discharge heat-conduction modeling. It constructs a dataset (DivMPS2HF) and proposes a framework (SafeDivertor) with physical prior-aware initialization, input perturbation, spectral-aware optimization, and progressive training, achieving SOTA performance across all five metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional infrared-based divertor heat-flux inversion is performed post-discharge and requires complex heat-conduction modeling with device-specific material properties, divertor geometry, and boundary conditions. There is a need for a real-time/online-oriented reconstruction paradigm that directly maps available plasma-state signals to heat-flux profiles during discharge, but this task lacks systematic data foundations and benchmarks.

Method: The paper (1) constructs DivMPS2HF, a multi-source discharge dataset for signal-based divertor heat-flux reconstruction; and (2) proposes SafeDivertor, a task-driven framework incorporating physical prior-aware initialization for radial-distribution guidance, input perturbation to mitigate over-reliance on heterogeneous signals, spectral-aware reconstruction optimization to exploit time-frequency priors and preserve transients, and progressive training to stabilize multi-objective optimization.

Result: Experiments on DivMPS2HF show SafeDivertor achieves the best overall performance among evaluated time-series baselines across all five metrics, establishing a new performance benchmark for signal-based divertor heat-flux reconstruction.

Conclusion: The proposed signal-based paradigm and SafeDivertor framework provide a viable online-oriented alternative to conventional IR-based inversion for divertor heat-flux reconstruction, with source code to be released publicly.

Abstract: Divertor heat-flux analysis is essential for understanding plasma-wall interactions and protecting plasma-facing components in magnetic-confinement fusion devices, while conventional infrared-based inversion is usually performed after discharge and requires heat-conduction modeling with device-specific material properties, divertor geometry, and boundary conditions. Rather than accelerating this conventional infrared-based inversion paradigm, we introduce a new online-oriented signal-based reconstruction paradigm that directly reconstructs time-resolved radial heat-flux profiles from multi-source macroscopic plasma-state signals available during discharge. To enable systematic study of this task, we construct \textbf{DivMPS2HF}, a multi-source discharge dataset that provides the data foundation and benchmark for signal-based divertor heat-flux reconstruction. We further propose \textbf{SafeDivertor}, a task-driven framework designed to address the key challenges of signal-based heat-flux reconstruction. It employs physical prior-aware initialization to provide radial-distribution guidance for target channels, input perturbation to reduce over-reliance on specific heterogeneous signals, spectral-aware reconstruction optimization to exploit time-frequency priors and preserve transient dynamics, and progressive training to stabilize the optimization of these complementary objectives. Experiments on DivMPS2HF demonstrate that SafeDivertor achieves the best overall performance among the evaluated time-series baselines across all five metrics, establishing a new performance benchmark for signal-based divertor heat-flux reconstruction. The source code will be released on https://github.com/Event-AHU/OpenFusion

</details>


### [136] [Polarization-Conditioned Fourier-enhanced DeepONet for Electric Field Reconstruction from EFISH Measurements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05937)
*Zhijian Yang,Edwin Setiadi Sugeng,Yaqi Zhang,Anbang Sun,Tat Loon Chng*

Main category: physics.plasm-ph

TL;DR: The paper introduces PC-FDON, a machine learning framework that reconstructs electric field profiles from EFISH measurements in plasmas. It uses a Fourier-enhanced branch for optical physics, polarization conditioning via FiLM, and a physics-informed loss. The model handles both polarizations in a unified manner, provides uncertainty estimates via Monte Carlo dropout for OOD detection, and is validated on realistic electrode configurations and experimental data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: EFISH is a standard laser diagnostic for measuring electric fields in plasmas, but its coherent, path-integrated signal makes accurate field reconstruction challenging. Existing polarization-specific models limit applicability, and there is a need for a unified framework that handles both polarizations, various optical parameters, and provides reliable uncertainty quantification.

Method: PC-FDON is a deep operator network with three key architectural innovations: (i) a Fourier-enhanced branch providing inductive bias for Gouy phase shift and wave-vector mismatch; (ii) a polarization-conditioning branch using FiLM and gated units to enable a single model to process both polarizations; and (iii) a physics-informed loss enforcing self-consistency with the EFISH governing equation. Uncertainty is estimated via Monte Carlo dropout, and a location-dependent exceedance fraction metric enables OOD detection.

Result: PC-FDON achieves promising field reconstruction under noise-free, incomplete, and noisy inputs, with generalizability comparable to prior polarization-specific models. The framework correctly flags OOD inputs in a simulated surface dielectric barrier discharge, and experimental validation shows good agreement with simulations.

Conclusion: The combination of spectral inductive bias, conditional modulation, and dataset-specific uncertainty quantification shows strong potential for broader applications beyond plasma diagnostics, demonstrating the value of unified operator-learning models for physics-based inverse problems.

Abstract: Electric-field-induced second-harmonic generation (EFISH) is an established laser diagnostic for quantifying electric fields in plasmas, yet ensuring field accuracy remains challenging given the coherent, path-integrated nature of the signal. We address this via machine learning, developing a Polarization-Conditioned Fourier-enhanced Deep Operator Network (PC-FDON) -- a unified operator-learning model that reconstructs field profiles from EFISH measurements across both polarizations and various optical parameters. Its architecture incorporates three advances: (i) a Fourier-enhanced branch providing inductive bias for the Gouy phase shift and wave-vector mismatch; (ii) a polarization-conditioning branch encoding signal polarization via Feature-wise Linear Modulation (FiLM) and gated units, enabling one model to handle both polarizations; and (iii) a physics-informed loss enforcing self-consistency with the governing EFISH equation. Trained on data spanning multiple function families, polarization states, and phase-mismatch values, PC-FDON achieves promising reconstruction under noise-free, incomplete, and noisy inputs, with generalizability comparable to our previous polarization-specific model. Pointwise epistemic uncertainty estimates via Monte Carlo dropout reflect model confidence and enable out-of-distribution (OOD) detection through a location-dependent exceedance fraction metric. Validation is performed on realistic electrode configurations under both polarizations and varying Rayleigh ranges, including a simulated surface dielectric barrier discharge where the framework correctly flags OOD inputs, and experimental data showing good agreement with simulations. The architecture -- spectral inductive bias, conditional modulation, and dataset-specific uncertainty -- shows strong potential for broader application beyond plasma diagnostics.

</details>


<div id='nlin.CD'></div>

# nlin.CD [[Back]](#toc)

### [137] [A Symplectic Map Approach to Magnetic Field-Line Dynamics in Tokamaks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05414)
*Diego F. M. Oliveira,Edson D. Leonel*

Main category: nlin.CD

TL;DR: The paper analyzes magnetic field-line transport in tokamaks using the conservative Tokamap (an exact symplectic mapping), revealing universal dynamic scaling in ensemble-averaged transport and showing that decreasing magnetic-shear slows transport via Poincaré recurrence statistics, with characteristic transport time scaling as τc ∝ x_q^-0.213 due to stickiness from KAM islands, resonance chains, and cantori.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a unified framework connecting the geometrical organization of Hamiltonian phase space (mixed dynamics with integrable islands and chaotic regions) to macroscopic transport properties in tokamak magnetic field-line dynamics.

Method: Investigates the conservative Tokamap using a combined geometrical, dynamical, and statistical approach: phase-space portraits and Lyapunov exponents characterize the mixed Hamiltonian dynamics, ensemble-averaged transport is analyzed through homogeneous scaling theory, and Poincaré recurrence statistics quantify transport timescales.

Result: Transport exhibits universal dynamic scaling with growth, crossover, and saturation regimes connected by homogeneous scaling theory. Decreasing magnetic-shear systematically slows transport, with characteristic transport time following algebraic scaling τc ∝ x_q^-0.213, reflecting increasingly dominant stickiness from KAM islands, resonance chains, and cantori.

Conclusion: A direct connection is established between geometrical phase-space organization (partial transport barriers) and macroscopic transport properties, providing a comprehensive framework for understanding long-time magnetic field-line transport in tokamaks and other Hamiltonian systems with mixed phase space.

Abstract: Magnetic field-line transport in tokamaks is governed by the interplay between chaotic dynamics and invariant phase-space structures that act as partial transport barriers. We investigate the conservative Tokamap, an exact symplectic mapping for magnetic field-line dynamics, using a unified geometrical, dynamical, and statistical framework. Phase-space portraits and Lyapunov exponents characterize the mixed Hamiltonian dynamics, while ensemble-averaged transport exhibits universal dynamic scaling with growth, crossover, and saturation regimes connected through a homogeneous scaling theory. Poincaré recurrence statistics reveal that decreasing the magnetic-shear parameter systematically slows transport, with the characteristic transport time following the algebraic scaling $τ_c\propto x_q^{-0.213}$. This behavior reflects increasingly dominant stickiness associated with KAM islands, resonance chains, and cantori. Our results establish a direct connection between the geometrical organization of Hamiltonian phase space and macroscopic transport properties, providing a comprehensive framework for understanding long-time magnetic field-line transport in tokamaks and other Hamiltonian systems with mixed phase space.

</details>

### [138] [Moduli of the Sourceless Framed Beltrami-Vekua Normal Form](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05459)
*Daniel Alayón-Solarz*

Main category: math.CV

TL;DR: This paper studies the moduli space of sourceless framed Beltrami-Vekua equations under gauge transformations (recombinations, scaling, and orientation-preserving changes of variables). On bounded simply connected domains, equations reduce to $w_{\bar z}=B\bar w$ on the unit disk with residual symmetries given by zero-free holomorphic gauges and Möbius transformations. Zero-free equations are classified by two Möbius-invariant data: hyperbolic mass density $\vartheta$ and phase-curvature current $K$, with exact ranges determined (every positive Hölder density and every phase Laplacian of a Hölder phase). For fields with zeros, classification extends via the triple $(\vartheta,d\eta,d{\star}\eta)$, showing pseudo-analytic mass and charge are projections of a larger infinite-dimensional moduli space, with explicit inequivalent equal-mass/equal-charge examples given.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a complete classification (moduli space) of sourceless framed Beltrami-Vekua equations under symmetries, determining the exact invariants and their ranges, and to show that the moduli space is infinite-dimensional beyond the classical pseudo-analytic mass and charge.

Method: The paper reduces Beltrami-Vekua equations on bounded simply connected domains to the unit disk via orientation-preserving changes of variables. It identifies residual symmetries (zero-free holomorphic gauges and Möbius transformations) and classifies equations using invariants: for zero-free $B$, the hyperbolic mass density $\vartheta$ and phase-curvature current $K=\Delta \arg B$; for fields with zeros, the extended triple $(\vartheta,d\eta,d{\star}\eta)$ where $\eta=\operatorname{Im}(dB/B)$. Exact ranges of invariants are established via Hölder regularity analysis.

Result: The exact range of invariants is determined: every positive Hölder density occurs as $\vartheta$, and every phase current arising as the Laplacian of a Hölder phase occurs as $K$. For fields with zeros in the phase-integrable sector, classification extends to the triple $(\vartheta,d\eta,d{\star}\eta)$, where $d\eta$ is the atomic charge measure and $d{\star}\eta$ captures remaining phase curvature. Explicit examples of equal-mass, equal-charge but inequivalent equations are exhibited, demonstrating the infinite-dimensionality of the moduli space.

Conclusion: The moduli space of sourceless framed Beltrami-Vekua equations is infinite-dimensional, with pseudo-analytic mass and charge being only numerical projections onto a coarser level. The complete classification requires the full invariant data $(\vartheta,K)$ for zero-free fields, or $(\vartheta,d\eta,d{\star}\eta)$ for the phase-integrable sector with zeros.

Abstract: We study the moduli of sourceless framed Beltrami-Vekua equations under recombinations of the unknown, scalings, and orientation-preserving changes of variables. On every bounded simply connected domain, such an equation reduces to $w_{\bar z}=B\bar w$ on the unit disk, with residual symmetries given exactly by zero-free holomorphic gauges and Möbius transformations. When $B$ is zero-free, the equation is completely classified by two data modulo Möbius: the hyperbolic mass density $\vartheta=\tfrac14(1-|z|^2)^2|B|^2$ and the phase-curvature current $K=Δ\arg B$, whose hyperbolic density at $C^2$ regularity is $κ=Δ_{\mathrm{hyp}}\arg B$. We determine the exact range of these invariants: every positive Hölder density and every phase current arising as the Laplacian of a Hölder phase occur. For fields with zeros, the classification extends to the phase-integrable sector through the triple $(\vartheta,dη,d{\star}η)$, where $η=\operatorname{Im}(dB/B)$. On the tame sector, $dη$ is the atomic charge measure, while $d{\star}η$ carries the remaining phase curvature. Thus the pseudo-analytic mass and charge are numerical projections of a larger infinite-dimensional moduli space. Explicit equal-mass, equal-charge, inequivalent equations are exhibited.

</details>


### [139] [An Orlicz variational formula for David-type Beltrami equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05618)
*Ryo Matsuda*

Main category: math.CV

TL;DR: This paper proves that the principal solution map for Beltrami equations with compactly supported David (exponential-Orlicz) coefficients is locally real C^{1,1} with values in W^{1,2}_{loc}, provided a sufficiently high finite exponential moment is available. The derivative solves a linearized Beltrami equation, and the key estimate is a pointwise cancellation bound that controls the linearization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The goal is to establish higher-order regularity (specifically C^{1,1}) of the principal solution map for Beltrami equations under David/Orlicz-type coefficient spaces, going beyond known existence and continuity results by analyzing the differentiability structure of the solution map.

Method: The proof employs a pullback by the base principal solution. The linearized equation is derived, and the key technical estimate |DF_ν|_{op}/(1-|F(ν)|^2) ≤ 1/2 converts the linearized equation into a ∂̄-equation controlled directly by the L^Φ-norm of the direction. This is combined with the principal degenerate L^2-resolvent and optimal Jacobian regularity for exponentially integrable distortion to obtain a uniform quadratic remainder estimate.

Result: The principal solution map is locally real C^{1,1} on the open subset of L^Φ_U(C) where a sufficiently high finite exponential moment exists, with values in W^{1,2}_{loc}(C). The derivative in direction η is the principally normalized solution of the linearized equation. At the origin, D Sol_0[η] = (1/2)Cη in W^{1,2}_{loc}.

Conclusion: The paper establishes optimal C^{1,1} regularity for the principal solution map in the David/Orlicz setting, with an explicit derivative formula and a clean identification at the origin via the Cauchy operator, enabled by the sharp cancellation estimate demonstrated.

Abstract: Let $\mathcal{U} \Subset \mathbb{C}$ be fixed, $Φ(s)=e^s-s-1$, $F(ν)=\fracν{2+|ν|}$, and let $f^{F(ν)}$ denote the principal solution of the corresponding Beltrami equation. The identity $K_{F(ν)}=1+|ν|$ identifies compactly supported David coefficients with exponential-Orlicz parameters. We prove that, on the open subset of $L^Φ_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{C})$ where a sufficiently high finite exponential moment is available, the principal solution map is locally real $C^{1,1}$ with values in $W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{C})$. The derivative in a direction $η\in L^Φ_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{C})$ is the principally normalized solution of $\bar{\partial} V - F(ν) \partial_z V = DF_ν(η) \partial_z f^{F(ν)}$. The proof uses a pullback by the base principal solution. The key estimate is the pointwise cancellation $\frac{\|DF_ν\|_{\mathrm{op}}}{1-|F(ν)|^2} \le \frac{1}{2}$, which converts the linearized equation into a $\bar{\partial}$-equation whose source is controlled directly by the $L^Φ$-norm of the direction. Combined with the principal degenerate $L^2$-resolvent and the optimal Jacobian regularity for exponentially integrable distortion, this yields a uniform quadratic remainder estimate. At the origin one obtains $D \mathrm{Sol}_0[η]=(1/2)\mathcal{C}η$ in $W^{1,2}_{\mathrm{loc}}$.

</details>

### [140] [Runge--Kutta Bias in Local Maximal-Canard Thresholds: A Chain-Tree Condition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04304)
*Haibo Lu*

Main category: math.DS

TL;DR: The paper analyzes how fixed-step Runge-Kutta discretizations can accurately approximate ordinary trajectories but displace the parameter at which attracting and repelling slow manifolds form a maximal canard. Using a minimal polynomial model, it proves class-wide leading-bias cancellation conditions (b^T A c = 1/6) and derives leading coefficient formulas for two- and three-stage Runge-Kutta families, with calibration via a van der Pol formula and validation through high-precision numerical experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Runge-Kutta discretizations can distort canard thresholds even when trajectories are accurately approximated, so understanding exactly how and when this displacement occurs is crucial for reliable numerical analysis of singularly perturbed systems with slow manifolds.

Method: The authors compare actual section-defined canard thresholds between the flow and its exact Runge-Kutta map on minimal polynomial models, using compact two-parameter domains, B-series defect analysis distinguishing bushy- and chain-tree coordinates, exponential selection shielding, exact common-target recurrence, and discrete Gaussian Melnikov quadrature.

Result: Leading-bias cancellation is equivalent to b^T A c = 1/6 regardless of the other classical order-three condition. Leading coefficients are (12ρ-1)/32 and (24ν-1)/128 for two- and three-stage families respectively, with displacement terms proportional to h²ε². Numerical validation at 192-bit precision confirms predicted sign reversals and cancellation.

Conclusion: The threshold functional detects only chain-tree defects in the B-series, meaning canard threshold accuracy requires a specific (weaker) subset of classical order conditions, and the derived leading coefficients provide a quantitative prediction for threshold displacement that is validated both theoretically and numerically.

Abstract: A fixed-step Runge--Kutta discretization may approximate ordinary trajectories accurately while displacing the parameter at which selected attracting and repelling slow manifolds form a maximal canard. For the minimal polynomial model $x'=x^2-y+xy$, $y'=ε(x-λ)$, we compare actual section-defined thresholds of the flow and its exact Runge--Kutta map, uniformly over all admissible choices of the attracting and repelling slow manifolds. We first expose the mechanism on a symmetric, A-stable two-stage slice and then prove it on a compact two-parameter domain of the complete self-adjoint two-stage family and on a separate self-adjoint three-stage family. The order-three B-series defect has independent bushy- and chain-tree coordinates, but the threshold functional detects only the chain-tree defect. Thus class-wide leading-bias cancellation is equivalent to $b^T A c=1/6$, while the other classical order-three condition may remain unsatisfied. On the sharp model, the leading coefficients are $(12ρ-1)/32$ and $(24ν-1)/128$ for the declared two- and three-stage families. Uniformly as $ε=r^2\to0$ in $r^2\le h\le r^{3/4}$, these coefficients multiply $h^2ε^2$ in the displacement between nearby actual map and flow roots. The proof transports a finite Runge--Kutta defect to an actual section-defined root through exponential selection shielding, an exact common-target recurrence, and discrete Gaussian Melnikov quadrature. A right-fold van der Pol formula provides a classical calibration. A rational Rosenzweig--MacArthur specialization gives an actual local selected-threshold law in the original source clock, and a joint-$(r,h)$ finite-boundary proxy computation, independently checked at 192-bit precision, illustrates the predicted sign reversal and leading-bias cancellation.

</details>


### [141] [Traveling fronts in a spatial epidemic model with slow loss of immunity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04594)
*Rossella Della Marca,Gabriele Grifò,Annalisa Iuorio,Mattia Sensi*

Main category: math.DS

TL;DR: This paper analyzes traveling front solutions in a spatial SIRS epidemic model with diffusion acting on infected populations, using Geometric Singular Perturbation Theory to exploit the slow-fast structure caused by a small loss-of-immunity parameter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the emergence and structure of traveling front solutions in a spatially extended SIRS epidemic model, particularly how the natural timescale separation from slow loss of immunity shapes the epidemic wave dynamics.

Method: The PDE system is reduced to a singularly perturbed system of ODEs via traveling wave reduction, then analyzed using Geometric Singular Perturbation Theory. The authors study the fast layer problem and slow dynamics near the critical manifold, identifying an entry-exit mechanism that characterizes transitions between slow and fast regimes with quantitative results.

Result: The analysis reveals that traveling front solutions consist of concatenated local, fast, and slow segments, and the entry-exit mechanism quantitatively captures the switching between regimes. Numerical simulations of the full system confirm the geometric picture.

Conclusion: The geometric singular perturbation framework successfully explains the structure of traveling epidemic fronts, and the entry-exit dynamics provide a quantitative understanding of the slow-fast transitions in the SIRS model.

Abstract: We investigate the emergence of traveling front solutions in a spatial SIRS epidemic model with diffusion acting on the infected population. The model exhibits a natural slow-fast structure due to the presence of a small parameter governing the loss of immunity, which induces a separation of scales in the dynamics. Using a traveling wave reduction, the PDE system is transformed into a singularly perturbed system of ODEs, which we analyze within the framework of Geometric Singular Perturbation Theory. In the singular limits, we study the fast excursions governed by the layer problem, and the slow evolution close to the critical manifold. In particular, we identify an entry-exit mechanism tracking the transitions between slow and fast regimes, and derive a quantitative characterization of the entry-exit dynamics. Numerical simulations of the full system confirm the validity of the proposed geometric picture. The traveling front is shown to consist of a concatenation of local, fast, and slow segments, in agreement with the theoretical analysis.

</details>


### [142] [Runge--Kutta Bias in Local Maximal-Canard Thresholds: A Chain-Tree Condition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04304)
*Haibo Lu*

Main category: math.DS

TL;DR: This paper analyzes how fixed-step Runge-Kutta discretizations can accurately approximate ordinary trajectories while perturbing the parameter threshold where attracting and repelling slow manifolds form a maximal canard. Using the minimal polynomial model x'=x^2-y+xy, y'=ε(x-λ), the authors compare exact section-defined thresholds of the flow and its Runge-Kutta map across self-adjoint two- and three-stage families, proving that leading-bias cancellation depends only on the chain-tree B-series defect (equivalently b^T A c=1/6), with leading coefficients (12ρ-1)/32 and (24ν-1)/128 multiplying h²ε² in the threshold displacement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand when Runge-Kutta discretizations faithfully reproduce canard phenomena—specifically, to characterize how numerical methods shift the parameter threshold at which attracting and repelling slow manifolds form a maximal canard, and to identify which classical order conditions actually matter for this dynamical structure rather than for standard trajectory approximation.

Method: The analysis works on a minimal polynomial model, first exposing the mechanism on a symmetric A-stable two-stage slice, then proving results on compact two-parameter domains of the complete self-adjoint two-stage family and a separate self-adjoint three-stage family. The proof transports a finite Runge-Kutta defect to an actual section-defined root via exponential selection shielding, an exact common-target recurrence, and discrete Gaussian Melnikov quadrature, with a right-fold van der Pol formula for calibration and a rational Rosenzweig-MacArthur specialization for validation.

Result: The order-three B-series defect has independent bushy- and chain-tree coordinates, but the threshold functional detects only the chain-tree defect, making leading-bias cancellation equivalent to b^T A c=1/6 while the other classical order-three condition may remain unsatisfied. The leading coefficients are (12ρ-1)/32 and (24ν-1)/128 for two- and three-stage families respectively, multiplying h²ε² in the displacement between actual map and flow roots, uniformly as ε=r²→0 in r²≤h≤r^(3/4).

Conclusion: Runge-Kutta methods can preserve canard thresholds under a less stringent condition than full classical order-three, revealing that the chain-tree defect alone governs leading-bias cancellation for maximal canards. The results are validated through a van der Pol calibration, a Rosenzweig-MacArthur specialization, and 192-bit precision proxy computations confirming predicted sign reversal and bias cancellation.

Abstract: A fixed-step Runge--Kutta discretization may approximate ordinary trajectories accurately while displacing the parameter at which selected attracting and repelling slow manifolds form a maximal canard. For the minimal polynomial model $x'=x^2-y+xy$, $y'=ε(x-λ)$, we compare actual section-defined thresholds of the flow and its exact Runge--Kutta map, uniformly over all admissible choices of the attracting and repelling slow manifolds. We first expose the mechanism on a symmetric, A-stable two-stage slice and then prove it on a compact two-parameter domain of the complete self-adjoint two-stage family and on a separate self-adjoint three-stage family. The order-three B-series defect has independent bushy- and chain-tree coordinates, but the threshold functional detects only the chain-tree defect. Thus class-wide leading-bias cancellation is equivalent to $b^T A c=1/6$, while the other classical order-three condition may remain unsatisfied. On the sharp model, the leading coefficients are $(12ρ-1)/32$ and $(24ν-1)/128$ for the declared two- and three-stage families. Uniformly as $ε=r^2\to0$ in $r^2\le h\le r^{3/4}$, these coefficients multiply $h^2ε^2$ in the displacement between nearby actual map and flow roots. The proof transports a finite Runge--Kutta defect to an actual section-defined root through exponential selection shielding, an exact common-target recurrence, and discrete Gaussian Melnikov quadrature. A right-fold van der Pol formula provides a classical calibration. A rational Rosenzweig--MacArthur specialization gives an actual local selected-threshold law in the original source clock, and a joint-$(r,h)$ finite-boundary proxy computation, independently checked at 192-bit precision, illustrates the predicted sign reversal and leading-bias cancellation.

</details>


### [143] [Traveling fronts in a spatial epidemic model with slow loss of immunity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04594)
*Rossella Della Marca,Gabriele Grifò,Annalisa Iuorio,Mattia Sensi*

Main category: math.DS

TL;DR: This paper analyzes traveling front solutions in a spatial SIRS epidemic model with diffusion on infected individuals, using Geometric Singular Perturbation Theory to understand the slow-fast structure and entry-exit dynamics of the traveling waves.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to understand the emergence and structure of traveling front solutions in an SIRS epidemic model where a small immunity-loss parameter creates a slow-fast timescale separation. The authors seek a rigorous geometric framework to characterize how these fronts form and propagate.

Method: The PDE system is reduced to a singularly perturbed system of ODEs via traveling wave reduction. The authors analyze this system using Geometric Singular Perturbation Theory, studying the layer problem (fast excursions) and slow dynamics near the critical manifold. They identify an entry-exit mechanism capturing transitions between slow and fast regimes and derive a quantitative characterization of this dynamics. Numerical simulations validate the geometric picture.

Result: The analysis reveals that traveling fronts consist of a concatenation of local, fast, and slow segments. An entry-exit mechanism tracks the transitions between slow and fast regimes, and numerical simulations of the full system confirm the theoretical predictions.

Conclusion: The geometric singular perturbation framework successfully captures the multi-scale structure of traveling fronts in the epidemic model, with numerical confirmation validating the proposed entry-exit mechanism and the concatenation structure of fronts.

Abstract: We investigate the emergence of traveling front solutions in a spatial SIRS epidemic model with diffusion acting on the infected population. The model exhibits a natural slow-fast structure due to the presence of a small parameter governing the loss of immunity, which induces a separation of scales in the dynamics. Using a traveling wave reduction, the PDE system is transformed into a singularly perturbed system of ODEs, which we analyze within the framework of Geometric Singular Perturbation Theory. In the singular limits, we study the fast excursions governed by the layer problem, and the slow evolution close to the critical manifold. In particular, we identify an entry-exit mechanism tracking the transitions between slow and fast regimes, and derive a quantitative characterization of the entry-exit dynamics. Numerical simulations of the full system confirm the validity of the proposed geometric picture. The traveling front is shown to consist of a concatenation of local, fast, and slow segments, in agreement with the theoretical analysis.

</details>


### [144] [Verifiable Regularity Criterion for Conditional Expectation Operators and Conditional Mean Embeddings with Applications to Nonparametric Regression, Bayesian Inverse Problems, and Koopman Operators](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06155)
*Maximiliano Hertel,Ilja Klebanov,Manuel Schaller,Karl Worthmann*

Main category: math.DS

TL;DR: This paper characterizes when conditional expectation operators (CEOs) map function spaces into reproducing kernel Hilbert spaces (RKHSs), showing that such mapping properties are governed by the regularity of the Radon-Nikodym density of the conditional law. The authors establish a verifiable sufficient condition for boundedness and Hilbert-Schmidt property, which reduces to Sobolev regularity for RKHSs norm-equivalent to Sobolev spaces. They validate the framework across nonparametric regression, Bayesian inverse problems, and Koopman operator theory.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conditional expectation operators and conditional mean embeddings are fundamental tools across applied mathematics and machine learning, yet a unified understanding of when a CEO maps one function space into another (particularly an RKHS) has been lacking. This mapping property is critical for validating CME representations and error bounds in practical estimators.

Method: The authors characterize CEO mapping properties in terms of Radon-Nikodym density regularity of the conditional law. They derive a simple sufficient condition ensuring the CEO is bounded and Hilbert-Schmidt. For RKHSs norm-equivalent to Sobolev spaces, this condition reduces to Sobolev regularity of the conditional density. They then verify the condition holds in three classical settings by leveraging existing regularity results.

Result: The paper demonstrates that classical regularity results from nonparametric regression, Bayesian inverse problems, and stochastic dynamical systems each imply the required mapping properties for the CEO. This validates CME representations and provides error bounds for Galerkin-type and CME-based estimators in these settings.

Conclusion: The framework provides a unified perspective connecting probability theory, operator theory, kernel methods, and stochastic dynamics through the lens of conditional expectation operators, offering practical criteria for validating CME-based approaches.

Abstract: Conditional expectation operators (CEOs) and their associated conditional mean embeddings (CMEs) play a central role across applied mathematics and machine learning, appearing in nonparametric regression, Bayesian inverse problems, and Koopman operator theory. A fundamental question is when a CEO maps a function space on $\mathcal{Y}$ into a prescribed function space on $\mathcal{X}$, particularly a reproducing kernel Hilbert space (RKHS). We show that such mapping properties are characterized by the regularity of the Radon--Nikodym density of the conditional law, and establish a simple, verifiable sufficient condition under which the CEO is bounded and Hilbert--Schmidt. For RKHSs norm-equivalent to Sobolev spaces, this condition reduces to Sobolev regularity of the conditional density. The result yields a direct route to validate CME representations and error bounds for Galerkin-type and CME-based estimators. We verify the regularity condition in three settings: nonparametric regression, Bayesian inverse problems, and Koopman operator theory for stochastic dynamical systems. We show in each case that classical regularity results on the underlying probabilistic model imply the required mapping properties. The resulting framework offers a unified perspective on conditional expectation operators across probability, operator theory, kernel methods, and stochastic dynamics.

</details>
