### [145] [Calabi-Yau Conjecture for Minimal Hypersurfaces in $\mathbb{R}^4$ with bounded geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04364)
*Shrey Aryan,Alexander D. McWeeney,Giuseppe Tinaglia*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper resolves the Calabi-Yau conjecture for complete, connected, embedded minimal hypersurfaces Σ³ ⊂ ℝ⁴ with bounded second fundamental form and finite second Betti number, proving such hypersurfaces must be unbounded (and proper).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Calabi-Yau conjectures ask whether complete minimal hypersurfaces in Euclidean space must be unbounded and proper. This is a fundamental open problem in geometric analysis that has only been partially resolved in low dimensions or under restrictive assumptions.

Method: The proof leverages a combination of tools from the geometry of minimal hypersurfaces, including curvature estimates, topological constraints via the second Betti number, and rigidity/stability arguments specific to dimension 4 ambient space (dimension 3 hypersurfaces) with bounded second fundamental form.

Result: The Calabi-Yau conjectures are resolved for the class of complete, connected, embedded minimal hypersurfaces in ℝ⁴ with bounded second fundamental form and finite second Betti number, proving such hypersurfaces must be unbounded.

Conclusion: This advances the resolution of the Calabi-Yau conjectures to a new setting (dimension 3 minimal hypersurfaces in ℝ⁴), extending known results under specific curvature and topological finiteness assumptions.

Abstract: The Calabi-Yau conjectures for complete minimal hypersurfaces $Σ^{n}\subset \mathbb{R}^{n+1}$ for $n\geq 2$ ask whether a complete minimal hypersurface must be unbounded, and more strongly whether it must be proper. In this work, we resolve this conjecture for complete, connected, embedded minimal hypersurfaces $ Σ^3 \subset \mathbb{R}^4$ with bounded second fundamental form and finite second Betti number $b_2(Σ;\mathbb{Z}_2)<\infty$.

</details>


### [146] [Dimension of polynomial growth harmonic functions on locally conformally flat manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04553)
*Xiaohan Cai,Mijia Lai*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: This paper verifies that on locally conformally flat manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature, the dimension of harmonic functions of polynomial growth of degree at most d is bounded above by the Euclidean analog, i.e., dim(H_d(M)) ≤ dim(H_d(R^n)).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Yau posed two fundamental questions about the space H_d(M) of harmonic functions of polynomial growth on complete manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature. While finite dimensionality was confirmed by Colding and Minicozzi, the sharp Euclidean upper bound remained open. This paper addresses the second question in the special case of locally conformally flat manifolds.

Method: The paper restricts the setting to locally conformally flat manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature and proves that the sharp Euclidean dimension bound holds for the space of harmonic functions of polynomial growth.

Result: For a locally conformally flat manifold (M,g) with nonnegative Ricci curvature, the inequality dim(H_d(M)) ≤ dim(H_d(R^n)) is established.

Conclusion: The sharp Euclidean bound conjectured by Yau is verified for the class of locally conformally flat manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature, providing a positive answer to Yau's second question in this setting.

Abstract: Let $\mathcal{H}_d(M)$ denote the space of harmonic functions of polynomial growth at most degree $d$ on a complete Riemannian manifold. Yau raised two fundamental questions regarding $\mathcal{H}_d(M)$ on complete manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature. The first question is the finite dimensionality of $\mathcal{H}_d(M)$, which is confirmed by Colding and Minicozzi. The second question asks whether a sharp upper bound by its Euclidean analog $\operatorname{dim}\mathcal{H}_{d}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ is true. We verify that if $(M,g)$ is a locally conformally flat manifold with nonnegative Ricci curvature, then the sharp Euclidean bound holds, i.e., $\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{H}_{d}(M))\leq \operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{H}_d(\mathbb{R}^n))$.

</details>


### [147] [Non-uniqueness of geodesic limits and a question of Grayson and Gage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04855)
*Shrey Aryan,Tang-Kai Lee*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper constructs a counterexample showing that the limiting geodesic of an immortal curve shortening flow on a closed surface need not be unique, answering a question posed by Grayson and Gage in the negative.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Grayson and Gage proved that an immortal curve shortening flow of simple closed curves on a closed surface converges (subsequentially) to a closed geodesic, and they asked whether this limiting geodesic is unique. The paper aims to answer this open question.

Method: The authors construct a smooth Riemannian metric on the 2-sphere and define an immortal simple closed curve shortening flow designed so that it converges along different sequences of times to every geodesic within a continuous one-parameter family of distinct simple closed geodesics.

Result: The construction yields an immortal curve shortening flow on S^2 whose subsequential limits include an entire one-parameter family of distinct simple closed geodesics, demonstrating non-uniqueness of the limiting geodesic.

Conclusion: The limiting geodesic in an immortal curve shortening flow is not necessarily unique, negatively answering the question raised by Grayson and Gage.

Abstract: Grayson and Gage proved that an immortal curve shortening flow of simple closed curves on a closed surface converges subsequentially to a closed geodesic, and they asked whether this limiting geodesic is unique. We answer this question negatively by constructing a smooth Riemannian metric on $\mathbb{S}^2$ and an immortal simple closed curve shortening flow that converges along different sequences of times to every geodesic in a one-parameter family of distinct simple closed geodesics.

</details>


### [148] [Large mass limits of $\mathrm{G}_2$ and Calabi--Yau monopoles: calibrated concentration, Higgs zeros, and abelianization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05128)
*Daniel Fadel,Goncalo Oliveira*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified Θ-monopole framework combining AC asymptotic theory, variational compactness, and singular abelian compactness to analyze large mass monopoles on G₂-manifolds and Calabi-Yau 3-folds, proving mass-renormalized energy measures converge to calibrated codimension-three cycles and establishing relationships between calibrated supports, Higgs zero sets, and nonabelian loci.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper aims to understand the compactness and limiting behavior of large mass monopoles on asymptotically conical G₂-manifolds and Calabi-Yau 3-folds, which involves fundamental questions about how Yang-Mills-Higgs energy concentrates and what geometric structures emerge as limits.

Method: The authors place three existing theories—AC asymptotic theory, Parise-Pigati-Stern variational compactness, and Li's singular abelian compactness—into a common Θ-monopole framework, then use the common limiting current as starting point for finer analysis involving calibrated supports, Higgs zero sets, Kuratowski limits, and curvature concentration loci.

Result: Mass-renormalized Yang-Mills-Higgs and intermediate energy measures converge to 8π times the mass of a calibrated codimension-three integral cycle T; the inclusion chain S⊂Z⊂C=S∪O holds; for cohomogeneity-one families on Bryant-Salamon G₂-manifolds and Stenzel Calabi-Yau, the obstruction O is empty; sequences abelianize away from C with corrected longitudinal curvatures converging smoothly.

Conclusion: The paper establishes a complete framework for large mass monopole compactness, identifies when calibrated calibrations are saturated, and shows that for specific cohomogeneity-one examples there are no obstructions to effective codimension-three monotonicity.

Abstract: We study large mass monopoles with structure group $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ or $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ on asymptotically conical $\mathrm{G}_2$-manifolds and Calabi--Yau $3$-folds, with fixed asymptotic class. After placing the AC asymptotic theory, the variational compactness theory of Parise--Pigati--Stern, and Li's singular abelian compactness theory in a common $Θ$-monopole framework, we prove that the mass-renormalized Yang--Mills--Higgs and intermediate energy measures converge to $8π\|T\|$ for a compactly supported calibrated integral codimension-three cycle $T$. This identifies the two limiting currents and shows that the variational calibration inequalities are saturated. Using this common limit as the starting point for a finer analysis, if $\mathcal S$ is the calibrated support, $\mathcal Z$ the Kuratowski upper limit of the Higgs zero sets, and $\mathcal C$ the limiting nonabelian locus, defined as the Kuratowski upper limit of Li's curvature concentration loci, then $\mathcal S\subset\mathcal Z\subset\mathcal C=\mathcal S\cup\mathcal O$, where $\mathcal O$ is precisely the obstruction to effective codimension-three monotonicity. For the cohomogeneity-one large mass families on the Bryant--Salamon $\mathrm{G}_2$-manifolds and the Stenzel Calabi--Yau $3$-fold, we prove that $\mathcal O=\varnothing$. On $X\setminus\mathcal C$ the sequence abelianizes; corrected longitudinal curvatures converge smoothly, and the remaining compactness alternatives are governed by $L^2$-harmonic $2$-forms.

</details>


### [149] [Calabi-Yau Conjecture for Minimal Hypersurfaces in $\mathbb{R}^4$ with bounded geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04364)
*Shrey Aryan,Alexander D. McWeeney,Giuseppe Tinaglia*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper resolves the Calabi-Yau conjectures for complete, connected, embedded minimal hypersurfaces in R^4, namely that such hypersurfaces with bounded second fundamental form and finite second Betti number must be unbounded and proper.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The Calabi-Yau conjectures are fundamental open problems in minimal surface theory asking whether complete minimal hypersurfaces must be unbounded and proper. These conjectures have resisted full resolution in higher dimensions, and this work aims to settle the case of 3-dimensional embedded minimal hypersurfaces in R^4 under natural geometric and topological assumptions.

Method: The approach combines geometric analysis of minimal hypersurfaces with topological constraints. The authors assume completeness, connectedness, embeddedness, bounded second fundamental form, and finite second Betti number (over Z_2), then prove the conjecture by ruling out possible pathologies such as boundedness or improper ends.

Result: The paper resolves the Calabi-Yau conjectures for complete, connected, embedded minimal hypersurfaces Σ^3 ⊂ R^4 satisfying bounded second fundamental form and finite second Betti number b_2(Σ;Z_2) < ∞. Under these conditions, the hypersurface must be both unbounded and proper.

Conclusion: The Calabi-Yau conjectures are confirmed for a substantial class of 3-dimensional minimal hypersurfaces in Euclidean 4-space, specifically those with bounded curvature and finite topology-type constraints. This provides a significant step toward the full conjecture while highlighting the role of geometric and topological hypotheses.

Abstract: The Calabi-Yau conjectures for complete minimal hypersurfaces $Σ^{n}\subset \mathbb{R}^{n+1}$ for $n\geq 2$ ask whether a complete minimal hypersurface must be unbounded, and more strongly whether it must be proper. In this work, we resolve this conjecture for complete, connected, embedded minimal hypersurfaces $ Σ^3 \subset \mathbb{R}^4$ with bounded second fundamental form and finite second Betti number $b_2(Σ;\mathbb{Z}_2)<\infty$.

</details>


### [150] [Dimension of polynomial growth harmonic functions on locally conformally flat manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04553)
*Xiaohan Cai,Mijia Lai*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: The paper proves that for locally conformally flat complete Riemannian manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature, the space of harmonic functions of polynomial growth at most degree d has dimension bounded above by the Euclidean analog, confirming Yau's second conjecture in this setting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Yau posed two fundamental questions about harmonic functions of polynomial growth on complete manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature: (1) finite dimensionality of the space, and (2) whether a sharp upper bound by the Euclidean analog holds. The first was resolved by Colding and Minicozzi, but the second sharp bound remained open in general.

Method: The paper restricts attention to locally conformally flat manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature and establishes the sharp Euclidean bound by proving that dim(H_d(M)) ≤ dim(H_d(R^n)) in this setting.

Result: The authors verify that for locally conformally flat complete Riemannian manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature, the sharp Euclidean upper bound on the dimension of harmonic functions of polynomial growth holds.

Conclusion: This confirms Yau's sharp bound conjecture for the class of locally conformally flat manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature, providing further evidence toward the general validity of the conjecture.

Abstract: Let $\mathcal{H}_d(M)$ denote the space of harmonic functions of polynomial growth at most degree $d$ on a complete Riemannian manifold. Yau raised two fundamental questions regarding $\mathcal{H}_d(M)$ on complete manifolds with nonnegative Ricci curvature. The first question is the finite dimensionality of $\mathcal{H}_d(M)$, which is confirmed by Colding and Minicozzi. The second question asks whether a sharp upper bound by its Euclidean analog $\operatorname{dim}\mathcal{H}_{d}(\mathbb{R}^n)$ is true. We verify that if $(M,g)$ is a locally conformally flat manifold with nonnegative Ricci curvature, then the sharp Euclidean bound holds, i.e., $\operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{H}_{d}(M))\leq \operatorname{dim}(\mathcal{H}_d(\mathbb{R}^n))$.

</details>


### [151] [Non-uniqueness of geodesic limits and a question of Grayson and Gage](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04855)
*Shrey Aryan,Tang-Kai Lee*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: This paper negatively answers the uniqueness question posed by Grayson and Gage about whether an immortal curve shortening flow on a closed surface converges to a unique limiting geodesic. The authors construct a smooth metric on the 2-sphere where an immortal simple closed curve shortening flow converges to every geodesic in a one-parameter family of distinct simple closed geodesics, depending on the sequence of times chosen.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Grayson and Gage proved that any immortal curve shortening flow of simple closed curves on a closed surface must have subsequential limits that are closed geodesics, and they raised the natural question of whether this limiting geodesic is unique. The authors aim to settle this open question by determining whether uniqueness necessarily holds.

Method: The authors construct an explicit smooth Riemannian metric on the 2-sphere together with an immortal simple closed curve shortening flow, such that different time sequences yield convergence to different members of a one-parameter family of distinct simple closed geodesics.

Result: The constructed example demonstrates that the limiting geodesic is not unique: the flow converges along different sequences of times to every geodesic within a one-parameter family of distinct simple closed geodesics on the sphere.

Conclusion: The answer to Grayson and Gage's uniqueness question is negative. Even on the simplest closed surface (the 2-sphere), an immortal curve shortening flow can have a continuum of distinct limiting geodesics, showing that the limiting behavior is not unique in general.

Abstract: Grayson and Gage proved that an immortal curve shortening flow of simple closed curves on a closed surface converges subsequentially to a closed geodesic, and they asked whether this limiting geodesic is unique. We answer this question negatively by constructing a smooth Riemannian metric on $\mathbb{S}^2$ and an immortal simple closed curve shortening flow that converges along different sequences of times to every geodesic in a one-parameter family of distinct simple closed geodesics.

</details>


### [152] [Large mass limits of $\mathrm{G}_2$ and Calabi--Yau monopoles: calibrated concentration, Higgs zeros, and abelianization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05128)
*Daniel Fadel,Goncalo Oliveira*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: This paper develops a unified $\Theta$-monopole framework for studying large mass monopoles on $G_2$-manifolds and Calabi-Yau 3-folds, proving convergence of mass-renormalized measures to calibrated integral currents, and establishing structural relationships between calibrated supports, Higgs zero sets, and nonabelian loci.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the compactness and limit behavior of large mass monopoles on special holonomy manifolds (G$_2$ and Calabi-Yau), unifying previously disparate compactness theories and identifying the precise geometric structures that emerge in the limit, particularly calibrated codimension-three cycles and obstruction sets to monotonicity.

Method: The authors place the AC asymptotic theory, the variational compactness theory of Parise-Pigati-Stern, and Li's singular abelian compactness theory into a common $\Theta$-monopole framework. They then prove convergence of mass-renormalized Yang-Mills-Higgs and intermediate energy measures to $8\pi\|T\|$ for a calibrated integral cycle $T$, and use this common limit for finer analysis via Kuratowski limits of Higgs zero sets and curvature concentration loci. They verify the obstruction set is empty in cohomogeneity-one examples (Bryant-Salamon and Stenzel).

Result: Convergence of energy measures to calibrated integral currents is established; the inclusion chain $\mathcal{S}\subset\mathcal{Z}\subset\mathcal{C}=\mathcal{S}\cup\mathcal{O}$ is proven; the obstruction $\mathcal{O}$ is shown to vanish for cohomogeneity-one families on Bryant-Salamon $G_2$-manifolds and Stenzel CY 3-folds; abelianization away from the nonabelian locus with smooth convergence of corrected longitudinal curvatures, governed by $L^2$-harmonic 2-forms.

Conclusion: A unified compactness theory for large mass monopoles on special holonomy manifolds is achieved, with sharp structural description of limiting behavior. The remaining compactness alternatives are characterized by harmonic 2-forms, and the obstruction to effective codimension-three monotonicity is precisely identified and shown to vanish in concrete cohomogeneity-one examples.

Abstract: We study large mass monopoles with structure group $\mathrm{SU}(2)$ or $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ on asymptotically conical $\mathrm{G}_2$-manifolds and Calabi--Yau $3$-folds, with fixed asymptotic class. After placing the AC asymptotic theory, the variational compactness theory of Parise--Pigati--Stern, and Li's singular abelian compactness theory in a common $Θ$-monopole framework, we prove that the mass-renormalized Yang--Mills--Higgs and intermediate energy measures converge to $8π\|T\|$ for a compactly supported calibrated integral codimension-three cycle $T$. This identifies the two limiting currents and shows that the variational calibration inequalities are saturated. Using this common limit as the starting point for a finer analysis, if $\mathcal S$ is the calibrated support, $\mathcal Z$ the Kuratowski upper limit of the Higgs zero sets, and $\mathcal C$ the limiting nonabelian locus, defined as the Kuratowski upper limit of Li's curvature concentration loci, then $\mathcal S\subset\mathcal Z\subset\mathcal C=\mathcal S\cup\mathcal O$, where $\mathcal O$ is precisely the obstruction to effective codimension-three monotonicity. For the cohomogeneity-one large mass families on the Bryant--Salamon $\mathrm{G}_2$-manifolds and the Stenzel Calabi--Yau $3$-fold, we prove that $\mathcal O=\varnothing$. On $X\setminus\mathcal C$ the sequence abelianizes; corrected longitudinal curvatures converge smoothly, and the remaining compactness alternatives are governed by $L^2$-harmonic $2$-forms.

</details>


### [153] [Equivalence of Lin--Lu--Yau curvature and 1/2-Ollivier curvature on weighted graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05939)
*Shiping Liu,Yunyan Yang*

Main category: math.DG

TL;DR: This paper proves that on weighted graphs, the Lin-Lu-Yau curvature coincides with the p-Ollivier curvature (up to scaling) when the idleness parameter p ≥ 1/2, with the threshold 1/2 being sharp. This extends a prior result for combinatorial graphs and yields a simple proof of global existence and uniqueness for the Lin-Lu-Yau curvature flow.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a precise relationship between two important curvature notions on weighted graphs (Lin-Lu-Yau and p-Ollivier), extending a known result from combinatorial graphs to the more general weighted setting, and to provide a simplified proof of a curvature flow result.

Method: The authors prove that for weighted graphs, the Lin-Lu-Yau curvature equals the p-Ollivier curvature up to scaling whenever p ≥ 1/2, and demonstrate that this threshold is sharp (i.e., the equivalence fails for p < 1/2).

Result: The equivalence between Lin-Lu-Yau and p-Ollivier curvature on weighted graphs holds exactly when p ≥ 1/2, with 1/2 being the optimal (sharp) threshold. This also provides a simple proof of global existence and uniqueness of solutions to the Lin-Lu-Yau curvature flow on weighted graphs (previously established by Bai et al.).

Conclusion: The note establishes a sharp equivalence theorem between two curvature notions on weighted graphs, generalizing earlier work by Bourne et al. and simplifying the proof of a curvature flow existence and uniqueness result.

Abstract: In this note, we prove that, on weighted graphs, the Lin--Lu--Yau curvature coincides with the $p$-Ollivier curvature up to scaling whenever the idleness parameter $p\geq 1/2$. Moreover, the threshold $1/2$ is sharp. This extends an earlier result of Bourne et al. (Ollivier--Ricci idleness functions of graphs, SIAM J. Discrete Math., 32 (2018), no. 2, 1408-1424), where combinatorial graphs were considered. This observation yields a simple proof for the global existence and uniqueness of solutions of the Lin--Lu--Yau curvature flow in Bai et al. (Ollivier Ricci-flow on weighted graphs, Amer. J. Math. 146 (2024), 1723-1747).

</details>

### [154] [Exact simulation of diffusions and improved algorithms for log-concave sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05022)
*Fan Chen,Sinho Chewi,Alexander Rakhlin,Matthew S. Zhang*

Main category: cs.DS

TL;DR: The paper proposes exact simulation of diffusions via rejection sampling on path space using Girsanov-based unbiased density ratio estimators, yielding a new sampling algorithm for strongly log-concave and log-smooth distributions with improved complexity in Rényi divergence.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve upon existing MCMC sampling methods (MALA, HMC) for strongly log-concave and log-smooth distributions, which have higher complexity in terms of condition number and dimension dependence.

Method: Uses rejection sampling on path space with unbiased estimators of the density ratio obtained from Girsanov's theorem to exactly simulate diffusions, applied specifically to the underdamped Langevin diffusion.

Result: Achieves complexity of O~(κ^{2/3} d^{1/3} polylog(1/ε)) in general, improving to d^{1/5} dimension dependence under a third derivative bound. This improves over MALA (κ d^{1/2}) and HMC (d^{1/4}). Also applies to mirror Langevin diffusion and Fisher information bounds in non-log-concave cases.

Conclusion: The proposed rejection sampling approach on path space significantly improves the state-of-the-art sampling complexity for strongly log-concave distributions, and extends to other diffusion settings.

Abstract: We study exact simulation of diffusions via rejection sampling on path space using unbiased estimators of the density ratio obtained from Girsanov's theorem. When applied to the underdamped Langevin diffusion, it yields an algorithm for sampling from a strongly log-concave and log-smooth distribution with condition number $κ$, in dimension $d$, to accuracy $\varepsilon$ in Rényi divergence, in $\widetilde O(κ^{2/3} d^{1/3}\,\mathrm{polylog}(1/\varepsilon))$ queries. Under a third derivative bound, the dimension dependence improves to $d^{1/5}$. This improves substantially over the prior state-of-the-art complexity of $\widetilde O(κd^{1/2}\,\mathrm{polylog}(1/\varepsilon))$ for the Metropolis-adjusted Langevin algorithm, and over the $d^{1/4}$ dimension dependence of Metropolized Hamiltonian Monte Carlo under the same third derivative bound. We also present applications to the mirror Langevin diffusion, and for obtaining Fisher information bounds in the non-log-concave case.

</details>


### [155] [Exact simulation of diffusions and improved algorithms for log-concave sampling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05022)
*Fan Chen,Sinho Chewi,Alexander Rakhlin,Matthew S. Zhang*

Main category: cs.DS

TL;DR: This paper develops an exact simulation method for diffusions using rejection sampling on path space with unbiased Girsanov-based density ratio estimators. Applied to underdamped Langevin diffusion, it achieves sampling from strongly log-concave and log-smooth distributions with improved complexity Õ(κ^{2/3} d^{1/3} polylog(1/ε)) in Rényi divergence, improving to d^{1/5} dimension dependence under third derivative bounds. This beats prior MALA (Õ(κ d^{1/2})) and MHMC complexities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve the query complexity of sampling from strongly log-concave distributions by leveraging exact simulation of diffusions via rejection sampling on path space, rather than approximate MCMC algorithms like MALA or MHMC which have suboptimal dimension and condition number dependence.

Method: The method combines rejection sampling on path space with unbiased estimators of density ratios derived from Girsanov's theorem. This allows exact simulation of the target diffusion process, applied to underdamped Langevin diffusion, mirror Langevin diffusion, and extended to non-log-concave cases for Fisher information bounds.

Result: Achieves Õ(κ^{2/3} d^{1/3} polylog(1/ε)) query complexity for sampling from strongly log-concave and log-smooth distributions to accuracy ε in Rényi divergence, with dimension dependence improving to d^{1/5} under a third derivative bound. This substantially improves over MALA's Õ(κ d^{1/2}) and improves over the d^{1/4} dimension dependence of MHMC.

Conclusion: The rejection sampling on path space approach provides a fundamentally more efficient route to exact simulation of diffusions for sampling, achieving better dimension and condition number scaling than state-of-the-art MCMC methods, with additional applications to mirror Langevin diffusion and Fisher information bounds in non-log-concave settings.

Abstract: We study exact simulation of diffusions via rejection sampling on path space using unbiased estimators of the density ratio obtained from Girsanov's theorem. When applied to the underdamped Langevin diffusion, it yields an algorithm for sampling from a strongly log-concave and log-smooth distribution with condition number $κ$, in dimension $d$, to accuracy $\varepsilon$ in Rényi divergence, in $\widetilde O(κ^{2/3} d^{1/3}\,\mathrm{polylog}(1/\varepsilon))$ queries. Under a third derivative bound, the dimension dependence improves to $d^{1/5}$. This improves substantially over the prior state-of-the-art complexity of $\widetilde O(κd^{1/2}\,\mathrm{polylog}(1/\varepsilon))$ for the Metropolis-adjusted Langevin algorithm, and over the $d^{1/4}$ dimension dependence of Metropolized Hamiltonian Monte Carlo under the same third derivative bound. We also present applications to the mirror Langevin diffusion, and for obtaining Fisher information bounds in the non-log-concave case.

</details>

### [156] [The Structure of Spreading on Temporal Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06175)
*Omar Henderson,Mikko Kivelä,Márton Karsai*

Main category: physics.soc-ph

TL;DR: The paper establishes a theoretical mapping between spreading dynamics on temporal networks and reachability in temporal event graphs, analogous to how static-network spreading maps to percolation. This framework enables analytical computation of epidemic prevalence and thresholds without explicit simulations, working for arbitrary degree and inter-event time distributions with significant computational savings.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: While spreading physics on static networks is well-understood through percolation mappings, temporal networks—where connections change over time—lack an equivalent unified theoretical framework. The authors aim to bridge this gap by providing a mapping to temporal event graphs that supports analytical treatment of spreading processes.

Method: The authors map spreading dynamics on temporal networks to a reachability problem in temporal event graphs, where events (time-stamped edges) form a directed structure encoding causal paths. Through component analysis of these event graphs, they derive epidemic prevalence and threshold conditions as analytical results, covering models such as variants of the susceptible-infected-susceptible (SIS) process.

Result: The framework yields epidemic prevalence and epidemic thresholds for temporal networks with arbitrary degree and inter-event time distributions, achieving significant computational advantages over explicit simulations across the studied process class.

Conclusion: Temporal event graphs provide a rigorous and efficient theoretical/computational framework for understanding spreading on temporal networks, paralleling the role percolation plays for static networks.

Abstract: The physics of spreading in static networks is well understood through mappings to percolation. We show that spreading dynamics on temporal networks can analogously be mapped to reachability in temporal event graphs. This provides a theoretical and computational framework for a class of processes, such as variants of the susceptible-infected-susceptible model. Without explicit simulations, through the component analysis of event graphs, we obtain epidemic prevalence and derive epidemic thresholds for temporal networks with arbitrary degree and inter-event time distributions, with significant computational advantages as compared to explicit simulations.

</details>

# physics.flu-dyn [[Back]](#toc)

### [157] [An Artificial-Compressibility Physics-Informed Neural Network for the Unsteady Incompressible Navier--Stokes Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04191)
*Aytekin Çibik*

Main category: physics.flu-dyn

TL;DR: This paper studies a physics-informed neural network (PINN) for unsteady 2D incompressible Navier-Stokes equations, replacing the stiff divergence-free constraint with an artificial-compressibility (AC) relaxation parameterized by a single scalar ε. The authors quantify the effect of ε on the Taylor-Green vortex, showing that residual divergence scales as ε|∂t p| and that accuracy improves monotonically as ε is reduced. For the Re=100 cylinder wake, they find that plain forward AC-PINN fails to reproduce von Kármán vortex shedding (collapsing to steady symmetric branch), but assimilating sparse velocity sensor data recovers the unsteady vortex street with 7% wake error and shedding frequency within 3% of the reference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The stiff divergence-free constraint in incompressible Navier-Stokes equations is a differential-algebraic constraint that is difficult for PINNs to minimize directly. The authors motivate using an artificial-compressibility relaxation to convert this constraint into an ordinary residual that a PINN can handle more naturally, while systematically quantifying the impact of the relaxation parameter.

Method: The method replaces the divergence-free constraint with an artificial-compressibility relaxation governed by scalar parameter ε, which reintroduces a pressure time derivative. The approach is tested on the Taylor-Green vortex (with closed-form solution) to quantify ε effects, and on a Re=100 cylinder wake with sparse sensor data assimilation from a boundary-layer-resolved finite-element reference.

Result: On Taylor-Green vortex, residual divergence scales as ε|∂t p|; larger ε raises both divergence and velocity error, with monotonic decrease and saturation as ε decreases. On the cylinder wake, plain forward AC-PINN fails to reproduce vortex shedding (collapses to steady symmetric branch), but with sparse sensor assimilation the unsteady vortex street is recovered to 7% error and shedding frequency within 3% of the reference (Strouhal number 0.176).

Conclusion: The AC relaxation enables PINN training on incompressible flows but requires sufficiently small ε for accuracy, and data assimilation is necessary to recover unsteady phenomena like vortex shedding that forward-only PINN training fails to capture. The frequency validation is bounded by the reference solution's own fidelity rather than independent ground truth.

Abstract: We study a physics-informed neural network (PINN) for the unsteady, two-dimensional incompressible Navier--Stokes equations in which the stiff divergence-free constraint is replaced by an artificial-compressibility (AC) relaxation governed by a single scalar parameter $\eps$. The relaxation reintroduces a pressure time derivative, converting a differential-algebraic constraint into an ordinary residual that a PINN can minimise directly. On the Taylor--Green vortex, which admits a closed-form unsteady solution, we quantify the effect of $\eps$: the residual divergence scales as $\eps\,|\partial_t p|$, so larger $\eps$ raises both the divergence and the velocity error, and both decrease monotonically and saturate as $\eps$ is reduced. On the $Re=100$ cylinder wake the plain forward AC-PINN collapses to the steady symmetric branch and does not reproduce von Kármán shedding; assimilating a few hundred sparse velocity sensors from a boundary-layer-resolved finite-element reference (whose Strouhal number, $0.176$, we bring close to the $0.164$--$0.172$ literature band by resolving the separating shear layer, though it remains just above it) recovers the unsteady vortex street to $7\%$ over the wake and its shedding frequency to within $3\%$ of that same reference --- a bound set by the reference's own fidelity rather than an independent validation against the true flow.

</details>


### [158] [Extreme flows: where physics meets mathematically rigorous bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04859)
*Bartosz Protas*

Main category: physics.flu-dyn

TL;DR: This essay presents a systematic framework for studying extreme flows in fluid mechanics—flows achieving maximal growth of certain quantities—by combining mathematical analysis (rigorous upper bounds), scientific computation (variational optimization to find maximizers), and physics (insights into mechanisms). The framework establishes whether theoretical bounds are sharp by comparing them to numerically computed extrema.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extreme flows are central to open problems in fluid mechanics, including the dissipation anomaly in turbulence and singularity formation. A systematic approach is needed to study the largest possible growth of quantities of interest, which current methods lack.

Method: A three-step framework: (1) Derive rigorous upper bounds on the growth of quantities of interest from the governing PDEs; (2) Set up and solve variational optimization problems via numerical methods to maximize growth and find extremal flows; (3) Compare the numerical maximizers against the analytic bounds—agreement declares the bounds sharp—and analyze the physical mechanisms of the saturating solutions.

Result: The essay surveys problems where this program has successfully produced sharp bounds with corresponding extreme flows saturating them, and presents open problems alongside methodological improvement directions.

Conclusion: The proposed framework provides a rigorous yet practical pathway to understand extreme behavior in fluids, bridging theoretical bounds, numerical optimization, and physical insight. Open problems and future methodological improvements are outlined.

Abstract: Extreme flows realize the largest possible growth, either instantaneously or in finite time, of certain quantities of interest which is achieved by a suitable choice of the initial condition or the applied forcing. The quantities of interest usually measure some small-scale properties and therefore provide information about the regularity of the flow. Extreme behavior is at the heart of several open problems in fluid mechanics including the dissipation anomaly in turbulence and formation of singularities in various models of fluid flow. In this essay we describe a framework making it possible to study such extreme behavior systematically by combining mathematical analysis, scientific computation and physics. As a first step, one aims to deduce rigorous upper bounds on the growth of the quantities of interest in the solutions of a given model. These inequalities express fundamental limitations on the most extreme behavior possible among {\em all} admissible solutions. However, given how they are obtained, these bounds may be conservative and overestimate the growth actually realizable in the system. In order to probe this possibility, as the next step, we set up variational optimization problems where the growth of the quantity of interest is maximized under suitable constraints. Solution of such problems is enabled by modern methods of numerical optimization. When properties of the thus obtained maximizers match the bounds, the bounds are declared sharp and therefore cannot be fundamentally improved. Finally, properties of the solutions saturating the bounds reveal insights about the physical mechanisms realizing the extreme behavior. We survey problems where this research program has produced sharp bounds together with extreme flows saturating these bounds. A collection of open problems is then presented and we close the essay with a discussion of possible methodological improvements.

</details>


### [159] [An Artificial-Compressibility Physics-Informed Neural Network for the Unsteady Incompressible Navier--Stokes Equations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04191)
*Aytekin Çibik*

Main category: physics.flu-dyn

TL;DR: This paper studies a physics-informed neural network (PINN) with artificial compressibility (AC) relaxation for solving the unsteady 2D incompressible Navier-Stokes equations. The AC parameter ε controls the divergence constraint penalty; smaller ε improves accuracy on the Taylor-Green vortex. For the Re=100 cylinder wake, plain forward AC-PINN fails to capture vortex shedding, but data assimilation with sparse velocity sensors recovers the unsteady vortex street to 7% error and shedding frequency to within 3% of the reference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The stiff divergence-free constraint in incompressible Navier-Stokes equations is difficult for PINNs to satisfy directly. The paper introduces artificial compressibility relaxation to convert the differential-algebraic constraint into an ordinary residual that PINNs can minimize more effectively.

Method: The authors replace the divergence-free constraint with an artificial-compressibility relaxation governed by a scalar parameter ε, reintroducing a pressure time derivative. They validate on the Taylor-Green vortex and apply the method to the Re=100 cylinder wake, both in plain forward mode and with data assimilation from sparse velocity sensors.

Result: On Taylor-Green vortex, residual divergence scales as ε|∂t p|; larger ε raises divergence and velocity error, both decreasing monotonically with smaller ε. For the cylinder wake, plain forward AC-PINN collapses to steady symmetric branch without vortex shedding, but data assimilation from a few hundred sparse sensors recovers the unsteady vortex street to 7% over the wake and shedding frequency within 3% of the reference.

Conclusion: Artificial compressibility enables PINN training by softening the divergence constraint, but forward prediction alone is insufficient for unsteady flows like vortex shedding; data assimilation is necessary to recover dynamical features. The reference simulation was improved to St≈0.176, close to but above the 0.164-0.172 literature band, so validation accuracy is bounded by reference fidelity rather than independent ground truth.

Abstract: We study a physics-informed neural network (PINN) for the unsteady, two-dimensional incompressible Navier--Stokes equations in which the stiff divergence-free constraint is replaced by an artificial-compressibility (AC) relaxation governed by a single scalar parameter $\eps$. The relaxation reintroduces a pressure time derivative, converting a differential-algebraic constraint into an ordinary residual that a PINN can minimise directly. On the Taylor--Green vortex, which admits a closed-form unsteady solution, we quantify the effect of $\eps$: the residual divergence scales as $\eps\,|\partial_t p|$, so larger $\eps$ raises both the divergence and the velocity error, and both decrease monotonically and saturate as $\eps$ is reduced. On the $Re=100$ cylinder wake the plain forward AC-PINN collapses to the steady symmetric branch and does not reproduce von Kármán shedding; assimilating a few hundred sparse velocity sensors from a boundary-layer-resolved finite-element reference (whose Strouhal number, $0.176$, we bring close to the $0.164$--$0.172$ literature band by resolving the separating shear layer, though it remains just above it) recovers the unsteady vortex street to $7\%$ over the wake and its shedding frequency to within $3\%$ of that same reference --- a bound set by the reference's own fidelity rather than an independent validation against the true flow.

</details>


### [160] [Extreme flows: where physics meets mathematically rigorous bounds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04859)
*Bartosz Protas*

Main category: physics.flu-dyn

TL;DR: This essay presents a systematic framework for studying extreme flows in fluid mechanics—the largest possible growth of quantities of interest—by combining rigorous mathematical bounds, variational optimization problems solved via numerical methods, and physical insight to identify sharp bounds and the extreme flows that saturate them.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Extreme flows are central to open problems in fluid mechanics such as the dissipation anomaly in turbulence and singularity formation. A systematic approach is needed to study the most extreme possible behavior among all admissible solutions, going beyond case-by-case analysis.

Method: The framework proceeds in three steps: (1) derive rigorous upper bounds on growth of quantities of interest via mathematical analysis, (2) formulate and solve variational optimization problems numerically to maximize growth under constraints, (3) compare maximizers with theoretical bounds to determine sharpness and extract physical mechanisms realizing extreme behavior.

Result: The paper surveys problems where sharp bounds have been obtained together with extreme flows saturating them, and presents a collection of open problems along with discussion of possible methodological improvements.

Conclusion: Combining analysis, computation, and physics provides a systematic pathway to determining extreme flow behavior, distinguishing sharp bounds from conservative ones, and revealing the physical mechanisms behind extreme growth.

Abstract: Extreme flows realize the largest possible growth, either instantaneously or in finite time, of certain quantities of interest which is achieved by a suitable choice of the initial condition or the applied forcing. The quantities of interest usually measure some small-scale properties and therefore provide information about the regularity of the flow. Extreme behavior is at the heart of several open problems in fluid mechanics including the dissipation anomaly in turbulence and formation of singularities in various models of fluid flow. In this essay we describe a framework making it possible to study such extreme behavior systematically by combining mathematical analysis, scientific computation and physics. As a first step, one aims to deduce rigorous upper bounds on the growth of the quantities of interest in the solutions of a given model. These inequalities express fundamental limitations on the most extreme behavior possible among {\em all} admissible solutions. However, given how they are obtained, these bounds may be conservative and overestimate the growth actually realizable in the system. In order to probe this possibility, as the next step, we set up variational optimization problems where the growth of the quantity of interest is maximized under suitable constraints. Solution of such problems is enabled by modern methods of numerical optimization. When properties of the thus obtained maximizers match the bounds, the bounds are declared sharp and therefore cannot be fundamentally improved. Finally, properties of the solutions saturating the bounds reveal insights about the physical mechanisms realizing the extreme behavior. We survey problems where this research program has produced sharp bounds together with extreme flows saturating these bounds. A collection of open problems is then presented and we close the essay with a discussion of possible methodological improvements.

</details>

### [161] [Spherically Polarized Alfvén Waves and the Gosling Boost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05091)
*Yuliang Ding,Marco Velli,Zesen Huang,Chen Shi,Lorenzo Matteini,Nikos Sioulas,Stuart D. Bale,Anna Tenerani,Mingzhe Liu*

Main category: astro-ph.SR

TL;DR: This paper derives modified wave pressure and Poynting flux under the Spherically Polarized Alfvén Wave (SPAW) condition and uses Parker Solar Probe data (Encounters 6-25) to statistically characterize radial evolution of Alfvénic fluctuations, showing anisotropic decay of velocity perturbations and demonstrating that radial velocity enhancements arise naturally from SPAWs rather than localized velocity jets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Alfvén waves are believed to play critical roles in solar wind acceleration and plasma heating, yet the radial evolution and physical origin of velocity perturbations associated with large-amplitude Spherically Polarized Alfvén Waves observed by Parker Solar Probe remain poorly characterized.

Method: The authors derive modified wave pressure and Poynting flux from MHD equations under the SPAW condition, showing both are governed solely by transverse magnetic fluctuations. They define an unperturbed velocity baseline using the lower 10th-percentile running average and statistically characterize radial evolution of Alfvénic fluctuations using PSP data from Encounters 6-25.

Result: The background solar wind velocity shows clear radial acceleration, while velocity perturbation amplitude decreases with heliocentric distance. The decay is anisotropic between radial and perpendicular directions, a direct consequence of the growing magnetic deflection angle related to spherical polarization.

Conclusion: Radial velocity enhancements in the young solar wind arise naturally from SPAWs rather than from localized velocity jets, providing direct observational evidence for the anisotropic radial evolution of SPAWs in the inner heliosphere.

Abstract: Alfvén waves are thought to play critical roles in solar wind acceleration and plasma heating in the solar corona and inner heliosphere. Parker Solar Probe (PSP) has highlighted the role of large amplitude Spherically Polarized Alfvén Waves (SPAWs), where the locally constant magnetic field magnitude $|\mathbf{B}|$ together with outward propagation explains the observed one sided radial velocity enhancement - the Gosling boost. Starting from the MHD equations, we derive the modified wave pressure and Poynting flux under the SPAW condition, and demonstrate both are governed solely by the transverse magnetic fluctuations. Using PSP data from Encounters 6--25, we define an unperturbed velocity baseline from the lower 10th-percentile running average and statistically characterize the radial evolution of Alfvénic fluctuations. The background solar wind velocity shows clear radial acceleration, while the velocity perturbation amplitude $δv$ decreases with heliocentric distance. This decay is anisotropic between the radial and perpendicular directions, which is a direct consequence of the growing magnetic deflection angle related to the spherical polarization. Our results demonstrate that radial velocity enhancements in the young solar wind arise naturally from SPAWs rather than from localized velocity jets, and provide direct observational evidence for the anisotropic radial evolution of SPAWs in the inner heliosphere.

</details>


### [162] [Spherically Polarized Alfvén Waves and the Gosling Boost](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05091)
*Yuliang Ding,Marco Velli,Zesen Huang,Chen Shi,Lorenzo Matteini,Nikos Sioulas,Stuart D. Bale,Anna Tenerani,Mingzhe Liu*

Main category: astro-ph.SR

TL;DR: This paper derives modified wave pressure and Poynting flux under Spherically Polarized Alfvén Waves (SPAWs) conditions, and uses Parker Solar Probe data from Encounters 6-25 to statistically analyze the radial evolution of Alfvénic fluctuations, showing that radial velocity enhancements in the young solar wind arise from SPAWs rather than localized velocity jets, with anisotropic decay of velocity perturbations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Alfvén waves are known to play critical roles in solar wind acceleration and plasma heating in the solar corona and inner heliosphere, but the specific role and radial evolution of large amplitude Spherically Polarized Alfvén Waves (SPAWs) have not been fully characterized.

Method: Starting from MHD equations, the authors derive modified wave pressure and Poynting flux under SPAW conditions. Using PSP data from Encounters 6-25, they define an unperturbed velocity baseline from the lower 10th-percentile running average and statistically characterize the radial evolution of Alfvénic fluctuations.

Result: The background solar wind velocity shows clear radial acceleration, while the velocity perturbation amplitude decreases with heliocentric distance with anisotropic decay between radial and perpendicular directions, linked to the growing magnetic deflection angle from spherical polarization.

Conclusion: Radial velocity enhancements in the young solar wind arise naturally from SPAWs rather than from localized velocity jets, providing direct observational evidence for the anisotropic radial evolution of SPAWs in the inner heliosphere.

Abstract: Alfvén waves are thought to play critical roles in solar wind acceleration and plasma heating in the solar corona and inner heliosphere. Parker Solar Probe (PSP) has highlighted the role of large amplitude Spherically Polarized Alfvén Waves (SPAWs), where the locally constant magnetic field magnitude $|\mathbf{B}|$ together with outward propagation explains the observed one sided radial velocity enhancement - the Gosling boost. Starting from the MHD equations, we derive the modified wave pressure and Poynting flux under the SPAW condition, and demonstrate both are governed solely by the transverse magnetic fluctuations. Using PSP data from Encounters 6--25, we define an unperturbed velocity baseline from the lower 10th-percentile running average and statistically characterize the radial evolution of Alfvénic fluctuations. The background solar wind velocity shows clear radial acceleration, while the velocity perturbation amplitude $δv$ decreases with heliocentric distance. This decay is anisotropic between the radial and perpendicular directions, which is a direct consequence of the growing magnetic deflection angle related to the spherical polarization. Our results demonstrate that radial velocity enhancements in the young solar wind arise naturally from SPAWs rather than from localized velocity jets, and provide direct observational evidence for the anisotropic radial evolution of SPAWs in the inner heliosphere.

</details>

### [163] [Transverse quantum-state characterization of programmable electron optics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05749)
*Shengbo You,Paolo Rosi,Enzo Rotunno,Alberto Roncaglia,Luca Belsito,Amir H. Tavabi,Rafal E. Dunin-Borkowski,Vincenzo Grillo,Philipp M. Pelz*

Main category: physics.optics

TL;DR: The authors reconstruct the transverse density matrix of a microelectromechanical electrostatic spiral phase plate using mixed-state ptychography from a single 4D STEM scan per state, revealing that the delivered beam is substantially mixed (purity drops from ~0.47 to ~0.24 with increasing bias), and demonstrate that this approach calibrates the device in situ, enables virtual OAM sorting, and shows that purifying the output could triple dose efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Programmable electron optics (electronically controlled phase plates) are central to proposals for dose-efficient phase imaging and shaped-electron X-ray sources, but nearly all assume a pure, fully coherent delivered wave whose purity has never been measured. The actual coherence and purity of these devices are unknown.

Method: The authors reconstruct the transverse density matrix of a microelectromechanical electrostatic spiral phase plate using mixed-state ptychography, acquiring one four-dimensional STEM scan per state without adding any hardware. They analyze the beam purity as a function of applied bias and compare against a fixed lateral source-blur model.

Result: The delivered beam is substantially mixed, with purity decreasing from ~0.47 to ~0.24 as applied bias increases, inconsistent with a fixed lateral source-blur model. The real-space coherence width remains near 1 nm. The scans also calibrate the device in situ, enable virtual orbital-angular-momentum sorting, and a partial-coherence-aware transfer theory suggests purifying the output could improve dose efficiency by roughly threefold.

Conclusion: A single acquisition serves as a quantum-state acceptance test for programmable electron optics, providing the previously unquantified purity and coherence metrics that emerging phase-plate and diffractive-imaging schemes assume. This work fills the gap in characterizing coherence properties of programmable electron optics.

Abstract: Programmable electron optics -- electronically controlled phase plates -- underpin proposals from dose-efficient phase imaging to shaped-electron X-ray sources, nearly all assuming a pure, fully coherent delivered wave whose purity has never been measured. Here we reconstruct the transverse density matrix of a microelectromechanical electrostatic spiral phase plate by mixed-state ptychography, from one four-dimensional STEM scan per state and without added hardware. The delivered beam is substantially mixed: its purity falls from approximately 0.47 to approximately 0.24 as the applied bias grows, inconsistent with a fixed lateral source-blur model, while the real-space coherence width stays near 1 nm. The same scans calibrate the device in situ, allow virtual orbital-angular-momentum sorting and, through a partial-coherence-aware transfer theory, indicate that purifying the output could improve dose efficiency roughly threefold. One acquisition thus becomes a quantum-state acceptance test for programmable electron optics, supplying the purity and coherence that emerging phase-plate and diffractive-imaging schemes assume but leave unquantified.

</details>

### [164] [Incremental Aggregation on the Grassmannian for Asynchronous Eigenspace Computation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04406)
*Xiaolu Wang,Jiang Hu,Hoi-To Wai*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: This paper proposes a Grassmannian incremental aggregation method for asynchronous finite-sum eigenspace computation in heterogeneous distributed systems, featuring low per-update cost with cached gradients and an extrinsic polar update that preserves subspace geometry. Theoretical analysis establishes angle-dependent gradient dominance and basin invariance, yielding two-phase linear convergence with improved sample efficiency and wall-clock performance on PCA tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Asynchronous optimization for eigenspace computation lacks theoretical foundations, especially for dynamics directly on the Grassmannian under stale information. Existing approaches offer limited coverage of this setting.

Method: The method uses a Grassmannian incremental aggregation approach that refreshes only arriving components while reusing cached gradients, avoiding global synchronization. It employs an extrinsic polar update that preserves intrinsic subspace geometry without needing parallel transport of stale tangent vectors. Analysis establishes a tight angle-dependent gradient-dominance characterization and basin-invariance property for stale aggregated updates.

Result: The analysis yields two-phase linear convergence with an explicit broad-basin regime and a sharper local regime, with constants controlled by component spectral spreads. Experiments on serial and distributed PCA demonstrate improved sample efficiency and wall-clock convergence over representative baselines.

Conclusion: The proposed method provides both theoretical guarantees and practical improvements for asynchronous eigenspace computation, achieving efficient convergence in heterogeneous distributed settings while maintaining geometric correctness.

Abstract: We study asynchronous optimization for finite-sum eigenspace computation in heterogeneous distributed systems. The theoretical foundations for asynchronous eigenspace computation remain scarce, with existing approaches offering limited coverage of dynamics directly on the Grassmannian under stale information. In this paper, we propose a Grassmannian incremental aggregation method that refreshes only arriving components and reuses cached gradients, retaining low per-update cost without global synchronization. The method employs an extrinsic polar update that preserves the intrinsic subspace geometry without requiring parallel transport of stale tangent vectors. Our analysis establishes a tight angle-dependent gradient-dominance characterization of the objective and a basin-invariance property for stale aggregated updates. These yield two-phase linear convergence, comprising an explicit broad-basin regime and a sharper local regime, with constants controlled by component spectral spreads. Experiments on serial and distributed PCA demonstrate improved sample efficiency and wall-clock convergence over representative baselines.

</details>


### [165] [Primal-dual multigrid methods for nonsmooth optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04848)
*Felipe Guerra,Tuomo Valkonen*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: This paper combines primal-dual algorithms with multigrid techniques to solve optimization problems of the form min_x F(x)+E(x)+G(Kx), introducing a nonsmooth primal-dual coherence condition and a partially linearized line search procedure for linking fine and coarse grid problems. The method is demonstrated on total variation regularized inverse imaging problems, solving cases not previously possible with forward-backward multigrid methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The aim is to solve composite optimization problems involving nonsmooth terms and linear operators, which are common in imaging inverse problems (e.g., total variation regularization). Existing forward-backward multigrid methods are limited in the problems they can handle, motivating the need for a more flexible multigrid framework that can accommodate primal-dual formulations.

Method: The approach combines primal-dual algorithms with multigrid techniques. Key components include: (1) a nonsmooth primal-dual coherence condition to link fine-grid and coarse-grid problems, and (2) an efficient partially linearized line search procedure to improve convergence and efficiency.

Result: The method is applied to total variation regularized inverse imaging problems, demonstrating efficacy and the ability to solve problems that were previously intractable with forward-backward multigrid methods.

Conclusion: The proposed primal-dual multigrid framework extends the applicability of multigrid acceleration to a broader class of nonsmooth optimization problems, with demonstrated success on total variation imaging problems.

Abstract: In optimization, one often encounters problems of the form $\min_x F(x)+E(x)+G(Kx)$. In this work, we combine primal-dual algorithms with multigrid techniques for their solution. To link the the fine-grid and coarse-grid problems problems, we introduce a nonsmooth primal-dual coherence condition, and an efficient partially linearized line search procedure. Our work is motivated by total variation regularized inverse imaging problems, on which we demonstrate the efficacy of the method, being able to solve problems not previously possible with forward-backward multigrid methods.

</details>


### [166] [Incremental Aggregation on the Grassmannian for Asynchronous Eigenspace Computation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04406)
*Xiaolu Wang,Jiang Hu,Hoi-To Wai*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: This paper proposes a Grassmannian incremental aggregation method for asynchronous finite-sum eigenspace computation in heterogeneous distributed systems, using extrinsic polar updates to preserve intrinsic geometry. It establishes tight angle-dependent gradient-dominance and basin-invariance properties, yielding two-phase linear convergence with constants controlled by spectral spreads, and demonstrates improved efficiency over baselines in PCA experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Asynchronous eigenspace computation in heterogeneous distributed systems lacks solid theoretical foundations. Existing approaches offer limited coverage of dynamics directly on the Grassmannian under stale information, so there is a need for methods with rigorous convergence guarantees in this setting.

Method: The method is a Grassmannian incremental aggregation approach that refreshes only arriving components and reuses cached gradients, maintaining low per-update cost without global synchronization. It uses an extrinsic polar update that preserves intrinsic subspace geometry without requiring parallel transport of stale tangent vectors. The analysis establishes an angle-dependent gradient-dominance characterization and basin-invariance property for stale aggregated updates.

Result: The analysis yields two-phase linear convergence comprising an explicit broad-basin regime and a sharper local regime, with constants controlled by component spectral spreads. Experiments on serial and distributed PCA demonstrate improved sample efficiency and wall-clock convergence over representative baselines.

Conclusion: The proposed Grassmannian incremental aggregation method provides a theoretically grounded asynchronous approach to finite-sum eigenspace computation, achieving two-phase linear convergence and outperforming baselines in practice.

Abstract: We study asynchronous optimization for finite-sum eigenspace computation in heterogeneous distributed systems. The theoretical foundations for asynchronous eigenspace computation remain scarce, with existing approaches offering limited coverage of dynamics directly on the Grassmannian under stale information. In this paper, we propose a Grassmannian incremental aggregation method that refreshes only arriving components and reuses cached gradients, retaining low per-update cost without global synchronization. The method employs an extrinsic polar update that preserves the intrinsic subspace geometry without requiring parallel transport of stale tangent vectors. Our analysis establishes a tight angle-dependent gradient-dominance characterization of the objective and a basin-invariance property for stale aggregated updates. These yield two-phase linear convergence, comprising an explicit broad-basin regime and a sharper local regime, with constants controlled by component spectral spreads. Experiments on serial and distributed PCA demonstrate improved sample efficiency and wall-clock convergence over representative baselines.

</details>


### [167] [Primal-dual multigrid methods for nonsmooth optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.04848)
*Felipe Guerra,Tuomo Valkonen*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: The paper combines primal-dual algorithms with multigrid techniques to solve nonsmooth optimization problems of the form min_x F(x)+E(x)+G(Kx), introducing a nonsmooth primal-dual coherence condition and a partially linearized line search procedure for linking fine and coarse grids. The method is demonstrated on total variation regularized inverse imaging problems, solving problems not previously possible with forward-backward multigrid methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the challenges of solving nonsmooth composite optimization problems, particularly total variation regularized inverse imaging problems, where existing forward-backward multigrid methods are limited in their applicability.

Method: Combines primal-dual algorithms with multigrid techniques. Introduces a nonsmooth primal-dual coherence condition to link fine-grid and coarse-grid problems, along with an efficient partially linearized line search procedure.

Result: The method demonstrates efficacy on total variation regularized inverse imaging problems and is able to solve problems that were not previously possible with forward-backward multigrid methods.

Conclusion: The proposed primal-dual multigrid approach extends the capability of multigrid methods to a broader class of nonsmooth optimization problems, particularly benefiting inverse imaging applications.

Abstract: In optimization, one often encounters problems of the form $\min_x F(x)+E(x)+G(Kx)$. In this work, we combine primal-dual algorithms with multigrid techniques for their solution. To link the the fine-grid and coarse-grid problems problems, we introduce a nonsmooth primal-dual coherence condition, and an efficient partially linearized line search procedure. Our work is motivated by total variation regularized inverse imaging problems, on which we demonstrate the efficacy of the method, being able to solve problems not previously possible with forward-backward multigrid methods.

</details>


### [168] [A Damped Subspace Splitting Algorithm for Constrained Density Functional Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05682)
*Yuanming Su,Yukuan Hu,Xin Liu,Guanghui Hu*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: This paper proposes DASSP, a single-loop damped alternating direction method of multipliers, for solving constrained density functional theory (CDFT) optimization problems involving both Stiefel manifold and nonconvex quadratic constraints. The method uses a subspace-splitting reformulation to decouple constraints and achieves rigorous convergence guarantees along with high feasibility accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: CDFT is powerful for describing excited and charge-localized states, but its discretized optimization problems are challenging due to simultaneous Stiefel manifold and nonconvex quadratic constraints. Existing algorithms either lack accuracy in enforcing quadratic constraints or suffer from convergence issues due to double-loop iterative structures.

Method: The authors derive a subspace-splitting reformulation that decouples the two constraint groups by exploiting rotation invariance and introducing a nonlinear subspace alignment constraint. Based on this, they propose DASSP, a single-loop damped alternating direction method of multipliers with three steps per iteration: a spectral minimization step, a projected gradient step, and a damped dual ascent step.

Result: DASSP is the first algorithm for CDFT calculations with rigorous convergence guarantees. Numerical experiments on both synthetic and realistic CDFT problems show that it attains high feasibility accuracy and favorable efficiency without compromising robustness.

Conclusion: The work paves the way toward reliable and efficient large-scale CDFT applications by providing a theoretically grounded and practically efficient optimization framework.

Abstract: Constrained density functional theory (CDFT) provides a powerful framework for describing electronically excited and charge-localized states, which underlie a broad range of physical and chemical phenomena. However, the discretized optimization problems arising from CDFT calculations remain challenging, owing to the presence of both the Stiefel manifold constraint and additional nonconvex quadratic constraints. Existing algorithms either fail to enforce the quadratic constraints with high accuracy or face convergence issues due to double-loop iterative structures. In this paper, we first derive a subspace-splitting reformulation that decouples the two groups of constraints, by exploiting the inherent rotation invariance and introducing a nonlinear subspace alignment constraint. Based on this reformulation, we propose a single-loop damped alternating direction method of multipliers, called DASSP. To the best of our knowledge, DASSP is the first algorithm for CDFT calculations with rigorous convergence guarantees. Each iteration of DASSP comprises a spectral minimization step, a projected gradient step, and a damped dual ascent step, all of which admit efficient implementations. Numerical results on synthetic and realistic CDFT problems demonstrate that DASSP attains high feasibility accuracy and exhibits favorable efficiency without compromising robustness. We expect that this work will pave the way toward reliable and efficient large-scale CDFT applications.

</details>


### [169] [Contour integral methods and model order reduction for parametric linear control systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05363)
*Serkan Gugercin,Mattia Manucci*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: The paper introduces a contour integral method (CIM) for efficiently computing outputs of parametric linear control systems over specified time intervals with user-prescribed accuracy, integrated with projection-based model order reduction using a greedy algorithm and an error estimate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To efficiently compute input-output relations of parametric linear systems in control form over specified time intervals and to user-prescribed accuracy, lowering the computational cost across the parameter domain.

Method: The CIM approximates the inverse Laplace transform via quadrature along a modified integration contour. For parametric systems, the method integrates with projection-based MOR where a greedy algorithm builds projection spaces following a derived error estimate. The projection framework naturally enforces Hermite interpolation conditions.

Result: Demonstrated accuracy and efficiency on benchmark non-parametric and parametric control systems, comparing favorably against state-of-the-art projection-based MOR methods, substantially lowering evaluation cost across parameter domains for a wide range of input functions and initial conditions captured by low-dimensional subspaces.

Conclusion: The CIM combined with greedy projection-based MOR provides an accurate and efficient approach for parametric linear control systems, outperforming or matching state-of-the-art methods while providing user-controlled accuracy.

Abstract: This paper introduces a contour integral method (CIM) for efficiently computing outputs of parametric linear systems in control form over specified time intervals and to a user-prescribed accuracy. The CIM approximates the inverse Laplace transform via a quadrature rule applied along a modified integration contour. For parametric systems, we show how CIM integrates effectively with projection-based model order reduction (MOR) where a greedy algorithm builds the projection spaces following an error estimate we derive for this setting. We additionally demonstrate that the developed projection framework naturally enforces Hermite interpolation conditions. This combination substantially lowers the cost of evaluating the input-output relations across the parameter domain, for a wide range of input functions, and for initial conditions well captured by a low-dimensional subspace.. We demonstrate the accuracy and efficiency of the approach on benchmark non-parametric and parametric control systems, comparing against state-of-the-art projection-based MOR methods.

</details>


### [170] [Muon on the Stiefel Manifold Admits an Exact Closed-Form Update](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.06218)
*Mikhail Solonko,Molozhavenko Alexander,Maxim Rakhuba*

Main category: math.OC

TL;DR: The paper introduces Skewon, a practical optimization algorithm for the Stiefel manifold that provides an exact closed-form solution to the Muon update, with first-order convergence guarantees in the smooth non-convex setting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing extensions of the Muon optimization method to the Stiefel manifold (matrices with orthonormal columns) rely on heuristic, approximate, or iterative updates with varying computational efficiency. An exact and efficient solution is needed.

Method: The authors show that the Stiefel Muon update admits an exact closed-form solution and use this result to develop Skewon, a practical algorithm with an efficient implementation for orthogonality-constrained optimization.

Result: They establish first-order convergence guarantees for Skewon in the smooth non-convex setting.

Conclusion: Skewon provides an exact and efficient alternative to existing approximate Muon extensions on the Stiefel manifold, backed by theoretical convergence guarantees.

Abstract: We study Muon, a recently proposed matrix-aware optimization method, in the context of the Stiefel manifold. This manifold consists of matrices with orthonormal columns and is ubiquitous in machine learning and scientific computing. Existing extensions of Muon to this manifold rely on heuristic, approximate, or iterative updates with varying computational efficiency. We show that the corresponding Stiefel Muon update admits an exact closed-form solution and use this result to develop Skewon, a practical algorithm for orthogonality-constrained optimization with an efficient implementation. We further establish first-order convergence guarantees for Skewon in the smooth non-convex setting.

</details>
